# 4 Proof of Lower Bound (Theorem 1)

We now prove the main lower bound, Theorem 1. The proof follows the plan outlined in Section 2: We construct a packing of alternative instances, show that low regret on a given instance implies low estimation error, and then deduce from an information-theoretic argument that this implies high regret an alternative instance. All omitted proofs for intermediate lemmas are given in Appendix F. Recall throughout that we assume  $\sigma_w^2 = 1$ .

# 4.1 Alternative Instances and Packing Construction

We construct a packing of alternate instances  $(A_e, B_e)$  which take the form  $(A_\star + K_\star \Delta_e, B_\star + \Delta_e)$ , for appropriately chosen perturbations  $\Delta_e$  described shortly. As discussed in Section 2.1, this packing is chosen because the learner cannot distinguish between alternatives if she commits to playing the optimal policy  $\mathbf{u}_t = K_\star \mathbf{x}_t$ , and must therefore deviate from this policy in order to distinguish between alternatives. We further recall Lemma 2.1, which describes how the optimal controllers from these instances varying with the perturbation  $\Delta$ .

Lemma 2.1 (Derivative Computation (Abeille and Lazaric [2018], Proposition 2)). Let  $(A_{\star},B_{\star})$  be stabilizable. Then

$$
\frac {d}{d t} K _ {\infty} (A _ {\star} - t \Delta K _ {\star}, B _ {\star} + t \Delta) \big | _ {t = 0} = - (R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B _ {\star} ^ {\top} P _ {\star} B _ {\star}) ^ {- 1} \cdot \Delta^ {\top} P _ {\star} A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star},
$$

where we recall  $A_{\mathrm{cl},\star} \coloneqq A_{\star} + B_{\star}K_{\star}$ .

In particular, if  $A_{\mathrm{cl}}$  is non-degenerate, then to first order, the Frobenius distance between between the optimal controllers for  $A_{\star}, B_{\star}$  and the alternatives  $(A_e, B_e)$  is  $\Omega(\| \Delta \|_{\mathrm{F}})$ .

To obtain the correct dimension dependence, it is essential that the packing is sufficiently large; a single alternative instance will not suffice. Our goal is to make the packing as large as possible while ensuring that if one can recover the optimal controller for a given instance, they can also recover the perturbation  $\Delta$ .

Let  $n = d_{\mathbf{u}}$ , and let  $m \leq d_{\mathbf{x}}$  be the free parameter from the theorem statement. We construct a collection of instances indexed by sign vectors  $e \in \{-1, 1\}^{[n] \times [m]}$ . Let  $w_1, \ldots, w_n$  denote an eigenbasis basis of  $(R_{\mathbf{u}} + B_{\star}^{\top} P_{\star} B_{\star})^{-1}$ , and  $v_1, \ldots, v_m$  denote the first  $m$  right-singular vectors of  $A_{\mathrm{cl},\star} P_{\star}$ . Then for each  $e \in \{-1, 1\}^{[n] \times [m]}$ , the corresponding instances is

$$
\left(A _ {e}, B _ {e}\right) := \left(A _ {\star} - \Delta_ {e} K _ {\star}, B _ {\star} + \Delta_ {e}\right), \quad \text {w h e r e} \Delta_ {e} = \epsilon_ {\text {p a c k}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} e _ {i, j} w _ {i} v _ {j} ^ {\top}. \tag {4.1}
$$

It will be convenient to adopt the shorthand  $K_{e} \coloneqq K_{\infty}(A_{e}, B_{e})$ ,  $P_{e} = P_{\infty}(A_{e}, B_{e})$  and  $\mathcal{J}_{e} = \mathcal{J}_{A_{e}, B_{e}}^{\star}$ , and  $\Psi_{e} = \max \{1, \| A_{e}\|_{\mathrm{op}}, \| B_{e}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\}$ . The following lemma—proven in Appendix F.1—gathers a number of bounds on the error between  $(A_{e}, B_{e})$  and  $(A_{\star}, B_{\star})$  and their corresponding system parameters. Perhaps most importantly, the lemma shows that to first order,  $K_{e}$  can be approximated using the derivative expression in Lemma 2.1.

Lemma 4.1. There exist universal polynomial functions  $\mathfrak{p}_1, \mathfrak{p}_2$  such that, for any  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}} \in (0,1)$ , if  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}}^2 \leq \mathfrak{p}_1(\|P_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}})^{-1} / nm$ , the following bounds hold:

1. Parameter error:  $\max \{\| A_e - A_\star \|_{\mathrm{F}}, \| B_e - B_\star \|_{\mathrm{F}}\} \leq \sqrt{\|P_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}}} \sqrt{mn} \epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}}$ .  
2. Boundedness of value functions:  $\Psi_{e} \leq 2^{1/5} \Psi_{\star}$  and  $\|P_{e} - P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq 2^{1/5}\|P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ .  
3. Controller error:  $\| K_{e} - K_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq 2\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}mn\epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}}^{2}$  
4. First-order error:  $\left\| K_{\star} + \frac{d}{dt} K_{\infty}(A_{\star} - t\Delta K_{\star},B_{\star} + t\Delta_{e})\right\|_{t = 0} - K_{e}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq \mathfrak{p}_{2}(\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}})^{2}(mn)^{2}\epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}}^{4}$

Notably, item 4 ensures that the first order approximation in Lemma 2.1 is accurate for  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}}$  sufficiently small.

Going forward, we we choose the polynomials in the above lemma  $\mathfrak{p}_1,\mathfrak{p}_2$  to satisfy  $\mathfrak{p}_1(x),\mathfrak{p}_2(x)\geq x$  (without loss of generality). We use that  $\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq 1$  repeatedly throughout the proof.

Lemma 4.2 (Lower bound on  $\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ ). If  $R_{\mathbf{x}}\succeq I$ , then  $P_{\star}\succeq I$ , and in particular  $\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq 1$

Proof. This is Part 4 of a more general statement, Lemma B.5, given in Appendix B.

Henceforth, we take  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}}$  sufficiently small so as to satisfy the conditions of Lemma 4.1.

Assumption 2 (Small  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}}$ ).  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}}^2 \leq \frac{1}{mn} (\mathfrak{p}_1(\|P_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}})^{-1} \wedge \frac{1}{20}\mathfrak{p}_2(\|P_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}})^{-1})$ .

# 4.2 Low Regret Implies Estimation for Controller

We now show that if one can achieve low regret on every instance, then one can estimate the infinite-horizon optimal controller  $K_{e}$ . Suppressing dependence on  $T$ , we introduce the shorthand  $\mathbb{E}\mathrm{Regret}_e[\pi ]\coloneqq \mathbb{E}\mathrm{Regret}_{A_e,B_e,T}[\pi ]$ . Going forward, we restrict ourselves to algorithms whose regret is sufficiently small on every packing instance; the trivial case where this is not satisfied is handled at the end of the proof.

Assumption 3 (Uniform Correctness). For all instances  $(A_e, B_e)$ , the algorithm  $\pi$  ensures that  $\mathbb{E}\mathrm{Regret}_e[\pi] \leq \frac{T}{6d_{\star} \|P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}} \Psi_{\star}^2} - \gamma_{\mathrm{err}}$ , where  $\gamma_{\mathrm{err}} := 6 \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}^3 \Psi_{\star}^2$ .

We now define an intermediate term which captures which captures the extent to which the control inputs under instance  $e$  deviate from those prescribed by the optimal infinite horizon controller  $K_{e}$  on the first  $T / 2$  rounds:

$$
\mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e} [ \pi ] := \mathbb {E} _ {A _ {e}, B _ {e}, \pi} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \| \mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {e} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| ^ {2} \right].
$$

The following lemma, proven in Appendix F.2, shows that regret is lower bounded by  $\mathrm{K - Err}_e[\pi]$ , and hence any algorithm with low regret under this instance must play controls close to  $K_{e}\mathbf{x}_{t}$ .

Lemma 4.3. There is a universal constant  $c_{\mathrm{err}} > 0$  such that if Assumptions 2 and 3 hold and  $T \geq c_{\mathrm{err}} \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}^2 \Psi_{\star}^4$ , then

$$
\mathbb {E} \mathrm {R e g r e t} _ {e} [ \pi ] \geq \frac {1}{2} \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e} [ \pi ] - \gamma_ {\mathrm {e r r}}.
$$

In light of Lemma 4.3, the remainder of the proof will focus on lower bounding the deviation  $\mathrm{K - Err}_e$ . As a first step, the next lemma—proven in Appendix F.3—shows that the optimal controller can be estimated well through least squares whenever  $\mathrm{K - Err}_e$  is small. More concretely, we consider a least squares estimator which fits a controller using the first half of the algorithm's trajectory. The estimator returns

$$
\widehat {K} _ {\mathrm {L S}} := \underset {K} {\arg \min } \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \| \mathbf {u} _ {t} - K \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| ^ {2}, \tag {4.2}
$$

when  $\sum_{t=1}^{T/2} \mathbf{x}_t \mathbf{x}_t^\top \succeq c_{\min} T \cdot I$ , and returns  $\widehat{K}_{\mathrm{LS}} = 0$  otherwise.

Lemma 4.4. If  $T \geq c_0 d_{\mathbf{x}} \log(1 + d_{\mathbf{x}} \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}})$  and Assumptions 2 and 3 hold, and if  $c_{\min}$  is chosen to be an appropriate numerical constant, then the least squares estimator Equation (4.2) guarantees

$$
\mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e} [ \pi ] \geq c _ {\mathrm {L S}} T \cdot \mathbb {E} _ {A _ {e}, B _ {e}, \pi} \left[ \| \widehat {K} _ {\mathrm {L S}} - K _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \right] - 1,
$$

where  $c_{0}$  and  $c_{\mathrm{LS}}$  are universal constants.

Henceforth we take  $T$  large enough such that Lemma 4.3 and Lemma 4.4 apply.

Assumption 4. We have that  $T \geq c_0 d_{\mathbf{x}} \log(1 + d_{\mathbf{x}} \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}) \vee c_{\mathrm{err}} \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}^2 \Psi_{\star}^4$ .

# 4.3 Information-Theoretic Lower Bound for Estimation

We have established that low regret under the instance  $(A_e, B_e)$  requires a small deviation from  $K_e$  in the sense that  $\mathrm{K - Err}_e[\pi]$  is small, and have shown in turn that any algorithm with low regret yields an estimator for the optimal controller  $K_e$  (Lemma 4.4). We now provide necessary condition for estimating the optimal controller, which will lead to the final tradeoff between regret on the nominal instance and the alternative instance. This condition is stated in terms of a quantity related to  $\mathrm{K - Err}_e$ :

$$
\mathrm {K} _ {\star} \mathrm {- E r r} _ {e} [ \pi ] := \mathbb {E} _ {A _ {e}, B _ {e}, \pi} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \| \mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {\star} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| ^ {2} \right].
$$

Both  $\mathrm{K}_{\star}\text{-Err}_e[\pi]$  and  $\mathrm{K - Err}_e[\pi]$  concern the behavior of the algorithm under instance  $(A_{e},B_{e})$  but former measures deviation from  $K_{\star}$  ("exploration error") while the latter measures deviation from the optimal controller  $K_{e}$ . Our proof essentially argues the following. Let  $(e,e^{\prime})$  be a pair of random indices on the hypercube, where  $\pmb{e}$  is uniform on  $\{-1,1\}^{nm}$ , and  $\pmb{e^{\prime}}$  is obtained by flipping a single, uniformly selected entry of  $\pmb{e}$ . Moreover, let  $\mathbb{P}_e,\mathbb{P}_{e'}$  denote the respective laws for our algorithm under these two instances. We show that—because our instances take the form  $(A_{\star} - \Delta K_{\star},B + \Delta)$  — $\mathrm{K}_{\star}\text{-Err}_e[\pi]$  captures the KL divergence between these two instances:

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {e} \mathrm {K} _ {\star} - \mathrm {E r r} _ {e} [ \pi ] \approx \mathbb {E} _ {e, e ^ {\prime}} \mathrm {K L} (\mathbb {P} _ {e}, \mathbb {P} _ {e ^ {\prime}}),
$$

where the expectations are taken with respect to the distribution over  $(e, e')$ . In other words, the average error  $\mathbb{E}_e \mathrm{K}_{\star} - \mathrm{Err}_e[\pi]$  corresponds to the average one-flip KL-divergence between instances. This captures the fact that the instances can only be distinguished by playing controls which deviate from  $\mathbf{u}_t = K_{\star} \mathbf{x}_t$ .

As a consequence, using a technique based on Assouad's lemma [Assouad, 1983] due to Arias-Castro et al. [2012], we prove an information-theoretic lower bound that shows that any algorithm that can recover the index vector  $e$  in Hamming distance on every instance must have  $\mathrm{K}_{\star} - \mathrm{Err}_e[\pi]$  is large on some instances.

As described above, the following lemma concerns the case where the alternative instance index  $e$  is drawn uniformly from the hypercube. Let  $\mathbb{E}_e$  denote expectation  $e \stackrel{\mathrm{unif}}{\sim} \{-1, 1\}^{[n] \times [m]}$ , and let  $d_{\mathrm{ham}}(e, e')$  denote the Hamming distance.

Lemma 4.5. Let  $\widehat{e}$  be any estimator depending only on  $(\mathbf{x}_1,\ldots ,\mathbf{x}_{T / 2})$  and  $(\mathbf{u}_1,\dots ,\mathbf{u}_{T / 2})$ . Then

$$
{ e i t h e r } { \mathbb { E } _ { e } \mathrm { K } _ { \star } \text {- E r r } _ { e } [ \pi ] \geq \frac { n } { 4 \epsilon _ { \mathrm { p a c k } } ^ { 2 } } , } { o r } { \mathbb { E } _ { e } \mathbb { E } _ { A _ { e } , B _ { e } , \mathsf { A l g } } \left[ d _ { \mathrm { h a m } } ( e , \widehat { e } ) \right] \geq \frac { n m } { 4 } . }
$$

The above lemma is proven in Appendix F.4. To apply this result to the least squares estimator  $\widehat{K}_{\mathrm{LS}}$ , we prove the following lemma (Appendix F.5), which shows that any estimator  $\widehat{K}$  with low Frobenius error relative to  $K_{e}$  can be used to recover  $e$  in Hamming distance.

Lemma 4.6. Let  $\widehat{e}_{i,j}(\widehat{K})\coloneqq \mathrm{sign}\left(w_i^\top (\widehat{K} -K_\star)v_j\right)$ , and define  $\nu_{k}\coloneqq \| R_{\mathbf{u}} + B_{\star}^{\top}P_{\star}B_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}} / \sigma_{k}(A_{\mathrm{cl},\star})$ . Then under Assumption 2,

$$
d _ {\mathrm {h a m}} (\widehat {e} _ {i, j} (\widehat {K}), e _ {i, j}) \leq \frac {2 \| \widehat {K} - K _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {F}}}{\nu_ {m} ^ {2} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {p a c k}} ^ {2}} + \frac {1}{2 0} n m.
$$

Combining Lemmas 4.4, 4.5, and 4.6, we arrive at a dichotomy: either the average exploration error  $\mathrm{K}_{\star} - \mathrm{Err}_e[\pi]$  is large, or the regret proxy  $\mathrm{K} - \mathrm{Err}_e[\pi]$  is large.

Corollary 4. Let  $e \stackrel{\text{unif}}{\sim} \{-1,1\}^{[n] \times [m]}$ . Then if Assumptions 2, 3, and 4 hold,

$$
\text {e i t h e r} \underbrace {\mathbb {E} _ {e} \mathrm {K} _ {\star} - \operatorname {E r r} _ {e} [ \pi ] \geq \frac {n}{4 \epsilon_ {\text {p a c k}} ^ {2}}} _ {\text {(s u f f i c i e n t e x p l o r a t i o n)}}, \quad \text {o r} \underbrace {\mathbb {E} _ {e} \mathrm {K} - \operatorname {E r r} _ {e} [ \pi ] \geq \frac {c _ {\mathrm {L S}}}{1 0} T n m \nu_ {m} ^ {2} \epsilon_ {\text {p a c k}} ^ {2} - \gamma_ {\mathrm {l s}}} _ {\text {(l a r g e d e v i a t i o n f r o m o p t i m a l)}}. \tag {4.3}
$$

Proof. Let  $\widehat{e} = \widehat{e} (\widehat{K}_{\mathrm{LS}})$ , where  $\widehat{e}$  is the estimator from Lemma 4.6, and  $\widehat{K}_{\mathrm{LS}}$  is as defined in Lemma 4.4. Since this estimator only depends on  $\mathbf{x}_1,\ldots ,\mathbf{x}_{T / 2}$  and  $\mathbf{u}_1,\dots ,\mathbf{u}_{T / 2}$ , we see that if the first condition in Equation (4.3) (sufficient exploration) fails, then by Lemma 4.5, we have  $\mathbb{E}_e\mathbb{E}_{A_e,B_e,\mathsf{Alg}}[d_{\mathrm{ham}}(\widehat{e},e)]\geq \frac{nm}{4} = \frac{nm}{5} +\frac{nm}{20}$ . Thus, by Lemma 4.6, we have  $\frac{2\mathbb{E}_e\mathbb{E}_{A_e,B_e,\mathsf{Alg}}\|\widehat{K} - K_e\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\nu_m^2\epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}}^2}\geq \frac{nm}{5}$ , yielding  $\mathbb{E}_e\mathbb{E}_{A_e,B_e,\mathsf{Alg}}\|\widehat{K} - K_e\|_{\mathrm{F}}^2\geq \frac{1}{10} nm\nu_m^2\epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}}^2$ . The bound now follows from Lemma 4.4.

# 4.4 Completing the Proof

To conclude the proof, we show (Appendix F.6) that  $\mathbb{E}_e\mathrm{K - Err}_e\approx \mathbb{E}_e\mathrm{K}_{\star}\text{-Err}_e$  , so that the final bound follows by setting  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}}^2\approx \sqrt{1 / mT}$

Lemma 4.7. Under Assumptions 2 and 3, we have  $\mathbb{E}_e\mathrm{K}_{\star}\text{-Err}_e[\pi ]\leq 2\mathbb{E}_e\mathrm{K}\text{-Err}_e[\pi ] + 4nmT\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^4\epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}}^2$

Combining Lemma 4.7 with Corollary 4, we have

$$
\max _ {e} \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e} [ \pi ] \geq \mathbb {E} _ {e} \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e} [ \pi ] \geq \left(\frac {n}{8 \epsilon_ {\mathrm {p a c k}} ^ {2}} - 2 n m T \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {4} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {p a c k}} ^ {2}\right) \wedge \frac {c _ {\mathrm {L S}}}{1 0} T n m \nu_ {m} ^ {2} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {p a c k}} ^ {2}.
$$

Setting  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}}^2 = \frac{1}{32\|P_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2\sqrt{mT}}$  and substituting in  $n = d_{\mathbf{u}}$ , we find that as long as  $T$  is large enough such that Assumptions 2-4 hold,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max _ {e} \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e} [ \pi ] \gtrsim d _ {\mathbf {u}} \sqrt {m T} / \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \wedge \sqrt {d _ {\mathbf {u}} ^ {2} m T} \nu_ {m} ^ {2} / \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} - 1 \\ \gtrsim (1 \wedge \nu_ {m} ^ {2}) \sqrt {m d _ {\bf u} ^ {2} T} / \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}) - 1. \\ \end{array}
$$

Thus, by Lemma 4.4, we have that for a sufficiently small numerical constant  $C_{\mathrm{lb}}$  (which we choose to have value at most 1 without loss of generality),

$$
\max _ {e} \mathbb {E} \mathrm {R e g r e t} _ {e} [ \pi ] \geq 2 C _ {\mathrm {l b}} \frac {(1 \wedge \nu_ {m} ^ {2}) \sqrt {n ^ {2} m T}}{\| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}} - \frac {1}{2} - \gamma_ {\mathrm {e r r}} \geq 2 C _ {\mathrm {l b}} \frac {(1 \wedge \nu_ {m} ^ {2}) \sqrt {n ^ {2} m T}}{\| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}} - 7 d _ {\mathbf {x}} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3} \Psi_ {\star} ^ {2}.
$$

It follows that once

$$
T \geq c _ {1} \left(\| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {p} \left(n ^ {2} m \vee \frac {d _ {\mathbf {x}} ^ {2} \Psi_ {\star} ^ {4} \left(1 \vee \nu_ {m} ^ {- 4}\right)}{m n ^ {2}} \vee d _ {\mathbf {x}} \log \left(1 + d _ {\mathbf {x}} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}\right)\right), \right. \tag {4.4}
$$

where  $c_{1}$  and  $p$  sufficiently numerical constants, Assumptions 2 and 4 are indeed satisfied, so we have

$$
\max _ {e} \mathbb {E} \mathrm {R e g r e t} _ {e} [ \pi ] \geq C _ {\mathrm {l b}} \frac {(1 \wedge \nu_ {m} ^ {2}) \sqrt {d _ {\mathbf {u}} ^ {2} m T}}{\| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}} := \mathcal {R}.
$$

We now justify Assumption 3. Suppose the assumption fails, i.e. for some instance  $e$  the algorithm has  $\mathbb{E}\mathrm{Regret}_e[\pi ]\geq \frac{T}{6\Psi_\star^2d_{\mathbf{x}}} -\gamma_{\mathrm{err}}$ . Then since  $C_\mathrm{lb}\leq 1$  and  $\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq 1$ , we see that if  $\sqrt{T}\geq 12\Psi_{\star}^{2}d_{\mathbf{x}} / \sqrt{mn^{2}}$ , then  $\mathbb{E}\mathrm{Regret}_e[\pi ]\geq 2\mathcal{R} - \gamma_{\mathrm{err}}\geq \mathcal{R}$ . By taking  $c_{1}$  sufficiently large, we see that whenever Equation (4.4) holds, we have  $\mathbb{E}\mathrm{Regret}_e[\pi ]\geq 2\mathcal{R} - \gamma_{\mathrm{err}}\geq \mathcal{R}$  as desired.

To conclude, we verify that the construction is consistent with the scale parameter  $\epsilon_T$  from the theorem statement:

$$
\| A _ {e} - A _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \vee \| B _ {e} - B _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \stackrel {(i)} {\leq} n m \epsilon_ {\mathrm {p a c k}} ^ {2} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \stackrel {(i i)} {\leq} n \sqrt {m / T} \leq \epsilon_ {T},
$$

where  $(i)$  follows by Lemma 4.1, and  $(ii)$  follows by plugging in our choice for  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}}$ .

# 5 Algorithm and Proof of Upper Bound (Theorem 2)

We now formally describe our main algorithm, Algorithm 1, and prove that it attains the upper bound in Theorem 2. The algorithm is a variant of certainty equivalent control with continual  $\varepsilon$ -greedy exploration. In line with previous work [Dean et al., 2018, Cohen et al., 2019, Mania et al., 2019], the algorithm takes as input a controller  $K_{0}$  that is guaranteed to stabilize the system but otherwise may be arbitrarily suboptimal relative to  $K_{\star}$ . The algorithm proceeds in epochs of doubling length. At the beginning of epoch  $k$ , the algorithm uses an ordinary least squares subroutine (Algorithm 2) to form an estimate  $(\widehat{A}_k,\widehat{B}_k)$  for the system dynamics using data collected in the previous epoch. The algorithm then checks whether the estimate is sufficiently close to  $(A_{\star},B_{\star})$  for the perturbation bounds developed in Theorem 3 take effect; such closeness guarantees that the optimal controller for  $(\widehat{A}_k,\widehat{B}_k)$  stabilizes the system and has low regret. If the test fails, the algorithm falls back on the stabilizing controller  $K_{0}$  for the remainder of the epoch, adding exploratory noise with constant scale. Otherwise, if the test succeeds, the algorithm forms the certainty equivalent controller  $\widehat{K}_k\coloneqq K_\infty (\widehat{A}_k,\widehat{B}_k)$  and plays this for the remainder of the epoch, adding exploratory noise whose scale is carefully chosen to balance exploration and exploitation.

Preliminaries Before beginning the proof, let us first give some additional definitions and notation. We adopt the shorthand  $d \coloneqq d_{\mathbf{x}} + d_{\mathbf{u}}$  and define  $k_{\mathrm{fin}} = \lceil \log_2 T \rceil$ . For every controllers  $K$  for which  $(A + BK)$  is stable, we define  $P_{\infty}(K; A, B) \coloneqq \mathsf{dlyap}(A + BK, R_{\mathbf{x}} + K^{\top}R_{\mathbf{u}}K)$ . It is a standard fact (see e.g. Lemma B.6) that such controllers have  $\mathcal{J}_{A,B}[K] = \operatorname{tr}(P_{\infty}(K; A, B))$ .

We make will make heavy use of the following system parameters for the controllers used within Algorithm 1:

$$
P _ {k} := P _ {\infty} (\widehat {K} _ {k}; A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}), \mathcal {P} _ {0} := \frac {\mathcal {I} _ {0}}{d _ {\mathbf {x}}} \leq \| P _ {\infty} (K _ {0}; A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}},
$$

$$
\mathcal {J} _ {k} := \mathcal {J} _ {A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}} [ \widehat {K} _ {k} ], \qquad \mathcal {J} _ {0} := J _ {A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}} [ K _ {0} ],
$$

$$
A _ {\mathrm {c l}, k} := A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} \widehat {K} _ {k}, \qquad A _ {\mathrm {c l}, 0} := A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} K _ {0}.
$$

# 5.1 Proof

We begin the proof by showing that the initial estimation phase (in which the algorithm uses the stabilizing controller  $K_{0}$ ) ensures that various regularity conditions hold for the epochs  $k \geq k_{\mathrm{safe}}$  (in which the algorithm uses the certainty-equivalent controller). One such regularity condition bounds the  $\mathcal{H}_{\infty}$ -norm, which describes the worst-case response of a system to perturbations. We recall the following definition from Appendix B:

Definition 5.1 ( $\mathcal{H}_{\infty}$  norm). For any stable  $\tilde{A} \in \mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathbf{x}}^2}$  (e.g.  $A + BK_{\infty}(A,B)$ ), we define  $\| \tilde{A} \|_{\mathcal{H}_{\infty}} \coloneqq \sup_{z \in \mathbb{C}: |z| = 1} \| (zI - \tilde{A})^{-1} \|_{\mathrm{op}}$ .

The following result is proved in Appendix G.1.

Lemma 5.1 (Correctness of Perturbations). On the event

$$
\mathcal {E} _ {\text {s a f e}} := \left\{\left\| \left[ \widehat {A} _ {k _ {\text {s a f e}}} - A _ {\star} \mid \widehat {B} _ {k _ {\text {s a f e}}} - B _ {\star} \right] \right\| _ {\text {o p}} ^ {2} \leq \operatorname {C o n f} _ {k _ {\text {s a f e}}} \right\},
$$

the following bounds hold for all  $k \geq k_{\mathrm{safe}}$ :

Algorithm 1: Certainty Equivalent Control with Continual Exploration  
1 Input: Stabilizing controller  $K_{0}$  confidence parameter  $\delta$    
2 Initialize: safe  $\leftarrow$  False.   
3 Play  $\mathbf{u}_1\sim \mathcal{N}(0,I)$    
4 for  $k = 2,3,\ldots$  do   
5 Let  $\tau_{k}\gets 2^{k}$    
6 /*OLS estimator and covariance matrix using samples  $\tau_{k - 1},\dots ,\tau_{k} - 1$  . See Algorithm 2. */   
7 Set  $(\widehat{A}_k,\widehat{B}_k,\Lambda_k)\gets \mathrm{OLS}(k)$    
8 if safe  $=$  False then   
9 Conf  $k\gets 6\lambda_{\min}(\Lambda_k)^{-1}\big(d\log 5 + \log \big(4k^2\det (3(\Lambda_k) / \delta)\big)$  (infinite if  $\Lambda_{k}\neq 0$    
10 if  $\Lambda_{k}\succeq I$  and  $1 / \mathrm{Conf}_{k}\geq 9C_{\mathrm{safe}}(\widehat{A}_{k},\widehat{B}_{k})^{2}$  then safe  $\leftarrow$  True,  $k_{\mathrm{safe}}\gets k$    
11  $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{safe}},\sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^{2}\gets \mathrm{SafeRoundInit}(\widehat{A}_{k},\widehat{B}_{k},\mathrm{Conf}_{k},\delta). / / \mathrm{Confidence~ball~(Algorithm~3)}.$    
12 else for  $t = \tau_{k},\ldots ,2\tau_{k} - 1$  play  $\mathbf{u}_t = K_0\mathbf{x}_t + \mathbf{g}_t$  , where  $\mathbf{g}_t\sim \mathcal{N}(0,I)$    
14 else   
15 Let  $(\widetilde{A}_k,\widetilde{B}_k)$  denote the euclidean projection of  $(\widehat{A}_k,\widehat{B}_k)$  onto  $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{safe}}$    
16  $\widehat{K}_k\gets K_\infty (\widetilde{A}_k,\widetilde{B}_k)$    
17 for  $t = \tau_{k},\ldots ,2\tau_{k} - 1$  do   
18 Play  $\mathbf{u}_t = \widehat{K}_k\mathbf{x}_t + \sigma_k\mathbf{g}_t$  , where  $\mathbf{g}_t\sim \mathcal{N}(0,I)$  , and  $\sigma_k^2 := \min \{1,\sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^2\tau_k^{-1 / 2}\}$

1.  $\mathcal{J}_k - \mathcal{J}_{\star} \leq C_{\mathrm{est}}(A_{\star}, B_{\star}) \left(\| \widehat{A} - A_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{F}}^2 + \| \widehat{B} - B_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{F}}^2\right) \lesssim \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}^8 \left(\| \widehat{A} - A_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{F}}^2 + \| \widehat{B} - B_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{F}}^2\right)$ .  
2.  $\mathcal{I}_k\lesssim \mathcal{I}_{\star}$  , and  $\| P_k\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim \| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}$  
3.  $\| \widehat{K}_k\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2\leq \frac{21}{20}\| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}$  
4.  $\| A_{\mathrm{cl},k}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\infty}}\lesssim \| A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\infty}}\lesssim \| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3 / 2}.$  
5.  $A_{\mathrm{cl},k}^{\top}\mathsf{dlyap}[A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}]A_{\mathrm{cl},k} \preceq (1 - \frac{1}{2}\| \mathsf{dlyap}[A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}] \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})$ , where  $I \preceq \mathsf{dlyap}[A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}] \preceq P_{\star}$ , where we recall the shorthand  $\mathsf{dlyap}[A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}] = \mathsf{dlyap}(A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}, I)$ .  
6.  $\sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^2\approx \sqrt{d_{\mathbf{x}}}\| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{9 / 2}\Psi_{B_\star}\sqrt{\log\frac{\|P_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{\delta}}.$

We will verify at the end of the proof that  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}}$  indeed holds with high probability. We remark that Part 5 of the above lemma plays a role similar to that of "sequential strong stability" in Cohen et al. [2019, Definition 2]. By using dlyap[  $A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}]$  as a common Lyapunov function, we remove the complications involved in applying sequential strong stability.

Building on this result, we provide (Appendix G.2) a decomposition of the algorithm's regret which holds conditioned on  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}}$ .

Lemma 5.2 (Regret Decomposition on Safe Rounds). There is an event  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{reg}}$  which holds with

probability at least  $1 - \frac{\delta}{8}$  such that, on  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{reg}} \cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}}$ , following bound holds

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k _ {\text {s a f e}}}} ^ {T} \left(\mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {x}} \mathbf {x} _ {t} + \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} \mathbf {u} _ {t} - \mathcal {J} _ {\star}\right) \lesssim \sum_ {k = k _ {\text {s a f e}}} ^ {k _ {\text {f i n}}} \tau_ {k} \left(J _ {k} - \mathcal {J} _ {\star}\right) + \log T \max  _ {k \leq \log_ {T}} \| \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \tag {5.1} \\ + \sqrt {T} \left(d _ {\mathbf {u}} \sigma_ {\text {i n}} ^ {2} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\text {o p}}\right) + \sqrt {d \log (1 / \delta)} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\text {o p}} ^ {4}) \\ + \log^ {2} \frac {1}{\delta} \left(1 + \sqrt {d} \sigma_ {\mathrm {i n}} ^ {2} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2}\right) \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {4}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Let us unpack the terms that arise in Equation (5.1). The term  $\sum_{k = k_{\mathrm{safe}}}^{k_{\mathrm{fin}}}\tau_k(\mathcal{J}_k - \mathcal{J}_{\star})$  captures the suboptimality of the controllers  $\widehat{K}_k$  selected at each epoch. We bound this term by using that, in light of Lemma 5.2, we have  $\mathcal{J}_k - \mathcal{J}_{\star} \propto \| \widehat{A}_k - A_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{F}}^2 + \| \widehat{B}_k - B_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{F}}^2$ . The next term,  $\log T \cdot \max_{k \leq \log_T} \| \mathbf{x}_{\tau_k} \|^2$ , is of lower order, and roughly captures the penalty for switching controllers at each epoch. The term proportional to  $\sqrt{T}$  captures both the penalty for injecting exploratory noise into the system (which incurs a dependence on  $d_{\mathbf{u}}$ ), as well as random fluctuations in the cost coming from the underlying noise process. Finally, the term on the last line of the display is also of lower order (poly( $\log T$ )). To proceed, we show that the norms  $\| \mathbf{x}_{\tau_k} \|$  appearing in the second term are well-behaved.

Lemma 5.3. There is an event  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{bound}}$  which holds with probability at least  $1 - \frac{\delta}{8}$  such that, conditioned on  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}} \cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{bound}}$ ,

$$
\| \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} \| \leq \sqrt {\mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} ^ {\top} \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l} , \star} ] \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}}} \lesssim \sqrt {\Psi_ {B _ {\star}} \mathcal {J} _ {0} \log (1 / \delta)} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3 / 2}, \quad \forall k \geq k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}.
$$

This bound is quite crude, but is sufficient for our purposes. We give a concise proof (Appendix G.3) using that in light of Lemma 5.1,  $\mathsf{dlyap}[A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}]$  acts as a Lyapunov function for all the systems  $A_{\mathrm{cl},k}$  conditioned on  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}}$ .

To bound the error terms  $\mathcal{I}_k - \mathcal{I}_{\star}$  appearing in Equation (5.1) we prove (Appendix G.4) the following bound, which ensures the correctness of the estimators  $(\widehat{A}_k,\widehat{B}_k)$  once  $k\geq k_{\mathrm{safe}}$ . [ms: this is the lemma]

Lemma 5.4. Define  $\tau_{\mathrm{ls}} \coloneqq d\max \{1, d_{\mathbf{u}} / d_{\mathbf{x}}\}\left(\|P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}\mathcal{P}_{0} + \|P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{11}\Psi_{B_{\star}}^{6}\right)\log \frac{d\|P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{\delta}$ . The, There is an event  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ls}}$ , which holds with probability at least  $1 - \delta/8$ , such that conditioned on  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ls}} \cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}} \cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{bound}}$

$$
\| \widehat {A} _ {k} - A _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + \| \widehat {B} _ {k} - B _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \lesssim \frac {d _ {\mathbf {x}} d _ {\mathbf {u}} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}}{\sigma_ {\mathrm {i n}} ^ {2} \tau_ {k} ^ {1 / 2}} \log (\frac {1}{\delta}) + \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3} \frac {d _ {\mathbf {x}} ^ {2}}{\tau_ {k}} \log (\frac {1}{\delta}) ^ {2}, \quad \forall k: c \tau_ {\mathrm {l s}} \leq \tau_ {k} \leq T,
$$

where  $c > 0$  is a universal constant.

We now put all of these pieces together to prove the final regret bound. Henceforth, we condition on the event  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}} \cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{bound}} \cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{reg}} \cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ls}}$ . To begin, consider the sum of errors  $\mathcal{I}_k - \mathcal{I}_\star$  in Equation (5.1).

# Algorithm 2:  $\mathrm{OLS}(k)$

1 Input: Examples  $\mathbf{x}_{\tau_k - 1},\ldots ,\mathbf{x}_{\tau_k}$ $\mathbf{u}_{\tau_{k - 1}},\dots ,\mathbf{u}_{\tau_k - 1}.$  
2 Return  $(\widehat{A}_k,\widehat{B}_k,\Lambda_k)$  , where

$$
\left[ \begin{array}{c c} \widehat {A} _ {k} & \widehat {B} _ {k} \end{array} \right] \gets \left(\sum_ {t = \tau_ {k - 1}} ^ {\tau_ {k} - 1} \mathbf {x} _ {t + 1} \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {u} _ {t} \end{array} \right] ^ {\top}\right) \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k} ^ {\dagger}, \quad \mathrm {a n d} \quad \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k} \gets \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k - 1}} ^ {\tau_ {k} - 1} (\mathbf {x} _ {t}, \mathbf {u} _ {t}) (\mathbf {x} _ {t}, \mathbf {u} _ {t}) ^ {\top}.
$$

# Algorithm 3: SafeRoundInit  $(\widehat{A},\widehat{B},\mathrm{Conf},\delta)$

1 Input: Stabilizable pair  $(\widehat{A},\widehat{B},\mathrm{Conf},\delta)$  
2 Return  $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{safe}} := \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{op}}(\mathrm{Conf}; \widehat{A}, \widehat{B})$  and

$$
\sigma_ {\mathrm {i n}} ^ {2} := \sqrt {d _ {\mathbf {x}}} \| P _ {\infty} (\widehat {A}, \widehat {B}) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {9 / 2} \max \{1, \| \widehat {B} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \} \sqrt {\log \frac {\| P _ {\infty} (\widehat {A} , \widehat {B}) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\delta}}.
$$

We apply Lemma G.2 followed by the bound on  $\mathcal{J}_k\lesssim \mathcal{J}_{\star}$  from Lemma 5.1, which yields

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}} ^ {k _ {\mathrm {f i n}}} \tau_ {k} (\mathcal {J} _ {k} - \mathcal {J} _ {\star}) + \log T \max _ {k \leq \log_ {T}} \| \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} \| ^ {2} \\ \leq \sum_ {k > \tau_ {\mathrm {l s}}} \tau_ {k} \left(\mathcal {J} _ {k} - \mathcal {J} _ {\star}\right) + \mathcal {J} _ {\star} \sum_ {k: \tau_ {k} \leq c \tau_ {\mathrm {l s}}} \tau_ {k} + \sqrt {\Psi_ {B _ {\star}} \mathcal {J} _ {0} \log (1 / \delta)} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3 / 2} \log T \\ \lesssim \left\{\sum_ {k > \tau_ {\mathrm {l s}}} \tau_ {k} \left(\mathcal {J} _ {k} - \mathcal {J} _ {\star}\right) \right\} + \tau_ {\mathrm {l s}} \mathcal {J} _ {\star} + \sqrt {\Psi_ {B _ {\star}} \mathcal {J} _ {0} \log (1 / \delta)} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3 / 2} \log T \\ \lesssim \left\{\sum_ {k > \tau_ {\mathrm {l s}}} \tau_ {k} (\mathcal {J} _ {k} - \mathcal {J} _ {\star}) \right\} + d _ {\mathbf {x}} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \tau_ {\mathrm {l s}} \log \frac {1}{\delta}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last line uses that  $\delta \leq 1 / T$  to combine the lower-order terms in the line preceding it. Next, using the bound  $\mathcal{J}_k - \mathcal{J}_{\star} \lesssim \| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^8\left(\| A_{\star} - \widehat{A}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^2 + \| B_{\star} - \widehat{B}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^2\right)$  from Lemma 5.1 followed by the error bound in Lemma 5.4, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k > \tau_ {\mathrm {l s}}} \tau_ {k} (\mathcal {J} _ {k} - \mathcal {J} _ {\star}) \leq \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {8} \sum_ {k > \tau_ {\mathrm {l s}}} \frac {d _ {\mathbf {u}} d _ {\mathbf {x}}}{\sigma_ {\mathrm {i n}} ^ {2} \sqrt {\tau_ {k}}} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \log \frac {1}{\delta} + \frac {d _ {\mathbf {x}} ^ {2}}{\tau_ {k}} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3} \log^ {2} \frac {1}{\delta} \\ \lesssim \frac {d _ {\mathbf {u}} d _ {\mathbf {x}} \sqrt {T}}{\sigma_ {\mathrm {i n}} ^ {2}} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 0} \log \frac {1}{\delta} + \underbrace {d _ {\mathbf {x}} ^ {2} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3} \log^ {2} \frac {1}{\delta} \log T} _ {\lesssim d _ {\mathbf {x}} \tau_ {\mathrm {l s}} \log^ {2} \frac {1}{\delta}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where again we use  $\log T \lesssim \log(1 / \delta)$ . Combining the computations so far shows that

$$
\sum_ {k = k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}} ^ {k _ {\mathrm {f i n}}} \tau_ {k} (\mathcal {J} _ {k} - \mathcal {J} _ {\star}) + \log T \max _ {k \leq \log T} \| \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} \| ^ {2} \leq \frac {d _ {\mathbf {u}} d _ {\mathbf {x}} \sqrt {T}}{\sigma_ {\mathrm {i n}} ^ {2}} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 0} \log \frac {1}{\delta} + d _ {\mathbf {x}} \tau_ {\mathrm {l s}} \log^ {2} \frac {1}{\delta}.
$$

Hence, on  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}} \cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{bound}} \cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{reg}} \cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{lsm}}$  the regret in the episodes  $k \geq k_{\mathrm{safe}}$  decomposes into a component scaling with  $\sqrt{T}$  and a component scaling with  $\log T$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}}} ^ {T} (\mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {x}} \mathbf {x} _ {t} + \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} \mathbf {u} _ {t} - \mathcal {I} _ {\star}) \\ \lesssim \underbrace {\sqrt {T} \left(d _ {\mathbf {u}} \sigma_ {\mathrm {i n}} ^ {2} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \sqrt {d \log (1 / \delta)} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {4} + \frac {d _ {\mathbf {u}} d _ {\mathbf {x}}}{\sigma_ {\mathrm {i n}} ^ {2}} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 0} \log \frac {1}{\delta}\right)} _ {\sqrt {T} \text {- c o m p o n e n t})} \\ + \underbrace {(1 + \sqrt {d} \sigma_ {\mathrm {i n}} ^ {2} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2}) \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {4} \log^ {2} \frac {1}{\delta} + d _ {\mathbf {x}} \tau_ {\mathrm {l s}} \log^ {2} \frac {1}{\delta}} _ {((\mathrm {p o l y} (\log T) - \mathrm {c o m p o n e n t})}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Using that  $\sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^2 \approx \sqrt{d_{\mathbf{x}}} \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{9/2} \Psi_{B_{\star}} \sqrt{\log \frac{\| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}}{\delta}}$  (Lemma 5.1) and recalling that  $d = d_{\mathbf{x}} + d_{\mathbf{u}}$ , we upper bound these terms as

$$
(\sqrt {T} \text {- c o m p o n e n t}) \lesssim \sqrt {T d _ {\mathbf {u}} ^ {2} d _ {\mathbf {x}} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 1} \log \frac {1}{\delta}},
$$

$$
(\operatorname {p o l y} (\log T) \text {- c o m p o n e n t}) \lesssim d _ {\mathbf {x}} \tau_ {\mathrm {l s}} \log^ {2} \frac {1}{\delta}.
$$

We conclude that conditioned on  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}}\cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{bound}}\cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{reg}}\cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ls}}$

$$
\sum_ {t = \tau_ {k _ {\text {s a f e}}}} ^ {T} \left(\mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {x}} \mathbf {x} _ {t} + \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} \mathbf {u} _ {t} - \mathcal {J} _ {\star}\right) \lesssim \sqrt {T d _ {\mathbf {u}} ^ {2} d _ {\mathbf {x}} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 1} \log \frac {1}{\delta}} + d _ {\mathbf {x}} \tau_ {\mathrm {l s}} \log^ {2} \frac {1}{\delta}. \tag {5.2}
$$

To finish the proof, we (a) verify that  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}}$  indeed holds with high probability, and (b) bound the regret contribution of the initial rounds (proof given in Appendix G.5).

Lemma 5.5. The event  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}}$  holds with probability  $1 - \frac{\delta}{2}$ , and the following event  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{reg,init}}$  holds with probability  $1 - \frac{\delta}{8}$ :

$$
\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau_ {k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}} - 1} \mathbf {x} _ {t, 0} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {x}} \mathbf {x} _ {t, 0} + \mathbf {u} _ {t, 0} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} \mathbf {u} _ {t, 0} \lesssim \mathcal {P} _ {0} d ^ {2} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 0} (1 + \| K _ {0} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}) \log \frac {d \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \mathcal {P} _ {0}}{\delta} \log \frac {1}{\delta}.
$$

Thus,  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}} \cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{bound}} \cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{reg}} \cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ls}} \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{reg,init}}$  holds with total probability at least  $1 - \delta$ , and conditioned on this event Lemma 5.5 and Equation (5.2) imply

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {R e g r e t} _ {T} [ \mathsf {A l g}; A _ {\star}, B _ {\star} ] = \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left(\mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {x}} \mathbf {x} _ {t} + \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} \mathbf {u} _ {t} - \mathcal {J} _ {\star}\right) \\ \lesssim \sqrt {T d _ {\mathbf {u}} ^ {2} d _ {\mathbf {x}} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 1} \log \frac {1}{\delta}} \\ + \mathcal {P} _ {0} d ^ {2} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 0} (1 + \| K _ {0} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}) \log \frac {d \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \mathcal {P} _ {0}}{\delta} \log \frac {1}{\delta} + d _ {\mathbf {x}} \tau_ {\mathrm {l s}} \log^ {2} \frac {1}{\delta} \\ \end{array}
$$

Recalling that  $\tau_{\mathrm{ls}} := d\max \{1, d_{\mathbf{u}} / d_{\mathbf{x}}\}$  ( $\| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3} \mathcal{P}_{0} + \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{11} \Psi_{B_{\star}}^{6}$ ) log  $\frac{d \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}}{\delta}$ , that  $\mathcal{P}_0, \| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}} \Psi_{B_\star} \geq 1$  and that  $d \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq d \mathcal{I}_{\star} \leq d \mathcal{J}_0 = d^2 \mathcal{P}_0$ , we move to a simplified upper bound:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathrm {R e g r e t} _ {T} [ \mathsf {A l g}; A _ {\star}, B _ {\star} ] \lesssim \sqrt {T d _ {\mathbf {u}} ^ {2} d _ {\mathbf {x}} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 1} \log \frac {1}{\delta}} \\ + d ^ {2} r \mathcal {P} _ {0} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {6} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 1} (1 + \| K _ {0} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}) \log \frac {d ^ {2} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \mathcal {P} _ {0}}{\delta} \log^ {2} \frac {1}{\delta}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $r = \max \{1, \frac{d_{\mathbf{u}}}{d_{\mathbf{x}}}\}$ . Since the square of  $d\Psi_{B_\star}$  inside the logarithm contributes only a constant factor, we may remove it in the final bound. Moreover, since This concludes the proof.

# 6 Conclusion

We have established that the asymptotically optimal regret for the online LQR problem is  $\widetilde{\Theta} (\sqrt{d_{\mathbf{u}}^2d_{\mathbf{x}}T})$ , and that this rate is attained by  $\varepsilon$ -greedy exploration. We are hopeful that the our new analysis techniques, especially our perturbation bounds, will find broader use within the non-asymptotic theory of control and beyond. Going forward our work raises a number of interesting conceptual questions. Are there broader classes of "easy" reinforcement learning problems beyond LQR for which naive exploration attains optimal sample complexity, or is LQR a fluke? Conversely, is there a more demanding (eg, robust) version of the LQR problem for which more sophisticated exploration techniques such as robust synthesis [Dean et al., 2018] or optimism in the face of uncertainty [Abbasi-Yadtori and Szepesvari, 2011, Cohen et al., 2019] are required to attain optimal regret? On the purely technical side, recall that while our upper and lower bound match in terms of dependence on  $d_{\mathbf{u}}$ ,  $d_{\mathbf{x}}$ , and  $T$ , they differ in their polynomial dependence on  $\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ . Does closing this gap require new algorithmic techniques, or will a better analysis suffice?
