# A.2 Organization of the Appendices

The appendix is divided into two parts. Part I establishes the main technical tools used throughout the upper and lower bounds. Appendix B describes and proves our main perturbation bounds, deferring additional proof details to Appendix C. Appendix D proves guarantees for the Self-Bounding ODE method, summarized in Corollary 3, as well as a slightly more general statement for generic self-bounding relations, Theorem 13. This part of the appendix concludes with Appendix E.1,

which describes a set of tools for analyzing ordinary least squares estimation, which we use in the proofs of both our upper and lower bounds.

Part II provides omitted details from the proofs of our main results. Specifically, Appendix F proves the constituent lemmas for the lower bound from Section 4, and Appendix G does the same for the proof of the upper bound given in Section 5.

# Part I

# Technical Tools

# B Main Perturbation Bounds

Preliminaries Throughout, we shall use extensively the dlyap operator, which we recall here.

Definition 3.1 (Discrete Lyapunov Equation). Let  $X, Y \in \mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathbf{x}} \times d_{\mathbf{x}}}$  with  $Y = Y^{\top}$  and  $\rho(X) < 1$ . We let  $\mathcal{T}_X[P] := X^\top PX - P$ , and let  $\mathrm{dlyap}(X,Y)$  denote the unique PSD solution  $\mathcal{T}_X[P] = Y$ . We let  $\mathrm{dlyap}[X] := \mathrm{dlyap}(X,I)$ .

We shall need to describe the "  $P$  " -matrix analogue of the functional  $\mathcal{I}$

Definition B.1. Suppose that  $(A_{\star} + B_{\star}K)$  is stable. We define  $P_{\infty}(K;A_{\star},B_{\star})\coloneqq \mathsf{dlyap}(A_{\star} + B_{\star}K,R_{\mathbf{x}} + K^{\top}R_{\mathbf{u}}K.$

It is a standard fact (see e.g. Lemma B.6) that  $\mathcal{J}_{A_{\star},B_{\star}}[K] = \mathrm{tr}(P_{\infty}(K;A_{\star},B_{\star}))$  whenever  $A_{\star} + B_{\star}K$  is stable. We also recall the definition of the  $\mathcal{H}_{\infty}$ -norm.

Definition 5.1 ( $\mathcal{H}_{\infty}$  norm). For any stable  $\tilde{A} \in \mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathbf{x}}^2}$  (e.g.  $A + BK_{\infty}(A,B)$ ), we define  $\| \tilde{A}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\infty}} \coloneqq \sup_{z \in \mathbb{C}:|z| = 1} \| (zI - \tilde{A})^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ .

Organization of Appendix B The remainder of this appendix is organized as follows. Appendix B.1 states our main perturbation upper bounds, and provides proofs in terms of various supporting propositions. Appendix B.2 walks the reader through the relevant computations of various derivatives. Appendix B.3 states numerous technical tools which we use in the proofs of our main perturbation bounds, and finally Appendix B.4 proves the supporting propositions leveraged in Appendix B.1. Many supporting proofs are deferred to Appendix C.

# B.1 Main Results

# B.1.1 Main Perturbation Upper Bound

Recall  $C_{\mathrm{safe}}(A_{\star}, B_{\star}) = 54 \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{5}$ , and  $C_{\mathrm{est}}(A_{\star}, B_{\star}) = 142 \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{8}$ . We state a strengthening of our main perturbation bound from the main text (Theorem 3) here.

Theorem 5. Let  $(A_{\star},B_{\star})$  be a stabilizable system. Given an alternate pair of matrices  $(\widehat{A},\widehat{B})$ , for each  $\circ \in \{\mathrm{op},F\}$  define  $\epsilon_{\circ} := \max \{\| \widehat{A} - A_{\star} \|_{\circ}, \| \widehat{B} - B_{\star} \|_{\circ}\}$ . Then if  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{op}} \leq 1 / C_{\mathrm{safe}}(A_{\star},B_{\star})$ ,

1.  $\| P_{\infty}(\widehat{A}, \widehat{B}) \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq 1.0835 \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}$  and  $\| B_{\star}(K_{\star} - K_{\infty}(\widehat{A}, \widehat{B})) \|_2 < \frac{1}{5 \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3/2}}$ .  
2.  $\mathcal{J}_{A_{\star},B_{\star}}[K_{\infty}(\widehat{A},\widehat{B})] - \mathcal{J}_{A_{\star},B_{\star}}^{\star}\leq C_{\mathrm{est}}(A_{\star},B_{\star})\epsilon_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.$  
3.  $\| P_{\infty}(K_{\infty}(\widehat{A},\widehat{B});A_{\star},B_{\star}) - P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{\mathrm{est}}(A_{\star},B_{\star})\epsilon_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}$  
4. Moreover,  $P_{\infty}(K_{\infty}(\widehat{A}, \widehat{B}); A_{\star}, B_{\star}) \preceq (21/20)P_{\star}$ .

Proof. Throughout, we use  $P_{\star} \succeq I$  (see Lemma 4.2). This theorem requires two constituent results. First, we have a perturbation bound for  $P_{\infty}$  and  $K_{\infty}$ , which refines Proposition 4, and is proven in Section B.4.1.

Proposition 6. Let  $(A_{\star},B_{\star})$  be a stabilizable system, and define the DARE solution  $P_{\star} := P_{\infty}(A_{\star},B_{\star})$  and controller  $K_{\star} = K_{\infty}(A_{\star},B_{\star})$ . Given an alternate pair of matrices  $(\widehat{A},\widehat{B})$ , define for norms  $\circ \in \{\mathrm{op},\mathrm{F}\}$  the error  $\epsilon_{\circ} := \max \{\| A_{\star} - \widehat{A}\|_{\circ}, \| B_{\star} - \widehat{B}\|_{\circ}\}$ . Then, if  $\alpha := 8\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\epsilon_{\mathrm{op}} < 1$ , the pair  $(\widehat{A},\widehat{B})$  is stabilizable, and

$$
\| P _ {\infty} (\widehat {A}, \widehat {B}) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq (1 - \alpha) ^ {- 1 / 2} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}},
$$

$$
\left\| R _ {\mathbf {u}} ^ {1 / 2} \left(K _ {\infty} (\widehat {A}, \widehat {B}) - K _ {\star}\right) \right\| _ {\circ} \leq 7 (1 - \alpha) ^ {- 7 / 4} \left\| P _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {7 / 2} \epsilon_ {\circ},
$$

$$
\left\| B _ {\star} \left(K _ {\infty} (\widehat {A}, \widehat {B}) - K _ {\star}\right) \right\| _ {\circ} \leq 8 (1 - \alpha) ^ {- 7 / 4} \left\| P _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {7 / 2} \epsilon_ {\circ}.
$$

In addition, if  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{op}} \leq 32 \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}$ , then

$$
\| P _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} B (K _ {\infty} (\widehat {A}, \widehat {B}) - K _ {\star}) \| _ {\circ} \leq 9 (1 - \alpha) ^ {- 7 / 4} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {7 / 2} \epsilon_ {\circ}.
$$

Next, we have a perturbation bound for the  $\mathcal{J}$ -functional as the controller  $K$ -is varied. The proof is deferred to Section B.4.2.

Proposition 7. Fix any controller  $K$  satisfying  $\| B_{\star}(K - K_{\star})\| _2\leq 1 / 5\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3 / 2}$ . Then,

$$
\mathcal {J} _ {A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}} [ K ] - \mathcal {J} _ {A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}} \leq \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \max  \left\{\| K - K _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}, \| P _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} B _ {\star} (K - K _ {\star}) \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \right\},
$$

$$
\left\| P _ {\infty} \left(K; A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}\right) - P _ {\infty} \left(A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}\right) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \left\| P _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \max  \left\{\left\| K - K _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}, \left\| P _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} B _ {\star} \left(K - K _ {\star}\right) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \right\}.
$$

Now, observe that  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{op}} \leq 1 / 54 \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{5} < 1 / 8 \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}$  and  $\alpha = 8 \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2} \epsilon_{\mathrm{op}}$ , Proposition 6 gives that

$$
\left\| P _ {\infty} (\widehat {A}, \widehat {B}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \left\| P _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} / \sqrt {1 - 8 / 5 4} \leq 1. 0 8 3 5 \left\| P _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}},
$$

and that

$$
\begin{array}{l} 5 \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3 / 2} \cdot \| B _ {\star} \left(K _ {\infty} (\widehat {A}, \widehat {B}) - K _ {\star}\right) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq 8 (1 - \alpha) ^ {- 7 / 4} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {7 / 2} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq 4 0 (1 - \alpha) ^ {- 7 / 4} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {5} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq 4 0 / 5 4 \cdot (1 - 8 / 5 4) ^ {- 7 / 4} <   1. \\ \end{array}
$$

Hence, for such  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{op}}$ , we find from Proposition 7 followed by Proposition C.3.1 that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {J} _ {A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}} [ K ] - \mathcal {J} _ {A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}} \leq \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \max  \left\{\| R _ {\mathbf {u}} ^ {1 / 2} (K - K _ {\star}) \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}, \| P _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} B _ {\star} (K - K _ {\star}) \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \right\} \\ \leq 8 1 \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {8} (1 - \alpha) ^ {- 7 / 2} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \\ \leq 1 4 2 \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p C F}} ^ {8}, \\ \end{array}
$$

and similarly, using  $\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq 1$

$$
\| P _ {\infty} (K; A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}) - P _ {\infty} (A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq 1 4 2 \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {8} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \leq \frac {1}{2 0},
$$

yielding  $P_{\infty}(K;A_{\star},B_{\star})\preceq (1 + \frac{1}{20})P_{\star}$  as  $P_{\star}\succeq I$

# B.1.2 Perturbation of  $\mathcal{H}_{\infty}$  Norm and Lyapunov Functions

Next, we establish perturbation bounds on the  $\mathcal{H}_{\infty}$  norm of the closed loop system, and show that all perturbed closed loop systems share a common Lyapunov function.

Theorem 8. Let  $A_{\star}, B_{\star}$  be stabilizable, and let  $(\widehat{A}, \widehat{B})$  satisfy the conditions of Theorem 5, with  $R_{\mathbf{x}} \succeq I$ , and  $R_{\mathbf{u}} = I$ . Define  $A_{\mathrm{cl},\star} \coloneqq A_{\star} + B_{\star}K_{\star}$ , and given  $(\widehat{A}, \widehat{B}) \in \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{op}}(\epsilon_{\star}, A_{\star}, B_{\star})$ , define and  $A_{\mathrm{cl},\widehat{\star}} \coloneqq A_{\star} + B_{\star}K_{\infty}(\widehat{A}, \widehat{B})$ . Then,

1.  $I \preceq \mathrm{dlyap}[A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}] \preceq P_{\star}$ .  
2.  $\| A_{\mathrm{cl},\widehat{\star}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\infty}}\leq 2\| A_{\mathrm{cl},0}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\infty}}\leq 4\| \mathsf{d}|\mathsf{yap}[A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}]\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3 / 2}\leq 4\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3 / 2}.$  
3.  $A_{\mathrm{cl},\widehat{\star}}^{\top}\cdot \mathsf{dlyap}[A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}]\cdot A_{\mathrm{cl},\widehat{\star}}\preceq (1 - \frac{1}{2}\| \mathsf{dlyap}[A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})\mathsf{dlyap}[A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}]\preceq (1 - \frac{1}{2}\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})\mathsf{dlyap}[A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}]$

Proof of Part 1. We can directly verify  $\mathsf{dlyap}[A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}]\succeq I$  from the definition, and  $\mathsf{dlyap}[A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}]\preceq P_{\star}$  by Lemma B.5.

Proof of Part 2. We use a general-purpose perturbation bound for the  $\mathcal{H}_{\infty}$  norm, proved in B.4.3.

Proposition 9 ( $\mathcal{H}_{\infty}$  Bounds). Fix  $u \in (0,1)$ , and matrixes  $A_{\mathrm{safe}}, A_1 \in \mathbb{R}^{d_x^2}$  with  $A_{\mathrm{safe}}$  stable. Then if  $\|A_1 - A_{\mathrm{safe}}\| \leq \frac{\alpha}{\|A_{\mathrm{safe}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\infty}}}$ ,  $\|A_1\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\infty}} \leq \frac{1}{1-\alpha}\|A_{\mathrm{safe}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\infty}}$ .

From Part 1 of Theorem 5,

$$
\left\| A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star} - A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \widehat {\star}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \left\| B _ {\star} \left(K _ {\infty} \left(A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}\right) - K _ {\infty} (\widehat {A}, \widehat {B})\right) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} <   \frac {1}{5 \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3 / 2}}. \tag {B.1}
$$

By Lemma B.11 followed by Lemma B.5, we have that

$$
\| A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star} \| _ {\mathcal {H} _ {\infty}} \leq 2 \| \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star} ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3 / 2} \leq 2 \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3 / 2}.
$$

Therefore, since  $\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq 1$  , we have

$$
\| A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star} - A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \widehat {\star}} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} <   \frac {1}{(5 / 2) \| A _ {\mathrm {c l} , \star} \| _ {\mathcal {H} _ {\infty}}} \leq \frac {1}{2 \| A _ {\mathrm {c l} , \star} \| _ {\mathcal {H} _ {\infty}}}.
$$

Proposition 9 then implies that  $\| A_{\mathrm{cl},\widehat{\star}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\infty}}\leq 2\| A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\infty}}$  . Moreover, by Lemma B.11, we can upper bound this in term by  $4\| \mathrm{dlyap}[A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}]\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3 / 2}\leq 4\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3 / 2}$

Proof of Part 3. Here, we use a perturbation bound which we prove from first principles, without the self-bounding ODE method (proved in Appendix B.4.4).

Proposition 10. Suppose that  $A$  is a stable matrix, and suppose that  $\widehat{A}$  satisfies

$$
\| \widehat {A} - A \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {1}{4} \min \left\{\frac {1}{\| \mathsf {d l y a p} [ A ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| A \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}, \| \mathsf {d l y a p} [ A ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\},
$$

Then,  $\widehat{A}^{\top}\mathsf{dlyap}[A]\widehat{A}\preceq (1 - \frac{1}{2}\| \mathsf{dlyap}[A]\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})\cdot \mathsf{dlyap}[A]$

By Lemma B.8, we have  $\| A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq \| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}$ . Since  $\| \mathsf{dlyap}[A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}]\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq \| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ , combining with Eq. B.1 gives

$$
\left\| A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star} - A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \widehat {\star}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} <   \frac {1}{5 \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3 / 2}} <   \frac {1}{4 \| \mathsf {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l} , \star} ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| A _ {\mathrm {c l} , \star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}.
$$

Similarly, we have  $\| A_{\mathrm{cl},\star} - A_{\mathrm{cl},\widehat{\star}}\|_{\mathrm{op}} < \frac{1}{\|\mathsf{dlyap}[A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}]\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1 / 2}}$ , which means that, in particular,  $A_{\mathrm{cl},\star},A_{\mathrm{cl},\widehat{\star}}$  satisfy the conditions for  $A,\widehat{A}$  in Proposition 10. This means that  $A_{\mathrm{cl},\widehat{\star}}^{\top}\mathsf{dlyap}[A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}]A_{\mathrm{cl},\widehat{\star}} \preceq (1 - \frac{1}{2}\|\mathsf{dlyap}[A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}]\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1}) \cdot \mathsf{dlyap}[A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}] (1 - \frac{1}{2}\|P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})$ . The last inequality follows from Part 1.  $\square$

# B.1.3 Continuity of the Safe Set

We show that the size of the so-called "safe" set is continuous in nearby instances. This allows us to use an instance  $(A_0, B_0)$  to guage whether the perturbed system  $(\hat{A}, \hat{B})$  is sufficiently close to  $(A_\star, B_\star)$  to ensure correctness of the perturbation bounds.

Theorem 11. Let  $(A_0, B_0)$  be a stabilizable system. Then, for any pair of systems  $(\widehat{A}, \widehat{B}), (A_\star, B_\star) \in \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{op}}\left(\frac{1}{3C_{\mathrm{safe}}(A_0, B_0)}, A_0, B_0\right)$  is stabilizable, and satisfies  $\max \{\|A_\star - \widehat{A}\|_{\mathrm{op}}, \| \widehat{B} - B_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}}\} \leq 1 / C_{\mathrm{safe}}(A_\star, B_\star)$ . Moreover,  $\|P_\infty(A_\star, B_\star)\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq 1.0835\|P_\infty(A_0, B_0)\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ .

Proof. Let  $\epsilon_0 := \max \{\|A_0 - \widehat{A}\|_{\mathrm{op}}, \|B_0 - B_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}}\} \leq 1 / C_{\mathrm{safe}}(A_0, B_0)$ . Applying Theorem 5 Part 1 with  $(\widehat{A}, \widehat{B}) \gets (A_\star, B_\star)$  and  $(A_\star, B_\star) \gets (A_0, B_0)$ , we have  $\|P_\infty(A_\star, B_\star)\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq 1.0835\|P_\infty(A_0, B_0)\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ . Hence,  $C_{\mathrm{safe}}(A_\star, B_\star) \leq 1.5C_{\mathrm{safe}}(A_0, B_0)$ . Hence  $(\widehat{A}, \widehat{B}), (A_\star, B_\star) \in \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{op}}\left(\frac{2}{3C_{\mathrm{safe}}(A_0, B_0)}, A_\star, B_\star\right) \subseteq \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{op}}\left(\frac{(2)\cdot(1.5)}{3C_{\mathrm{safe}}(A_\star, B_\star)}, A_\star, B_\star\right)$ , which means by triangle inequality that  $\max \{\|\widehat{A} - A_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}}, \|\widehat{B} - B_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}}\} \leq 1 / C_{\mathrm{safe}}(A_\star, B_\star)$ .

# B.1.4 Quality of First-Order Taylor Approximation

We bound the error of the first-order taylor expression in the following theorem.

Theorem 12. There exists universal constants  $c, p > 0$  such that the following holds. Let  $(A_{\star}, B_{\star})$  be stabilizable, and let  $\epsilon_{\circ} \coloneqq \max \{\| \widehat{A} - A_{\star} \|_{\circ}, \| \widehat{B} - B_{\star} \|_{\circ}\}$ , and suppose that  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{op}} \leq 1 / C_{\mathrm{safe}}(A_{\star}, B_{\star})$  and  $R_{\mathbf{x}} \succeq I$ ,  $R_{\mathbf{u}} = I$ . Let

$$
K ^ {\prime} := \frac {d}{d t} K _ {\infty} (A _ {\star} + t (A _ {\star} - \widehat {A}), B _ {\star} + t (B _ {\star} - \widehat {B})).
$$

Then,  $\| K_{\infty}(\widehat{A},\widehat{B}) - (K_{\star} + K^{\prime})\|_{\circ}\leq c\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{p}\epsilon_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\epsilon_{\circ}^{2}.$

Proof. Consider the curve  $K(t) = K_{\infty}(A(t), B(t))$  for  $A(t) = (1 - t)A_{\star} + t\widehat{A}$  and  $B(t) = (1 - t)B_{\star} + t\widehat{B}$ . By Theorem 5, the curve  $A(t), B(t)$  for  $t \in [0,1]$  consists of all stabilizable matrices with  $\| P_{\infty}(A(t), B(t)) \|_{\mathrm{op}} \lesssim \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}$ . By Lemma 3.1, the curve  $K(t)$  is analytic on  $[0,1]$ . Moreover, from Lemma B.3 below, we have  $\| K''(t) \|_{\circ} \leq c_0 \| P(t) \|_{\mathrm{op}}^p \epsilon_{\mathrm{op}}^2 \epsilon_{\circ}^2 \leq c \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}^p \epsilon_{\mathrm{op}}^2 \epsilon_{\circ}^2$  for universal constants  $c_0, p$ . The bound now follows by Taylor's theorem.

# B.2 Key Derivative Computations

In the following computations, let  $\Delta_A = \widehat{A} - A_\star$  and  $\Delta_A \coloneqq \widehat{B} - B_\star$ . We recall  $\epsilon_\circ \coloneqq \max \{\| \Delta_A \|_\circ, \| \Delta_B \|_\circ\}$ .

We consider derivatives allow curves  $(A(t), B(t)) = (A_{\star} + t\Delta_A, B_{\star} + t\Delta_B)$ , and associated functions  $P(t) \coloneqq P_{\infty}(A(t), B(t))$  and  $K_{\infty}(A(t), B(t))$  defined at stabilizes  $A(t), B(t)$ . All proofs are given in Section C.2.

We begin by recalling the derivative computation from the main text, which also establishes local smoothness of  $K(t)$  and  $P(t)$ .

Lemma 3.1 (Derivative and Smoothness of the DARE). Let  $(A(t), B(t))$  be an analytic curve, and define  $\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(t) \coloneqq A'(t) + B'(t)K_{\infty}(A(t), B(t))$ . Then for any  $t$  such that  $(A(t), B(t))$  is stabilizable, the functions  $P(u)$  and  $K(u)$  are analytic in a neighborhood around  $t$ , and we have

$$
P ^ {\prime} (u) = \mathsf {d l y a p} (A _ {\mathrm {c l}} (u), Q _ {1} (u)), \quad \text {w h e r e} Q _ {1} (u) := A _ {\mathrm {c l}} (u) ^ {\top} P (u) \Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}} (u) + \Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}} (u) ^ {\top} P (u) A _ {\mathrm {c l}} (u).
$$

Note that the above lemma allows for general analytic curves  $(A(t),B(t))$ . For our purposes, we restrict to linear curves given a above. For  $K^{\prime}$ , we have the following computation

Lemma B.1 (Computation of  $K'$ ). The first derivative of the optimal controller can be expressed as

$$
K ^ {\prime} = - \left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B\right) ^ {- 1} \left(\Delta_ {B} ^ {\top} P A _ {\mathrm {c l}} + B ^ {\top} P \left(\Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}}\right) + B ^ {\top} P ^ {\prime} A _ {\mathrm {c l}}\right). \tag {B.2}
$$

Of importance to our lower bound is the setting where the perturbations are of the form  $(\Delta_A, \Delta_B) := (\Delta K_\star, \Delta)$ . In this case, the expression for the derivative of  $K$  simplifies considerably we recall the following from the main text

Lemma 2.1 (Derivative Computation (Abeille and Lazaric [2018], Proposition 2)). Let  $(A_{\star},B_{\star})$  be stabilizable. Then

$$
\frac {d}{d t} K _ {\infty} (A _ {\star} - t \Delta K _ {\star}, B _ {\star} + t \Delta) \big | _ {t = 0} = - (R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B _ {\star} ^ {\top} P _ {\star} B _ {\star}) ^ {- 1} \cdot \Delta^ {\top} P _ {\star} A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star},
$$

where we recall  $A_{\mathrm{cl},\star} \coloneqq A_{\star} + B_{\star}K_{\star}$ .

Proof. Observe that for the perturbation in question,  $\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}(0) = \Delta K_{\star} - \Delta K(0) = \Delta K_{\star} - \Delta K_{\star} = 0$  By Lemma 3.1 and the fact that  $\mathsf{dlyap}(X,0) = 0$ , we have that  $P^{\prime}(0) = 0$ . Thus, the term  $B^{\top}P(\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}) + B^{\top}P^{\prime}A_{\mathrm{cl}}$  in Eq. (C.2) is 0 at  $t = 0$ . The result follows.

# B.2.1 Bounds on the Derivatives

Here, we state bounds on the various derivatives. Recall  $\epsilon_{\circ} := \max \{\| \Delta_A \|_{\circ}, \| \Delta_B \|_{\circ}\}$ . These bounds are established in Sections C.3.1 and C.3.2, respectively.

Lemma 3.2 (Bound on First Derivatives). Let  $(A(t), B(t))$  be an analytic curve. Then, for all  $t$  at which  $(A(t), B(t))$  is stabilizable, we have

$$
\| P ^ {\prime} (t) \| _ {\circ} \leq 4 \| P (t) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3} \epsilon_ {\circ}, \quad a n d \quad \| K ^ {\prime} (t) \| _ {\circ} \leq 7 \| P (t) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {7 / 2} \epsilon_ {\circ}.
$$

In fact, it will be more useful to prove the following related bound.

Lemma B.2.  $\| R_{\mathbf{u}}^{1 / 2}K^{\prime}\|_{\circ}\vee \| P^{1 / 2}BK^{\prime}\|_{\circ}\vee \| BK^{\prime}\|_{\circ}\leq 7\| P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{7 / 2}\epsilon_{\circ}$

For our lower bounds, we shall also use a second-order derivative bound

Lemma B.3 (Bound on  $K''$ ). If  $\epsilon_{\circ} = \max \{\| A_{\star} - \widehat{A}\|_{\circ}, \| B_{\star} - \widehat{B}\|_{\circ} \}$  and  $K(t) = K_{\infty}(A(t), B(t))$  for  $A(t) = (1 - t)A_{\star} + t\widehat{A}$  and  $B(t) = (1 - t)B_{\star} + t\widehat{B}$ , that at any  $t$  at which  $(A(t), B(t))$  is stabilizable,

$$
\left\| K ^ {\prime \prime} (t) \right\| _ {\circ} \leq \operatorname {p o l y} \left(\left\| P (t) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}\right) \epsilon_ {\mathrm {o p}} \epsilon_ {\circ}.
$$

# B.3 Main Control Theory Tools

# B.3.1 Properties of the dlyap Operator

We begin by describing relevant facts about dlyap operator. The first is standard (see e.g. [Bof et al., 2018, Boyd, 2008]), and gives a closed-form expression for the function.

Lemma B.4. Let  $Y = Y^{\top}$  and  $\rho(X) < 1$ . Then  $\mathcal{T}_X(Y) \coloneqq X^{\top}YX - Y$  is an invertible map from  $\mathbb{S}^{d_{\mathbf{x}}} \to \mathbb{S}^{d_{\mathbf{x}}}$ , and

$$
\mathrm {d l y a p} (X, Y) = \mathcal {T} _ {X} ^ {- 1} (Y) = \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(X ^ {\top}\right) ^ {k} Y X ^ {k}. \tag {B.3}
$$

Next, we show that dlyap is order-preserving in the following sense.

Lemma B.5 (Elementary dlyap bounds). The following bounds hold

1. If  $Y \preceq Z$  and  $A_{\mathrm{safe}}$  is stable, then  $\mathrm{dlyap}(A_{\mathrm{safe}}, X) \preceq \mathrm{dlyap}(A_{\mathrm{safe}}, Y)$ .  
2.  $Y\succeq 0$  and  $A_{\mathrm{safe}}$  is stable,  $\mathsf{dlyap}(A_{\mathrm{safe}},Y)\succeq Y$  
3. Suppose  $R_{\mathbf{x}} \succeq I$ , and let  $A + BK$  is stable. Then,

$$
\pm \mathrm {d l y a p} (A + B K, Y) \preceq \mathrm {d l y a p} (A + B K, I) \| Y \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \preceq \| Y \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot P _ {\infty} [ K; A, B ].
$$

4. When  $R_{\mathbf{x}} \succeq I$ ,  $\mathrm{dlyap}[A + BK] \preceq P_{\infty}[K; A, B]$ , and  $I \preceq \mathrm{dlyap}[A + BK_{\infty}(A, B)] \preceq P_{\infty}(A, B)$ .  
5. If  $A_{\mathrm{safe}}$  is stable,  $\| \mathsf{dlyap}[A_{\mathrm{safe}}] \|_{\mathrm{op}} = \| \mathsf{dlyap}[A_{\mathrm{safe}}^{\top}] \|_{\mathrm{op}}$ .

Next, we give a standard identity which relates the cost functions  $J$  to the dlyap operator.

Lemma B.6 (PSD bounds on  $P$ ). Let  $(A_{\star},B_{\star})$  be a stabilizable system, and let  $A_{\star} + B_{\star}K$  be stable. Set  $K_{\star} = K_{\infty}(A_{\star},B_{\star})$ . Then,

$$
P _ {\infty} [ K; A _ {\star}, B _ {\star} ] \succeq P _ {\infty} (A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}) = P _ {\infty} (K _ {\star}; A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}).
$$

Moreover, we have  $\mathcal{J}_{A_{\star},B_{\star}}^{\star}[K] = \mathrm{tr}(P_{\infty}[K;A,B])$ , and in particular,  $\mathcal{J}_{A_{\star},B_{\star}}^{\star} = \mathcal{J}_{A_{\star},B_{\star}}[K_{\star}] = \mathrm{tr}(P_{\infty}(A_{\star},B_{\star}))$ . As a consequence, if  $R_{\mathbf{x}} \succeq I$ , then  $\mathcal{J}_{A_{\star},B_{\star}}^{\star}[K] \geq \mathcal{J}_{A_{\star},B_{\star}}^{\star} \geq d_{\mathbf{x}}$  by Lemma B.5 part 4.

The following is a consequence of the above lemmas, and is useful for deriving interpretable corollaries of our main results.

Lemma B.7. Suppose that  $R_{\mathbf{x}} = I$ . If  $A_{\star}$  is  $(\gamma, \kappa)$ -strongly stable, then  $\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq \gamma_{\mathrm{sta}}^{-1}$  and  $\frac{1}{d_{\mathbf{x}}} \mathcal{J}_{A_{\star},B_{\star}}[0] \leq \gamma_{\mathrm{sta}}^{-1}$ . More generally, if  $(A_{\star} + B_{\star}K)$  is  $(\gamma, \kappa)$ -strongly stable, then  $\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq \gamma_{\mathrm{sta}}^{-1}(1 + \| K\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2})$ .

Proof of Lemma B.7. By considering the controller  $K = 0$ , Lemma B.6 implies  $P_{\star} \preceq \mathrm{dlyap}[A_{\star}, R_{\mathbf{x}}]$  and  $\mathcal{J}_{A_{\star}, B_{\star}}[0] = \operatorname{tr}(\mathrm{dlyap}[A_{\star}, R_{\mathbf{x}}]) \leq \frac{\|\mathrm{dlyap}[A_{\star}, R_{\mathbf{x}}]\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{d_{\mathbf{x}}}$ . If  $R_{\mathbf{x}} = I$ , and we can bound

$$
\| \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A _ {\star}, I ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \sum_ {t \geq 0} \| A ^ {t} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}.
$$

If there exists a transform  $T$  with  $\sigma_{\max}(T) / \sigma_{\min}(T) \leq \kappa$  such that  $\|TA_{\star}T^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq 1 - \gamma$ , then  $\|A^t\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq \kappa(1 - \gamma)$ . Hence,  $\|P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq \|\mathsf{dlyap}[A_{\star}, I]\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq \kappa^2 \sum_{t \geq 0} \gamma^2 \leq \frac{\kappa^2}{1-(1-\gamma)^2} \leq \frac{\kappa^2}{1-(1-\gamma)} = \kappa^2 \gamma^{-1}$ . More generally, we have that  $P_{\star} \preceq \mathsf{dlyap}[A_{\star} + B_{\star}K, R_{\mathbf{x}} + K^\top R_{\mathbf{u}}K]$  for  $R_{\mathbf{x}}, R_{\mathbf{u}} = I$ ,  $R_{\mathbf{x}} + K^\top R_{\mathbf{u}}K \preceq (1 + \|K\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2)I$ , and the bound follows by invoking Lemma B.5.

# B.3.2 Helpful Norm Bounds

Lemma B.8 (Helpful norm bounds). Let  $(A_{\star},B_{\star})$  be given, with  $P_{\star} = P_{\infty}(A_{\star},B_{\star})$ ,  $K_{\star} = K_{\infty}(A_{\star},B_{\star})$ , and  $A_{\mathrm{cl},\star} = A_{\star} + B_{\star}K_{\star}$ . If  $R_{\mathbf{x}} \succeq I$ ,  $R_{\mathbf{u}} = I$ , then the following bounds hold:

1.  $P_{\star} \succeq I$ , so that  $\| P_{\star}^{-1}\|_2 \leq 1$ , and  $\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}} \geq 1$ .  
2.  $\| K_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\leq \| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}$  and  $\| A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\leq \| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}$  
3. More generally, if  $(A_{\star} + B_{\star}K)$  is stable,  $K^{\top}K \preceq P_{\infty}(K; A_{\star}, B_{\star}) = \mathsf{dlyap}(A_{\star} + B_{\star}K, R_{\mathbf{x}} + K^{\top}R_{\mathbf{u}}K)$ .

# B.3.3 Bounds on  $P_{\infty}(K;A_{\star},B_{\star})$  and  $\mathcal{J}_{A_{\star},B_{\star}}[K]$

We now state a variant of a result due to Fazel et al. [2018], which bounds the effect of perturbations on  $P_{\infty}(K; A_{\star}, B_{\star}) - P_{\infty}(A_{\star}, B_{\star})$ .

Lemma B.9 (Generalization of Lemma 12 of Fazel et al. [2018], see also Eq 3.2 in Ran and Vreugdenhil [1988]). Let  $K$  be an arbitrary static controller which stabilizes  $A_{\star}, B_{\star}$ . Then,

$$
P _ {\infty} (K; A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}) - P _ {\infty} (A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}) = \mathsf {d l y a p} (A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} K, (K - K _ {\star}) ^ {\top} (R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B _ {\star} ^ {\top} P _ {\star} B _ {\star}) (K - K _ {\star})).
$$

As a consequence of Lemma B.9 and B.5, we have the following corollary.

Corollary 5. Let  $K$  be any arbitrary static controller which stabilities  $A_{\star}, B_{\star}$ , and suppose  $R_{\mathbf{u}} = I$ . Define the adjoint<sup>10</sup> as  $\Sigma_{A_{\star},B_{\star}}^{\mathrm{adj}}[K] \coloneqq \mathsf{d} \mathsf{lyap}(A_{\star} + B_{\star}K, I)$  covariance matrix. Then,

$$
\mathcal {J} _ {A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}} [ K ] - \mathcal {J} _ {A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}} \leq \| \Sigma_ {A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}} ^ {\mathrm {a d j}} [ K ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \max  \{\| R _ {\mathbf {u}} ^ {1 / 2} (K - K _ {\star}) \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}, \| P _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} B (K - K _ {\star}) \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \},
$$

$$
\| P _ {\infty} (K; A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}) - P _ {\infty} (A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \| \Sigma_ {A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}} ^ {\mathrm {a d j}} [ K ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \max  \{\| R _ {\mathbf {u}} ^ {1 / 2} (K - K _ {\star}) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}, \| P _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} B (K - K _ {\star}) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \}.
$$

# B.3.4 Linear Lyapunov Theory

We now state a classical result in Lyapunov theory (see, e.g. Boyd [2008]). Recall the notation  $\mathsf{dlyap}[A] \coloneqq \mathsf{dlyap}(A, I)$ .

Lemma B.10. For any  $x \in \mathbb{R}_{\mathbf{x}}^{d}$  and stable  $A$ , we have  $\mathrm{dlyap}[A] \succeq I$  and

$$
A ^ {\top} \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A ] A \preceq (1 - \| \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {- 1}) \cdot \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A ].
$$

Lemma B.11. For any stable  $A$ ,  $\| A \|_{\mathcal{H}_{\infty}} \leq 2 \| \mathrm{dlyap}[A] \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3/2}$ . More generally, suppose that  $P \succeq I$  is a matrix satisfying  $(Ax)^{\top} \mathrm{dlyap}[A](Ax) \leq (1 - \rho)x^{\top}Px$ . Then,

$$
\| A \| _ {\mathcal {H} _ {\infty}} \leq \sum_ {t \geq 0} \| A ^ {t} \| _ {2} \leq 2 \frac {\sqrt {\| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}}{\rho}.
$$

# B.4 Proofs for Supporting Perturbation Upper Bounds

# B.4.1 Proof of Proposition 6

The proof is analogous to that of Proposition 4, except we also apply the derivative bound on  $R_{\mathbf{u}}^{1/2}K'(t)BK'(t)$  from Lemma B.2. That bound also gives

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| R _ {\mathbf {u}} ^ {1 / 2} K ^ {\prime} (t) \right\| _ {\circ} \leq 7 \left\| P (t) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {7 / 2} \epsilon_ {\circ} \\ \| B _ {\star} K ^ {\prime} (t) \| _ {\circ} = \epsilon_ {\mathrm {o p}} \| K ^ {\prime} (t) \| _ {\circ} + \| B (t) K ^ {\prime} (t) \| _ {\circ} \leq (1 + \epsilon_ {\mathrm {o p}}) 7 \| P (t) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {7 / 2} \epsilon_ {\circ} \leq 8 \| P (t) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {7 / 2} \epsilon_ {\circ}, \\ \end{array}
$$

so that the desired bound follows by the mean value theorem.

Moreover, we have

$$
\| P _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} B _ {\star} K ^ {\prime} (t) \| _ {\circ} \leq \| P _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} P (t) ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| P B _ {\star} K ^ {\prime} (t) \| _ {\circ} \leq \| P _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} P (t) ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} 8 \| P (t) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {7 / 2} \epsilon_ {\circ},
$$

which translates to a bound of

$$
\| P _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} B (K _ {\infty} (\widehat {A}, \widehat {B}) - K _ {\star}) \| _ {\circ} \leq \max _ {t \in [ 0, 1 ]} \| P _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} P (t) ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq 7 \| P (t) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {7 / 2} \epsilon_ {\circ}.
$$

Finally, by the mean value theorem, we can bound for  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{op}} \leq 1/32 \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}$  and  $\alpha = 8 \epsilon_{\mathrm{op}} \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2} \leq 1/4$ ,

$$
\| P (t) - P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq 4 \max _ {t \in [ 0, 1 ]} \| P (t) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq 4 \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3} (1 - \alpha) ^ {- 3 / 2} \leq \frac {1}{8} (4 / 3) ^ {3 / 2}.
$$

Since  $P(t) \succeq I$ , then, this implies that for  $t \in [0,1]$ ,  $P(t) \succeq (1 - \frac{1}{8}(4/3)^{3/2})P_{\star}$ , yielding  $\| P_{\star}^{1/2}P(t)^{-1/2}\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq \sqrt{(1 - \frac{1}{8}(4/3)^{3/2}} \leq 9/8$ . Hence, for this  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{op}}$ , we have

$$
\| P _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} B _ {\star} (K _ {\infty} (\widehat {A}, \widehat {B}) - K _ {\star}) \| _ {\circ} \leq 9 \| P (t) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {7 / 2} \epsilon_ {\circ}.
$$

# B.4.2 Proof of Proposition 7

Since the final bound we derive does not depend on the control basis, we may assume without loss of generality that  $R_{\mathbf{u}} = I$ . Recall the steady state covariance matrix  $\Sigma_{A_{\star},B_{\star}}^{\mathrm{adj}}[K_{\star}] \coloneqq \mathsf{d}\mathsf{lyap}(A_{\star} + B_{\star}K,I)$ . We shall prove the following lemma.

Lemma B.12. Suppose that  $\|B_{\star}(K-K_{\star})\|_2 \leq 1/5\|\Sigma_{A_{\star},B_{\star}}^{\mathrm{adj}}[K_{\star}]\|\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3/2}$ , then  $\|\Sigma_{A_{\star},B_{\star}}^{\mathrm{adj}}[K]\| \leq 2\|\Sigma_{A_{\star},B_{\star}}^{\mathrm{adj}}[K_{\star}]\|$ .

Note that a similar result was given by Lemma 16 [Fazel et al., 2018]; we give our proof using the self-bounding ODE method to demonstrate the generality of its scope, and to avoid dependence on system matrices. Noting that  $\| \Sigma_{A_\star, B_\star}^{\mathrm{adj}}[K_\star] \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq \| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}$  as verified above, it is enough that  $\| B_\star (K - K_\star) \|_2 \leq 1/5 \| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}$  to ensure that  $\| \Sigma_{A_\star, B_\star}^{\mathrm{adj}}[K] \| \leq 2P_\star$ . When this holds, we have by Corollary 5, we have (assuming  $R_{\mathbf{u}} = I$ )

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {J} _ {A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}} [ K ] - \mathcal {J} _ {A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}} \leq \| \Sigma_ {A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}} ^ {\mathrm {a d j}} [ K ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \max  \{\| R _ {\mathbf {u}} ^ {1 / 2} (K - K _ {\star}) \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}, \| P _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} B _ {\star} (K - K _ {\star}) \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \} \\ \leq \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \max \{\| R _ {\mathbf {u}} ^ {1 / 2} (K - K _ {\star}) \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}, \| P _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} B _ {\star} (K - K _ {\star}) \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \}, \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\| P _ {\infty} (K; A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}) - P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \max \{\| R _ {\mathbf {u}} (K - K _ {\star}) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}, \| P _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} B _ {\star} (K - K _ {\star}) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \},
$$

as needed.

Proof of Lemma B.12. We shall now use the self-bounding machinery developed above to bound  $\Sigma_{A_\star, B_\star}^{\mathrm{adj}}[K]$ . Introduce the straight curve  $\widetilde{K}(t) \coloneqq K_\star + t\Delta_K$ , where  $\Delta_K = K - K_\star$ , and where the  $(\cdot)$  is to avoid confusion with the curve  $K(t) = K_\infty(A(t), B(t))$ . Let  $\Sigma(t) = \mathsf{dlyap}(A_\star + B_\star \widetilde{K}(t), I)$  so that  $\Sigma(0) = \Sigma_{A_\star, B_\star}^{\mathrm{adj}}[K_\star]$  and  $\Sigma(1) = \Sigma_{A_\star, B_\star}^{\mathrm{adj}}[K]$ .

By the definition of dlyap, we have that at all  $t$  for which  $K(t)$  stabilizes  $A_{\star}, B_{\star}$ ,

$$
\Sigma (t) = \big (A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} \widetilde {K} (t) \big) ^ {\top} \Sigma (t) \big (A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} \widetilde {K} (t) \big) + I.
$$

We shall now prove that  $\Sigma(t)$  satisfies a self-bounding relation analogous to Proposition 4.

Claim B.13. For all  $t \in [0,1]$  for which  $\Sigma(t)$  is defined,  $\| \Sigma'(t) \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq 2 \| \Sigma(t) \|^5 / 2 \| B_\star \Delta_K \|_{\mathrm{op}}$ .

Proof. Taking a derivative with respect to  $\Sigma$ , we have

$$
\Sigma^ {\prime} (t) = \left(A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} \widetilde {K} (t)\right) ^ {\top} \Sigma^ {\prime} (t) \left(A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} \widetilde {K} (t)\right) + Q _ {\Sigma} (t),
$$

where  $Q_{\Sigma}(t) = (B_{\star}\Delta_{K})^{\top}\Sigma(t)(A_{\star} + B_{\star}\widetilde{K}(t)) + (A_{\star} + B_{\star}\widetilde{K}(t))^{\top}\Sigma(t)B_{\star}\Delta_{K}$ . Thus, we can render

$$
\Sigma^ {\prime} (t) = \mathrm {d l y a p} \left(A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} \widetilde {K} (t), Q _ {\Sigma} (t)\right).
$$

By an argument analogous to Lemma B.5, we have  $\pm \Sigma'(t) \preceq \|Q_{\Sigma}(t)\| \mathsf{dlyap}(A_{\star} + B_{\star}\widetilde{K}(t), I) = \|Q_{\Sigma}(t)\| \Sigma(t)$ , yielding the self-bounding relation

$$
\left\| \Sigma^ {\prime} (t) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \left\| Q _ {\Sigma} (t) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \left\| \Sigma (t) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}.
$$

Moreover, we can bound for  $t \in [0,1]$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| Q _ {\Sigma} (t) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq 2 \| \Sigma (t) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| B _ {\star} \Delta_ {K} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} \widetilde {K} (t) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq 2 \| \Sigma (t) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3 / 2} \| B _ {\star} \Delta_ {K} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we use that  $\| A_{\star} + B_{\star}\widetilde{K}(t)\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2} \leq \| \mathsf{dlyap}(A_{\star} + B_{\star}\widetilde{K}(t),I)\|_{\mathrm{op}} = \| \Sigma(t)\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ .

We check explicitly that  $\Sigma(t)$  corresponds to the solution of a valid implicit function with domain  $\mathcal{U} := \{\Sigma^{\mathrm{adj}} : \Sigma^{\mathrm{adj}} > 0\}$  (using the more general second condition that ensures that  $t \mapsto \Sigma(t)$  is a continuously differentiable functor, which follows from the form of dlyap). Applying Corollary 3 with  $p = 5/2$  and  $c = 2\|B_{\star}\Delta_K\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ , this yields that if  $\alpha = (p - 1)c\|\Sigma(0)\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3/2} = 3\|\Sigma(0)\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3/2}\|B_{\star}\Delta_K\|_{\mathrm{op}} < 1$ , then,  $\| \Sigma(1)\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq (1 - u)^{-2/3}\|\Sigma(0)\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ . In particular, if  $\| B_{\star}\Delta_K\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq 1/5\|\Sigma(0)\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3/2}$ , then we can show  $\| \Sigma(1)\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq 2\|\Sigma(0)\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ .

# B.4.3 Proof of Proposition 9

Introduce the curve  $A(t) = A_{\mathrm{safe}} + t\Delta_A$ , where  $\Delta_A = A_1 - A_{\mathrm{safe}}$ , define  $Y_{z}(t)\coloneqq (zI - A(t))^{-1}$ . Then,  $\| A(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\infty}} = \sup_{z\in \mathbb{T}}\| Y_z(t)\| _2$ . Let us now use the self-bounding method to bound  $\| Y_{z}(t)\|$ . We can observe that

$$
Y _ {z} ^ {\prime} (t) = (z I - A (t)) ^ {- 1} \Delta_ {A} (z I - A (t)) ^ {- 1},
$$

so that  $\| Y_z'(t) \|_2 \leq \| Y_z(t) \|_2^2 \| \Delta_A \|$ . Since  $Y_z(t)$  corresponds to the zeros of the valid implicit function  $F$ ,  $z(A, Y) = Y \cdot (zI - A(t)) - I$ , Theorem 13 implies that, if  $\| \Delta_A \| \leq \frac{u}{\|A_{\text{safe}} \|_{\mathcal{H}_\infty}} = \min_{z \in \mathbb{T}} \frac{u}{\|Y_z(0)\|_2}$ , then we have  $\| Y_z(1) \| \leq \frac{1}{1 - \alpha} \| Y_z(0) \|$  for all  $z \in \mathbb{T}$ . Hence,

$$
\| A _ {1} \| _ {\mathcal {H} _ {\infty}} = \max  _ {z \in \mathbb {T}} \| Y _ {z} (1) \| \leq \max  _ {z \in \mathbb {T}} \| Y _ {z} (0) \| = \frac {1}{1 - \alpha} \| A _ {\text {s a f e}} \| _ {\mathcal {H} _ {\infty}},
$$

as needed.

![](images/07fbf66c25a20dc486b50382cb27f2f9eae01922dda7ebe660c3748eecbe5569.jpg)

# B.4.4 Proof of Proposition 10

Observe that we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} (\widehat {A} x) ^ {\top} \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A ] (\widehat {A} x \\ \leq (A x) ^ {\top} \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A ] (A x) + x ^ {\top} (\widehat {A} - A) ^ {\top} \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A ] A x + x ^ {\top} (\widehat {A} - A) ^ {\top} \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A ] (\widehat {A} - A) x \\ \leq \left(1 - \| d l y a p [ A ] \| _ {o p} ^ {- 1}\right) \cdot x ^ {\top} d l y a p [ A ] x + \| x \| _ {2} ^ {2} \left(\| \widehat {A} - A \| _ {o p} \| A \| _ {o p} + \| \widehat {A} - A \| _ {o p} ^ {2}\right) \| d l y a p [ A ] \| _ {o p}) \\ \leq \left(1 - \| d l y a p [ A ] \| _ {o p} ^ {- 1} + \left(\| \widehat {A} - A \| _ {o p} \| A \| _ {o p} + \| \widehat {A} - A \| _ {o p} ^ {2}\right) \| d l y a p [ A ] \| _ {o p})\right) \cdot x ^ {\top} d l y a p [ A ] x, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used that  $\mathsf{dlyap}[A] \succeq I$ . In particular, if

$$
\| \widehat {A} - A \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {1}{4} \min  \left\{\frac {1}{\| A \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}, \| \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\},
$$

then, the above is at most,  $(1 - \frac{1}{2}\| \mathrm{dlyap}[A]\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1})\cdot x^{\top}\mathrm{dlyap}[A]x$

# C Supporting Proofs for Appendix B

# C.1 Proofs for Main Technical Tools (Section B.3)

We begin with the following lemma, which follows from a standard computation.

# C.1.1 Proof of Lemma B.5

Proof. Let  $\rho(A_0) < 1$ , and so from (B.3) we have that for any  $Z$  with  $Y \preceq Z$  that

$$
\mathsf {d l y a p} (A _ {0}, Y) = \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {\infty} (A _ {0} ^ {k}) ^ {\top} Y A _ {0} ^ {k} \preceq \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {\infty} (A _ {0} ^ {k}) ^ {\top} Z (A _ {0} ^ {k}).
$$

Second, if  $Y \succeq 0$ ,  $\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(A_0^k)^\top Y(A_0^k)\mathrm{d}\mathsf{lyap}(A_0,Y) \succeq Y$ .

The third statement is a direct consequence of the first. Moreover, since  $I \preceq R_{\mathbf{x}} \preceq R_{\mathbf{x}} + K^{\top}R_{\mathbf{u}}K$ , taking  $Z = \| Y\| (R_{\mathbf{x}} + K^{\top}R_{\mathbf{u}}K)$  yields the fourth inequality.

For the last statement, let  $\| x\| _2 = 1$  . Then, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} x ^ {\top} \mathsf {d l y a p} [ A _ {0} ] x = \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {\infty} x ^ {\top} (A _ {0} ^ {\top}) ^ {k} (A _ {0} ^ {k}) x = \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {\infty} \operatorname {t r} (A _ {0} ^ {k} x x ^ {\top} (A _ {0} ^ {k}) ^ {\top}) \\ = \mathrm {d l y a p} \left(A _ {0}, x x ^ {\top}\right) \\ \preceq \| x x ^ {\top} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| \mathrm {d l y a p} (A + B K, I) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} I = \| \mathrm {d l y a p} (A + B K, I) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} I. \\ \end{array}
$$

# C.1.2 Proof of Lemma B.6

We begin with the following lemma, whose proof is a straightforward computation.

Lemma C.1. Let  $A_{\star}, B_{\star}$  be stabilizable. For a controller  $K$  such that  $A_{\star} + B_{\star}K$  is stable, we define the value function

$$
V ^ {K} (x) := \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {\infty} c \left(x _ {t} ^ {K, x}, K x _ {t} ^ {K, x}\right), \quad \text {w h e r e} x _ {0} ^ {K, x} = x, \quad \text {a n d} x _ {t} ^ {K, x} = \left(A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} K\right) x _ {t - 1} ^ {K, x}.
$$

We then have  $x_{t}^{K,x} = (A_{\star} + B_{\star}K)^{t}x$ ,  $\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}(x_{t}^{K,x})^{\top}Yx_{t}^{K,x} = \mathsf{dlyap}(A_{\star} + B_{\star}K,Y)$ , and in particular

$$
V ^ {K} (x) = x ^ {\top} \mathrm {d l y a p} \left(A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} K, R _ {\mathbf {x}} + K ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} K\right) x = x ^ {\top} P _ {\infty} (K) x,
$$

We now prove Lemma B.6.

Proof. Introduce the shorthand  $P_{\star} = P_{\infty}(A_{\star}, B_{\star})$ ,  $P_{\infty}(K) = P_{\infty}(K; A_{\star}, B_{\star})$ , and in particular  $V^{K_{\infty}}(x) = x^{\top}P_{\infty}(x)$ . It is well known that  $x^{\top}P_{\infty}x = V^{K_{\infty}}(x)$  and that  $V^{K_{\infty}}(x) = \inf_K V^K (x)\leq V^K (x)$  [Bertsekas, 2005]. Hence,  $P_{\infty}(K)\succeq P_{\infty}$ . Finally, observe that by using that  $A + BK$  is stable, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {J} _ {A, B} [ L ] = \lim _ {t \to \infty} \frac {1}{t} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \mathbb {E} _ {A, B, K} [ \mathbf {x} _ {i} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {x}} \mathbf {x} _ {i} + \mathbf {u} _ {i} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} \mathbf {u} _ {i} ] \\ = \lim  _ {t \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb {E} _ {A, B, K} \left[ \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {x}} \mathbf {x} _ {t} + \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} \mathbf {u} _ {t} \right] \\ = \operatorname {t r} \left(\sum_ {s = 0} ^ {\infty} \left((A + B K) ^ {\top}\right) ^ {s} \left(R _ {\mathbf {x}} + K ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} K\right) (A + B K) ^ {s}\right), \\ = \operatorname {t r} \left(P _ {\infty} (K)\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

The identity for  $P_{\infty}$  is the special case where  $K = K_{\infty}$ .

# C.1.3 Proof of Lemma B.8

Proof. We address each bound in succession.

1.  $\sigma_{\mathrm{min}}(P_\star) \geq 1$  by Lemma B.5.  
2. We have that

$$
P _ {\infty} = \mathrm {d l y a p} \left(A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} K _ {\star}, R _ {\mathbf {x}} + K _ {\star} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} K _ {\star}\right) \succeq R _ {\mathbf {x}} + K _ {\star} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} K _ {\star} \succeq K _ {\star} ^ {\top} K _ {\star},
$$

since  $R_{\mathbf{u}} \succeq I$  and  $R_{\mathbf{x}} \succeq I$ . Moreover, we have that

$$
\begin{array}{l} P _ {\infty} = \mathrm {d l y a p} \left(A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} K _ {\star} R _ {\mathbf {x}} + K _ {\star} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} K _ {\star}\right) = \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t} \left(\left(A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} K _ {\star}\right) ^ {\top}\right) ^ {t} \left(R _ {\mathbf {x}} + K _ {\star} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} K _ {\star}\right) \left(A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} K _ {\star}\right) ^ {t} \\ \succeq \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {t} \left(\left(A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} K _ {\star}\right) ^ {\top}\right) ^ {t} \left(A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} K _ {\star}\right) ^ {t} \\ \succeq \left(A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} K _ {\star}\right) ^ {\top} \left(A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} K _ {\star}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

□

# C.1.4 Proof of Lemma B.9

Proof. The first inequality is precisely Lemmas 12 in Fazel et al. [2018]. In light of Lemma C.1, it suffices to show that

$$
V ^ {K} (x) - V ^ {K _ {\star}} (x) = x ^ {\top} \mathsf {d l y a p} \left(A _ {\star} + B K _ {\star}, \left(K - K _ {\star}\right) ^ {\top} \left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B _ {\star} ^ {\top} P _ {\star} B _ {\star}\right) \left(K - K _ {\star}\right)\right)
$$

Lemma 10 in Fazel et al. [2018] implies (noting  $E_{K_{\star}} = 0$  for  $E_K$  defined therein) that

$$
\begin{array}{l} V ^ {K} (x) - V ^ {K _ {\star}} (x) = \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {\top} (x _ {t} ^ {K, x}) ^ {\top} (K - K _ {\star}) ^ {\top} (R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B _ {\star} ^ {\top} P _ {\star} B _ {\star}) (K - K _ {\star}) x _ {t} ^ {K, x} \\ = \mathsf {d l y a p} (A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} K, (K - K _ {\star}) ^ {\top} (R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B _ {\star} ^ {\top} P _ {\star} B _ {\star}) (K - K _ {\star})), \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second inequality uses Lemmas 12 in Fazel et al. [2018].

# C.1.5 Proof of Lemma B.11

Let us prove the more general claim.

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| A \| _ {\mathcal {H} _ {\infty}} \leq \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {\infty} \| A ^ {i} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {\infty} \sqrt {\| (A ^ {i}) ^ {\top} (A ^ {i}) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}} \\ \leq \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {\infty} \sqrt {\frac {1}{\sigma_ {\mathrm {m i n}} (\mathsf {d l y a p} [ A ])} \| (A ^ {i}) ^ {\top} P (A ^ {i}) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}} \\ \leq \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {\infty} \frac {1}{\sigma_ {\mathrm {m i n}} (P)} \sqrt {(1 - \rho) ^ {i} \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}} \\ \leq \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 / 2} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {\infty} \sqrt {1 - \rho} ^ {i} \quad \text {s i n c e} P \succeq I \\ = \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 / 2} \frac {1}{1 - \sqrt {1 - \rho}} \\ \leq \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 / 2} \frac {1 + \sqrt {1 - \rho}}{1 - (1 - \rho} \\ \leq 2 \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 / 2} / \rho . \\ \end{array}
$$

# C.2 Derivative Computations

# C.2.1 Proof of Lemma 3.1

Recall the function

$$
\mathcal {F} _ {\mathrm {D A R E}} ([ A, B ], P) = A ^ {\top} P A - P - A ^ {\top} P B (R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B) ^ {- 1} B ^ {\top} P A + R _ {\mathbf {x}}.
$$

Let us compute the differentiable of this map. To keep notation, let us suppress the dependence of the  $A, B$  arguments on  $t$ . We have that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left. \mathsf {D F} _ {\mathsf {D A R E}} [ \mathrm {d} P, \mathrm {d} t ] \right| _ {A (t), B (t), P} = \mathsf {D} (A ^ {\top} P A) - \mathsf {D} P + \mathsf {D} (A ^ {\top} P B) \cdot (R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B) ^ {- 1} B ^ {\top} P A \\ - \left(A ^ {\top} P B\right) \left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B\right) ^ {- 1} \cdot \left(B ^ {\top} P A\right) D \\ - \left(A ^ {\top} P B\right) \cdot D \left(\left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B\right) ^ {- 1}\right) \cdot B ^ {\top} P A \\ = \mathrm {D} (A ^ {\top} P A) + \mathrm {D} (A ^ {\top} P B) \cdot K + K ^ {\top} \cdot \mathrm {D} (B ^ {\top} P A) \\ - \left(A ^ {\top} P B\right) \cdot \mathrm {D} \left(\left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B\right) ^ {- 1}\right) \cdot B ^ {\top} P A, \\ \end{array}
$$

where for compactness, we substituted in the formula

$$
K = K (t, P) = - \left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B (t) ^ {\top} P B (t)\right) ^ {- 1} B (t) ^ {\top} P A (t). \tag {C.1}
$$

Recall that for a symmetric matrix, we have  $((X^{-1})' = -X^{-1}X'X^{-1})$ . Thus, substituting in the definition of  $K$ , we can write the last term in the expression above as

$$
\begin{array}{l} - \left(A ^ {\top} P B\right) \cdot D \left(\left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B\right)\right) ^ {\prime} B ^ {\top} P A \\ = (A ^ {\top} P B) (R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B) ^ {- 1} (R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B) \mathsf {D} (R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B) ^ {- 1} B ^ {\top} P A \\ = K ^ {\top} \mathrm {D} \left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B\right) K. \\ \end{array}
$$

Hence, gathering terms, we have

$$
\left. \mathsf {D} \mathcal {F} _ {\mathsf {D A R E}} [ \mathrm {d} P, \mathrm {d} t ] \right| _ {A (t), B (t), P} = \mathsf {D} (A ^ {\top} P A) - \mathsf {D} (P) + \mathsf {D} (A ^ {\top} P B) K + K ^ {\top} \cdot \mathsf {D} (B ^ {\top} P A) + K ^ {\top} \mathsf {D} (R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B) K.
$$

Let us now adopt shorthand  $(\cdot)' := \frac{d}{dt}(\cdot)$ . Expanding the derivatives using the product rule, we then have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left. \mathsf {D} \mathcal {F} _ {\mathsf {D A R E}} [ \mathrm {d} P, \mathrm {d} t ] \right| _ {A (t), B (t), P} = A ^ {\top} \mathsf {D} P A - \mathsf {D} (P) + A ^ {\top} \mathsf {D} B K + K ^ {\top} B ^ {\top} \mathsf {D} A + K ^ {\top} B ^ {\top} \cdot \mathsf {D} P \cdot B K \\ + A ^ {\prime \top} P A + A ^ {\top} P A ^ {\prime} + A ^ {\prime \top} B K + (B K) ^ {\top} P A ^ {\prime} \\ + A ^ {\top} P (B ^ {\prime} K) + (B ^ {\prime} K) ^ {\top} P A + (B ^ {\prime} K) ^ {\top} P B K + (B K) ^ {\top} P (B ^ {\prime} K). \\ \end{array}
$$

Grouping terms, this is equal to

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left. \mathsf {D F} _ {\mathsf {D A R E}} [ \mathrm {d} P, \mathrm {d} t ] \right| _ {A (t), B (t), P} = (A + B K) ^ {\top} \mathrm {d} P (A + B K) - \mathrm {d} P \big | _ {A (t), B (t), P} \\ + A ^ {\prime \top} P (A + B K) + (A + B K) ^ {\top} P A ^ {\prime} \big | _ {A (t), B (t), P} \\ + \left(B ^ {\prime} K\right) ^ {\prime \top} P (A + B K) + \left(A + B K\right) ^ {\top} P \left(B ^ {\prime} K\right) \big | _ {A (t), B (t), P} \\ = (A + B K) ^ {\top} \cdot \mathrm {d} P \cdot (A + B K) - \mathrm {d} P \big | _ {A (t), B (t), P} \\ + \underbrace {\left(A ^ {\prime} (t) + B ^ {\prime} (t) K\right) ^ {\top} P (A (t) + B (t) K) + \left(A (t) + B (t) K\right) P \left(A ^ {\prime} (t) + B ^ {\prime} (t) K\right)} _ {:= Q _ {1} (t, P), \text {a n d} K = (t, P) \text {a s i n E q . (C . 1)}} \\ = \mathcal {T} _ {A (t) + B (t) K} [ \mathrm {d} P ] + Q (t, P) \mathrm {d} t. \\ \end{array}
$$

In particular, if  $\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{DARE}}([A(t),B(t)],P) = 0$ , then for  $K(t,p)$  as in Eq. (C.1), the matrix  $A(t) + B(t)K(t,P)$  is stable. Hence,  $\mathcal{T}_{A(t) + B(t)K(t,P)}[\cdot ]$  is invertible on  $\mathbb{S}^d$ . Moreover, since the second term

has no-explicit depending on  $\mathrm{d}P$ , we find that  $(\mathrm{d}P,\mathrm{d}t)\mapsto \mathsf{D}\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{DARE}}[\mathrm{d}P,\mathrm{d}t]\big|_{A(t),B(t),P}$  is full-rank, with zero solution

$$
\mathrm {d} P = \mathcal {T} _ {A (t) + B (t) K (t, P)} ^ {- 1} [ Q _ {1} (t, P) \mathrm {d} t ] = \mathsf {d l y a p} (A (t) + B (t) K (t, P), Q _ {1} (t, P)).
$$

By the implicit function theorem, this implies that there is a neighborhood of  $u \mapsto P(u)$  on which the function  $u \mapsto P(u)$  is analytic (recall  $\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{DARE}}$  is analytic), and  $\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{DARE}}([A(u), B(u)], P(u)) = 0$  on this neighborhood. By the above display then, we have  $P'(u) = \mathrm{dlyap}(A(u) + B(u)K(u), Q_1(u))$ , where  $Q_1(u) \gets Q_1(u, P(u))$  and  $K(t) \gets K(u, P(u))$  are specializations of the above to the curve  $u \mapsto P(u)$ .

# C.2.2 Computation of  $K'$  (Lemma B.1)

Throughout, we suppress dependence on  $t$ , and the computations are understood to hold only at those  $t$  for which  $(A(t), B(t))$  is stabilizable.

Proof. Note that we can take derivatives freely by Lemma 3.1. Invoking the product rule and the identity  $((X^{-1})' = -X^{-1}X'X^{-1})$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} K ^ {\prime} = \left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B\right) ^ {- 1} \cdot \left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B\right) ^ {\prime} \cdot \left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B\right) ^ {- 1} B ^ {\top} P A - \left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B\right) ^ {- 1} \cdot \left(B ^ {\top} P A\right) ^ {\prime} \\ = - \left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B\right) ^ {- 1} \left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B\right) ^ {\prime} \cdot K - \left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B\right) ^ {- 1} \left(B ^ {\top} P A\right) ^ {\prime} \\ = - \left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B\right) ^ {- 1} \left(\left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B\right) ^ {\prime} K + \left(B ^ {\top} P A\right) ^ {\prime}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

We simplify the expression inside the parentheses as

$$
\begin{array}{l} (R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B) ^ {\prime} K + (B ^ {\top} P A) ^ {\prime} = B ^ {\prime \top} P (A + B K) + B ^ {\top} P (A ^ {\prime} + B ^ {\prime} K) + B ^ {\top} P ^ {\prime} (A + B K) \\ = B ^ {\prime \top} P A _ {\mathrm {c l}} + B ^ {\top} P \left(\Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}}\right) + B ^ {\top} P ^ {\prime} A _ {\mathrm {c l}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Since  $B^{\prime} = \Delta_{B}$ , this yields the result.

# C.2.3 Computation of  $P''$

Again, suppress dependence on  $t$ . We compute  $P''$ , which Lemma 3.1 ensures exists whenever  $(A(t), B(t))$  is stabilizable.

Lemma C.2 (Computation of  $P''$ ). The second derivative of the optimal cost matrix has the form

$$
P ^ {\prime \prime} = \mathrm {d l y a p} \left(A _ {\mathrm {c l}}, Q _ {2}\right),
$$

where  $Q_{2} \coloneqq A_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\prime \top}P^{\prime}A_{\mathrm{cl}} + A_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\top}P^{\prime}A_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\prime} + Q_{1}^{\prime}$  is a symmetric matrix defined in terms of

$$
Q _ {1} ^ {\prime} := A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\prime \top} P (\Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}}) + A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\top} P ^ {\prime} \Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}} + A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\top} P (B ^ {\prime} K ^ {\prime}) + (B ^ {\prime} K ^ {\prime}) ^ {\top} P A _ {\mathrm {c l}} + \Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}} ^ {\top} P ^ {\prime} A _ {\mathrm {c l}} + \Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}} ^ {\top} P A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\prime}.
$$

Proof. Applying the product rule to the expression for  $P'$  from Lemma 3.1, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} P ^ {\prime \prime} = A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\top} P ^ {\prime \prime} A _ {\mathrm {c l}} + A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\prime \top} P ^ {\prime} A _ {\mathrm {c l}} + A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\top} P ^ {\prime} A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\top} \\ + A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\prime \top} P \Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}} + A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\top} P ^ {\prime} \Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}} + A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\top} P \left(\Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}}\right) ^ {\prime} + \left(\Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}}\right) ^ {\prime \top} P A _ {\mathrm {c l}} + \Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}} ^ {\top} P ^ {\prime} A _ {\mathrm {c l}} + \Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}} ^ {\top} P A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\prime} \\ = \mathrm {d l y a p} \left(A _ {\mathrm {c l}}, Q _ {2}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $Q_{2}:= A_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\prime \top}P^{\prime}A_{\mathrm{cl}} + A_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\top}P^{\prime}A_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\prime} + A_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\prime \top}P\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}} + A_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\top}P^{\prime}\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}} + A_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\top}P(\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}})^{\prime} + (\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}})^{\prime \top}PA_{\mathrm{cl}} + \Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\top}P^{\prime}A_{\mathrm{cl}} + \Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\top}PA_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\prime}$ . We conclude by observing that  $(\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}})' = (A'' + B''K + B'K') = B'K'$ , since  $A$  and  $B$  are linear in  $t$ .

# C.3 Norm Bounds for Derivatives

# C.3.1 Norm bounds for First Derivatives

In this section, we work through obtaining concrete bounds on the derivatives of  $P(t), K(t)$  using the expressions derived in the previous section. As above, we assume that  $R_{\mathbf{u}} \succeq I$  and  $R_{\mathbf{x}} \succeq I$ . We state some more bounds that will be of use to use.

Lemma C.3 (Norm-Bounds for Derivative Quantities). Let  $(A_{\star},B_{\star})$  be given, with  $P_{\star} = P_{\infty}(A_{\star},B_{\star})$ ,  $K_{\star} = K_{\infty}(A_{\star},B_{\star})$ , and  $A_{\mathrm{cl},\star} = A_{\star} + B_{\star}K_{\star}$ . If  $R_{\mathbf{u}},R_{\mathbf{x}}\succeq I$ , then the following bounds hold:

1. Let  $R_0 \coloneqq R_{\mathbf{u}} + B_\star^\top P_\star B_\star$ . Then for any  $X, Y \in \{B_\star, P_\star^{1/2}B_\star, R_{\mathbf{u}}^{1/2}, I\}$ ,  $\| XR_0^{-1}Y^\top \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq 1$ .  
2. For  $\circ \in \{\mathrm{op},\mathrm{F}\}$ , we have  $\| \Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\|_{\circ} \leq 2\| P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1 / 2}\epsilon_{\circ}$ .

Proof. First, we have that  $\| X R_0^{-1}Y^\top \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq \| X R_0^{-1/2} \|_{\mathrm{op}} \| Y R_0^{-1/2} \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq \sqrt{\| X R_0^{-1}X^\top \|_{\mathrm{op}} \| Y R_0^{-1}Y^\top \|_{\mathrm{op}}}$ . Since  $R_{\mathbf{u}}, P \succeq I$ , we can verify that  $XX^\top, YY^\top \preceq R_0$ , which means that  $\| X R_0^{-1}X^\top \|_{\mathrm{op}}, \| Y R_0^{-1}Y^\top \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq 1$ .

Second, for  $\| \cdot \|_{\circ}$  denoting either the operator or Frobenius norm, we bound  $\| \Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\|_{\circ} = \| \Delta_A + \Delta_B K\|_{\circ}\leq \| \Delta_A\|_{\circ} + \| \Delta_B\|_{\circ}\| K\|_{\mathrm{op}} = \epsilon_{\circ}(1 + \| K\|_{\mathrm{op}})\leq 2\sqrt{\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}}\epsilon_{\circ}$ .

# C.3.2 Proof of Lemma 3.2 and Lemma B.2

Recall that  $P' = \mathsf{dlyap}(A_{\mathrm{cl}}, Q_1)$ , where  $Q_1 \coloneqq A_{\mathrm{cl}}^\top P(\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}) + (\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}})^\top PA_{\mathrm{cl}}$ . Hence, using Lemma B.5 with  $R_{\mathbf{x}} \succeq I$ , followed by Lemmas B.8 and C.3, we can bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| P ^ {\prime} \| _ {\circ} = \left\| d l y a p \left(A _ {c l}, Q _ {1}\right) \right\| _ {\circ} \\ \leq \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| Q _ {1} \| _ {\circ} \leq 2 \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \| A _ {\mathrm {c l}} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| \Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}} \| _ {\circ} \\ \leq 2 \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \cdot \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 / 2} \cdot 2 \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 / 2} \epsilon_ {\circ} = 4 \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Next, recall from Lemma B.1 that we have the identity

$$
K ^ {\prime} = - R _ {0} ^ {- 1} \left(\Delta_ {B} ^ {\top} P A _ {\mathrm {c l}} + B ^ {\top} P \left(\Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}}\right) + B ^ {\top} P ^ {\prime} A _ {\mathrm {c l}}\right),
$$

where  $R_0 \coloneqq R_{\mathbf{u}} + B^\top PB$ . Next bound each of the three terms that arise. Again using  $\| R_0^{-1} \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq 1$  and  $\| A_{\mathrm{cl}} \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq \| P \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}$  (Lemma B.8), we have

$$
\left\| R _ {0} ^ {- 1} \Delta_ {B} P A _ {\mathrm {c l}} \right\| _ {\circ} \leq \left\| P \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3 / 2} \epsilon_ {\circ}.
$$

Next, since  $\| R_0^{-1}B^\top P^{1 / 2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1$  (Lemma C.3), we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B\right) ^ {- 1} \left(B ^ {\top} P \left(\Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}}\right) + B ^ {\top} P ^ {\prime} A _ {\mathrm {c l}}\right) \| _ {\circ} \leq \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 / 2} \| \Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \| P ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| P ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| A _ {\mathrm {c l}} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq 2 \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \epsilon_ {\circ} + \| P ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| P ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 / 2} \\ \leq 2 \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \epsilon_ {\circ} + 4 \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {7 / 2} \epsilon_ {\circ}. \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second to last line uses Lemma B.8, and the last line uses  $\| P^{-1/2}\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq 1$ , as well as  $\| P' \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq 4 \| P \|_{\mathrm{op}}^3$ . Putting the bounds together, we have  $\| K' \|_{\circ} \leq 7 \| P \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{7/2} \epsilon_{\circ}$ .

□.

We also restate and prove an analogous bound that pre-conditions  $K'(t)$  by appropriate matrices.

Lemma B.2.  $\| R_{\mathbf{u}}^{1 / 2}K^{\prime}\|_{\circ}\vee \| P^{1 / 2}BK^{\prime}\|_{\circ}\vee \| BK^{\prime}\|_{\circ}\leq 7\| P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{7 / 2}\epsilon_{\circ}$

Proof. The bound is analogous to the bound on  $K'$  from Lemma 3.2, but now uses right multiplication of  $R_0^{-1}$  which addresses left-multiplication by  $B, P^{1/2}B, R_{\mathbf{u}}^{1/2}$ .

# C.3.3 Norm Bounds for Second Derivatives

Next, we turn to bounding  $P''$  and  $K''$ . We shall need some intermediate lemmas. Let us bound the intermediate term  $A_{\mathrm{cl}}'$

Lemma C.4. It holds that  $\max \{\| \Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}\|_{\circ},\| A_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\prime}\|_{\circ}\} \leq 9\| P\| _2^{7 / 2}\epsilon_{\circ}$ , and  $\| \Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\prime}\|_{\circ}\leq \epsilon_{\circ}\epsilon_{\mathrm{op}}\| P\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{7 / 2}$ .

Proof.  $A_{\mathrm{cl}}' = \Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}} + BK'$ . From Lemma C.3,  $\| \Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}} \|_{\circ} \leq 2 \sqrt{\|P\|_{\mathrm{op}}} \epsilon_{\circ}$ . Moreover, from Lemma B.2,  $\| BK' \|_{\circ} \leq 7 \| P \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{7/2} \epsilon_{\circ}$ . Thus,  $\| A_{\mathrm{cl}}' \|_{\circ} \leq 9 \| P \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{7/2} \epsilon_{\circ}$ . The second bound uses  $\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}^{\prime} = \Delta_B K'$ , and the same bound on  $\| K' \|_{\circ}$ .

Next, we bound the norm of  $P''$ .

Lemma C.5. We have the bound  $\| P''\|_{\circ}\leq \mathrm{poly}(\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}})\epsilon_{\mathrm{op}}\epsilon_{\circ}$

Proof. Recall that  $P'' = \mathsf{dlyap}(A_{\mathrm{cl}}, Q_2)$ , where

$$
\begin{array}{l} Q _ {2} = A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\prime \top} P ^ {\prime} A _ {\mathrm {c l}} + A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\top} P ^ {\prime} A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\prime} \\ + A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\prime \top} P \left(\Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}}\right) + A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\top} P ^ {\prime} \Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}} + A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\top} P \left(B ^ {\prime} K ^ {\prime}\right) + \left(B ^ {\prime} K ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {\top} P A _ {\mathrm {c l}} + \Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}} ^ {\top} P ^ {\prime} A _ {\mathrm {c l}} + \Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}} ^ {\top} P A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\prime}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Hence,  $\| P''\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq \| P\|_{\mathrm{op}}\| Q_2\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ . We upper bound the norm of  $Q_{2}$  by

$$
\| Q _ {2} \| _ {\circ} \leq 2 \left(\| A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\circ} \| P ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| A _ {\mathrm {c l}} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \| A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\circ} \| \Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \| B ^ {\prime} \| _ {\circ} \| K ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| P A _ {\mathrm {c l}} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \| A _ {\mathrm {c l}} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| P ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| \Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}} \| _ {\circ}\right).
$$

Using Lemma B.8 and Lemma 3.2, one can show that

$$
\left\| P ^ {\prime \prime} \right\| _ {\circ} \leq \operatorname {p o l y} \left(\left\| P _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}\right) \epsilon_ {\mathrm {o p}} \epsilon_ {\circ}.
$$

Proof of Lemma B.3. From Lemma B.1, we have that

$$
K ^ {\prime} = - \left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B\right) ^ {- 1} \left(\Delta_ {B} ^ {\top} P A _ {\mathrm {c l}} + B ^ {\top} P \left(\Delta_ {A _ {\mathrm {c l}}}\right) + B ^ {\top} P ^ {\prime} A _ {\mathrm {c l}}\right). \tag {C.2}
$$

Denote  $Q_{3} \coloneqq \Delta_{B}^{\top}PA_{\mathrm{cl}} + B^{\top}P(\Delta_{A_{\mathrm{cl}}}) + B^{\top}P'A_{\mathrm{cl}}$ , and  $R_0 \coloneqq R_{\mathbf{u}} + B^{\top}PB$ . Then, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} K ^ {\prime \prime} = R _ {0} ^ {- 1} Q _ {3} ^ {\prime} (t) + R _ {0} ^ {- 1} \left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B\right) ^ {\prime} R _ {0} ^ {- 1} Q _ {3} (t) \\ = R _ {0} ^ {- 1} Q _ {3} ^ {\prime} (t) + R _ {0} ^ {- 1} \left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B\right) ^ {\prime} K ^ {\prime}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Lets first handle the term  $R_0^{-1}Q_3'(t)$ . From Lemma C.3, we have that  $\| R_0^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1,\| R_0^{-1}B\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ . Thus,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| R _ {0} ^ {- 1} Q _ {3} ^ {\prime} (t) \| _ {\circ} \leq \| R _ {0} ^ {- 1} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| \Delta_ {B} ^ {\top} P A _ {\mathrm {c l}}) ^ {\prime} \| _ {\circ} + \| R _ {0} ^ {- 1} B \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| \left(P ^ {\prime} A _ {\mathrm {c l}}) ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {\circ} \\ \leq \left\| \left(\Delta_ {B} ^ {\top} P A _ {\mathrm {c l}}\right) ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {\circ} + \left\| \left(P ^ {\prime} A _ {\mathrm {c l}}\right) ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {\circ} \\ \leq \| \Delta_ {B} \| _ {\circ} \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\prime} \| P ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| A _ {\mathrm {c l}} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \| P ^ {\prime \prime} \| _ {\circ} \| A _ {\mathrm {c l}} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \| P ^ {\prime} \| _ {\circ} \| A _ {\mathrm {c l}} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq \operatorname {p o l y} \left(\left\| P _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}\right) \epsilon_ {\mathrm {o p}} \epsilon_ {\circ}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we invoke the derivative computations above. Similarly, we can show that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| R _ {0} ^ {- 1} \left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P B\right) ^ {\prime} K ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {\circ} \leq \left\| R _ {0} ^ {- 1} \Delta_ {B} P B K ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {\circ} + \left\| R _ {0} ^ {- 1} B ^ {\top} P ^ {\prime} B K ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {\circ} + \left\| R _ {0} ^ {- 1} B P \Delta_ {B} K ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {\circ} \\ \leq \left(\| R _ {0} ^ {- 1} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {o p}} + \| R _ {0} ^ {- 1} B ^ {\top} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| P ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}\right) \| B K ^ {\prime} \| _ {\circ} + \| R _ {0} ^ {- 1} B \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \epsilon_ {\circ} \| K ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq \left(\| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {o p}} + \| P ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}\right) \| B K ^ {\prime} \| _ {\circ} + \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \epsilon_ {\circ} \| K ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \operatorname {p o l y} (\| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}) \epsilon_ {\circ} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {o p}}. \\ \end{array}
$$
