# D Self-Bounding ODE Method

We begin by stating Theorem 13, which provides a generic guarantee for self-bounding ODES (Definition 3.3).

Theorem 13. Let  $(F, \mathcal{U}, g, \| \cdot \|, x(\cdot))$  be a self-bounding tuple. Suppose that for some  $\eta > 0$ ,  $h(\cdot)$  satisfies  $h(z) \geq g(z) + \eta$  for all  $z \geq \| y(0)\|$ , and that the scalar ODE

$$
w (0) = \| y (0) \| + \eta , \quad w ^ {\prime} (t) = h (w (t)
$$

has a continuously differentiable solution on  $[0,1]$ . Then, there exists a unique continuously differentiable function  $y(t) \in \mathcal{U}$  defined on  $[0,1]$  which satisfies  $F(x(t), y(t)) = 0$ , and this solution satisfies  $\|y(t)\| \leq w(t) \leq w(1)$ ,  $\|y'(t)\| \leq g(w(t)) \leq g(w(1))$  for all  $t \in [0,1]$ .

We shall prove the above theorem, and then derive Corollary 3 as a consequence. We begin the proof of this theorem with a simple scalar comparison inequality.

Lemma D.1 (Scalar Comparison Inequalities for Curves). Suppose that  $x(t), w(t)$  are continuously differentiable curves defined on  $[0, u)$ . Suppose further that, for a function  $f(\cdot, \cdot)$ ,  $x'(t) = f(x(t), t)$  and that  $w'(t) = g(x(t))$ . In addition, suppose

1.  $w(0) > x(0)$  
2.  $g(\cdot)\geq 0$  
3. For  $t \in [0, u)$  such that  $x(t) \geq w(0)$ ,  $g(x(t)) > f(x(t), t)$ .

Then,  $x(t) < w(t)$  for  $t \in [0, u)$ .

Proof. Define  $\delta(t) = w(t) - x(t)$ . Since  $\delta(0) > 0$ , there exists an  $s > 0$  such that  $\delta(t)$  for  $t \in [0, s)$ . Choose the maximal such  $s := \sup \{t : \delta(t') \geq 0, \forall t' < t\}$ , and suppose for the sake of contradiction that  $s < u$ . Then, by continuity,  $\delta(s) = 0$ , and therefore  $\delta'(s) = g(w(s)) - f(x(s), s) = g(x(s)) - f(x(s), s)$ , since  $x(s) = w(u)$  for  $\delta(s) = 0$ .

Next, note that since  $g(\cdot) \geq 0$ ,  $w(t)$  is non-decreasing on  $[0, u)$ , and thus  $w(s) \geq w(0)$  for all  $s \in [0, u)$ . Since  $x(s) = w(s)$  at  $s$ , we have  $x(s) \geq w(0)$  as well. Thus,  $\delta'(s) = g(x(s)) - f(x(s), s) > 0$ , by the assumption of the lemma. Hence, for an  $\epsilon > 0$  sufficiently small,  $\delta(s - \epsilon) < \delta(s) = 0$ . This contradicts the fact that of  $\delta(t') = 0$  for all  $t' < s$ .

Next, we extend the above scalar comparison inequality to a comparison inequality between scalar ODEs, and vector ODEs.

Lemma D.2 (Norm Comparison for Vector ODE). Let  $\| \cdot \|$  denote an arbitrary norm. Suppose that  $v(t)\in \mathbb{R}^d$  is a continuously differentiable curve defined on  $[0,u)$  such that  $\| v^{\prime}(t)\| \leq g(\| v(t)\|)$  for a non-decreasing function  $g$ . Fix  $\eta >0$ , and let  $h(z)$  denote a function such that  $h(z)\geq \max \{0,g(z) + \eta \}$  for all  $z\geq \| v(0)\|$ . Then, if the ODE

$$
w (0) = \| v (0) \| + \eta , \quad w ^ {\prime} (t) = h (w (t))
$$

has a continuously differentiable solution defined on  $[0, u)$ , then  $\| v(t) \| \leq w(t)$  for all  $t \in [0, u)$

Proof of Lemma D.2. The main challenge is that  $\| \cdot \|$  may be non-smooth. We circumvent this with a Gaussian approximation. Let  $c_{Z} \coloneqq \mathbb{E}_{Z \sim \mathcal{N}(0,I)}[\| Z\|]$ , and for every  $\eta > 0$ , and define  $\Psi_{\eta}(v) \coloneqq \mathbb{E}_{Z \sim \mathcal{N}(0,I)}[\| v + \frac{\eta}{2c_Z} Z\|]$ . Defining  $c_{Z} \coloneqq \mathbb{E}_{Z \sim \mathcal{N}(0,1)}[\| Z\|]$ . Moreover, we can see that  $0 \leq \| v \| \leq \Psi_{\eta}(v) \leq \| v \| + \eta / 2 < \| v \| + \eta$  by Jensen's inequality and the triangle inequality. Consider the curve  $x(t)$

$$
x (t) = \Psi_ {\eta} (v (t)), t \in [ 0, u),
$$

Note then that the curve satisfies

$$
x (0) = \Psi_ {\eta} (v (0)), \quad \mathrm {a n d} \quad x ^ {\prime} (t)) = f (t, x (t)) = \frac {\mathrm {d}}{\mathrm {d} t} (\Psi_ {\eta} (v (t))),
$$

where  $f(t,x(t))$  does not depend implicitly on  $x(t)$ , but only on  $t$  through the function  $t\mapsto v(t)$ .

Now, let  $g$  be a monotone function satisfying  $\| v'(t) \| \leq g(\| v(t) \|)$ , and let  $h$  be the assumed function satisfying  $h(z) \geq g(z) + \eta$  for all  $z \geq \| v(0) \| + \eta$ . We define the associated ODE

$$
w (0) = \| v (0) \| + \eta , w ^ {\prime} (t) = h (w (t)),
$$

which we assume is also defined on  $[0, u)$ . We would like to show that  $w(t) > x(t)$  for  $t \in [0, u)$ . To this end, we would like to verify the conditions of Lemma D.1. First, we have  $w(0) = \| v(0) \| + \eta > \Psi_{\eta}(v(0)) = x(0)$ , by above application of the triangle inequality.

For the second condition, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} f (t, x (t)) := \frac {\mathrm {d}}{\mathrm {d} t} (\Psi_ {\eta} (v (t))) = \frac {\mathrm {d}}{\mathrm {d} t} \mathbb {E} _ {Z \sim \mathcal {N} (0, I)} \left[ \left\| v + \frac {\eta}{2 c _ {Z}} Z \right\| \right] \\ \leq \mathbb {E} _ {Z \sim \mathcal {N} (0, 1)} \left[ \left\| v ^ {\prime} (t) + \frac {\eta}{2 c _ {Z}} Z \right\| \right] \\ = \Psi_ {\eta} \left(v ^ {\prime} (t)\right) <   \| v ^ {\prime} (t) \| + \eta \\ \leq g (\| v (t) \|) + \eta \quad \mathrm {(s i n c e} g \mathrm {s a t i s f i e s} \| v ^ {\prime} (t) \| \leq g (\| v (t) \|) \\ \leq g \left(\Psi_ {\eta} (v (t)) + \eta \quad (\text {s i n c e} g \text {i s m o n o t o n e}) \right. \\ = g (x (t)) + \eta . \\ \end{array}
$$

Now, if  $h(z) \geq g(z) + \eta$  for any  $z \geq w(0)$ , then, we see that, for any  $t \in [0, u)$  such that  $x(t) \geq w(0)$ , we have  $f(t, x(t)) \leq h(x(t))$ . Lemma D.1 therefore implies that  $x(t) \leq w(t)$  for  $t \in [0, u)$ . But  $x(t) = \Psi_{\eta}(v(t)) \geq \| v(t) \|$ .

Let us now prove the general guarantee for self-bounding functions.

Proof of Theorem 13. Observe that by the valid-function assumption and the assumption that  $F(x(0), y(0))$  has a solution, there exists some interval  $[0, u)$  on which a solution  $y(t)$  to  $F(x(t), y(t)) = 0$  exists. Let  $u$  denote the maximal value of  $u \leq 2$  for which this holds.

First, let us bound  $\| y(t) \|$  for  $t \in \mathcal{I} := [0, u) \cap [0, 1]$ . By assumption, there is a function  $h(z) \geq g(z) + \eta$ , where  $g(z)$  is non-negative and non-decreasing, such that the scalar ODE  $w'(t) = h(w(t))$  has a solution on  $[0, 1]$  with  $w(0) = \| y(0) \| + \eta$ . By Lemma D.2, we then that  $\| y(t) \| \leq w(t)$  on  $\mathcal{I}$ . Moreover, since  $w'(t) \geq 0$  since  $h$  is non-negative, we have  $\| y(t) \| \leq w(t) \leq w(1)$  on  $\mathcal{I}$ .

We conclude by showing that  $\mathcal{I} = [0,1]$ . Suppose for the sake of contradiction that  $\mathcal{I} \neq [0,1]$ . Then  $u \in (0,1]$ . Moreover, by Definition 3.2,  $F(x(u),\cdot) = 0$  has no solution, since otherwise,  $y(t)$  would be defined on  $[0,u + \epsilon)$  for some  $\epsilon > 0$ , contradicting the maximality of  $u$ . Therefore, to contradict our hypothesis  $\mathcal{I} \neq [0,1]$ , it suffices to show that  $F(x(u),\cdot) = 0$  has a solution. To this end, define

$$
\widetilde {y} (s) := \int_ {0} ^ {s} y ^ {\prime} (t) d t,
$$

which is well defined and continuous for  $s \in [0, u)$ , since  $y'(s)$  is continuously differentiable on this interval. Moreover,  $\| y'(t) \| \leq g(y(t)) \leq g(w(t)) \leq g(w(1))$  on  $[0, u)$  since  $y(t) \leq w(t) \leq w(1)$ . Therefore,  $y'(t)$  is uniformly bound on  $[0, u)$ , so that  $\widetilde{y}(u) = \lim_{s \to u} \widetilde{y}(s)$  is well-defined at  $u$ , and in fact continuous on  $[0, u]$ .

Since  $\widetilde{y}(s)$  is continuous on  $[0, u]$ , and since  $F(\cdot, \cdot)$  and  $x(s)$  are continuous,  $\lim_{s \to u} F(x(s), \widetilde{y}(s)) = F(x(u), \widetilde{y}(u))$ . But by the fundamental theorem of Calculus, we see that  $\widetilde{y}(s) = y(s)$  for  $s \in [0, u)$ , so that  $F(x(u), \widetilde{y}(s)) = F(A(s), B(s), y(s)) = 0$  for  $s \in [0, u)$ . Thus,  $\lim_{s \to u} F(A(s), B(s), \widetilde{y}(s)) = 0$ , and hence  $F(x(u), \widetilde{y}(u)) = 0$ . This shows that  $F(x(u), \cdot) = 0$  has a solution, as needed.

We now prove the corollary for the specific function form  $g(z) = cz^p$ .

Proof of Corollary 3. Fix  $\eta >0$  to be selected later. By assumption, we have

$$
\| y ^ {\prime} (t) \| \leq g (\| y (t) \|), \quad g (z) = c z ^ {p}.
$$

Moreover, for an  $\eta > 0$  to be selected, and for  $z \geq \|y(0)\|$ , we have

$$
g (z) + \eta \leq \underbrace {(1 + \frac {\eta}{c \| y (0) \| ^ {p}})} _ {:= c _ {\eta}} z ^ {p} := h (z).
$$

Now, consider the ODE

$$
w _ {\eta} ^ {\prime} (t) = h (w _ {\eta} (t)), \quad w _ {\eta} (0) = \| y (0) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \eta .
$$

Let us show that, for  $\eta$  sufficiently small, this ODE exists on  $[0,1]$ . Indeed, the solution to this the ODE is

$$
\frac {1}{(p - 1) w _ {\eta} ^ {p - 1} (0)} - \frac {1}{(p - 1) w _ {\eta} ^ {p - 1} (t)} = c _ {\eta} t.
$$

So that a continuously differentiable solution  $w_{\eta}(t)$  exists for  $t \in [0,1]$  as long as

$$
c _ {\eta} <   \frac {1}{(p - 1) w _ {\eta} ^ {p - 1} (0)} = \frac {1}{(p - 1) (\| y (0) \| + \eta) ^ {p - 1}}, \tag {D.1}
$$

and the solution is given by

$$
w _ {\eta} (t) = \left(\frac {1}{\left(\| y (0) \| + \eta\right) ^ {p - 1}} - (p - 1) c _ {\eta} t\right) ^ {- 1 / (p - 1)}.
$$

In particular, if  $c < \frac{1}{(p - 1)(\|y(0)\|)^{p - 1}}$ , then since  $\lim_{\eta \to 0}c_{\eta} = c$ , there exists an  $\eta_0 > 0$  sufficiently small so that the condition in (D.1) the above display holds for all  $\eta \in (0,\eta_0)$ . Therefore, by Theorem 13,

$$
\max  _ {t \in [ 0, 1 ]} \| y (t) \| \leq \inf  _ {\eta \in (0, \eta_ {0})} w _ {\eta} (t) = \left(\frac {1}{\| y (0) \| ^ {p - 1}} - c t\right) ^ {- (p - 1)} \leq \left(\frac {1}{\| y (0) \| ^ {p - 1}} - c (p - 1)\right) ^ {- 1 / (p - 1)}.
$$

In particular, when  $\alpha = c(p - 1)\| y(0)\|^{p - 1} < 1$ , then

$$
\max  _ {t \in [ 0, 1 ]} \| y (t) \| \leq (1 - \alpha) ^ {- 1 / (p - 1)} \| y (0) \|.
$$

Hence, for all  $t \in [0,1]$ , we have that  $\| y(t) \| \leq c (1 - \alpha)^{-p / (p - 1)} \| y(0) \|$ .

# E Concentration and Estimation Bounds

# E.1 Ordinary Least Squares Tools

In what follows, we develop a general toolkit for analyzing the performance of ordinary least squares.

Definition E.1 (Martingale Least Square Setup). First, let  $\{\mathbf{z}_t\}_{t\geq 1}\in (\mathbb{R}^d)^\mathbb{N}$  and  $\{\mathbf{y}_t\}_{t\geq 1}\in (\mathbb{R}^m)^\mathbb{N}$  denote sequences of random vectors adapted to a filtration  $\{\mathcal{F}_t\}_{t\geq 0}$ . We define the empirical covariance matrix  $\Lambda \coloneqq \sum_{t = 1}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t}\mathbf{z}_{t}^{\top}$ , we define the least-squares estimator

$$
\widehat {\boldsymbol {\Theta}} _ {T} := \left(\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \mathbf {y} _ {t} \mathbf {z} _ {t}\right) \left(\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \mathbf {z} _ {t} \mathbf {z} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {\dagger}
$$

Lastly, we assume that the sequence  $\pmb{e}_t\coloneqq \mathbf{y}_t - \Theta_\star \mathbf{z}_t\in \mathbb{R}^m$  is  $\sigma^2$ -sub-Gaussian conditioned on  $\mathcal{F}_{t - 1}$ .

We begin with a standard self-normalized tail bound (cf. Abbasi-Yadtori et al. [2011]).

Lemma E.1 (Self-Normalized Tail Bound). Suppose that  $\{\pmb{e}_t\}_{t\geq 1}\in \mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}}$  is a scalar  $\mathcal{F}_t$ -adapted sequence such that  $\pmb{e}_t\mid \mathcal{F}_{t - 1}$  is  $\sigma^2$  sub-Gaussian. Fix a matrix  $V_{0}\succeq 0$ . Then with probability  $1 - \delta$

$$
\left\| \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \mathbf {e} _ {t} \right\| _ {(V _ {0} + \boldsymbol {\Lambda}) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \leq 2 \sigma^ {2} \log \left\{\frac {1}{\delta} \det  (V _ {0} ^ {- 1 / 2} (V _ {0} + \boldsymbol {\Lambda}) V _ {0} ^ {- 1 / 2}) \right\}.
$$

As a corollary, we have the following Frobenius norm bound for regression, proved in Appendix E.3.1.

Lemma E.2 (Frobenius Norm Least Squares, Coarse Bound). In the martingale least squares setting of Definition E.1, we have

$$
\mathbb {P} \left[ \left\{\| \widehat {\Theta} _ {T} - \Theta_ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \geq 3 m \lambda_ {\min } (\boldsymbol {\Lambda}) ^ {- 1} \log \left\{\frac {m \det (3 \Lambda_ {0} ^ {- 1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {\Lambda}) \Lambda_ {0} ^ {- 1 / 2}}{\delta} \right\} \right\} \cap \{\boldsymbol {\Lambda} \succeq \Lambda_ {0} \} \right] \leq \delta ,
$$

and

$$
\mathbb {P} \left[ \left\{\| \widehat {\Theta} _ {T} - \Theta_ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \geq 6 \lambda_ {\min } (\boldsymbol {\Lambda}) ^ {- 1} (d \log 5 + \log \left\{\frac {\det  \left(3 \Lambda_ {0} ^ {- 1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {\Lambda}) \Lambda_ {0} ^ {- 1 / 2}}{\delta} \right\} \right\} \cap \{\boldsymbol {\Lambda} \succeq \Lambda_ {0} \} \right] \leq \delta . \quad (E. 1) \right.
$$

Unfortunately, this tail bound will lead to a dimension dependence of  $\Omega(d)$ , which may be suboptimal if  $\Lambda$  has eigenvalues of varying magnitude. Instead, we opt for a related bound that pays for Rayleigh quotients between  $\Lambda_0$  and  $\Lambda$ , proved in Appendix E.3.1.

Lemma E.3 (Two-Scale OLS Estimate). Consider martingale least squares setting of Definition E.1. Let  $\mathsf{P} \in \mathbb{R}^{d \times d}$  denote a projection matrix onto a subspace of dimension  $p$ , and fix an orthonormal basis of  $\mathbb{R}^d$ ,  $v_1, \ldots, v_d$ , such that  $v_1, \ldots, v_p$  form an orthonormal basis for the range of  $\mathsf{P}$ . Further fix positive constants  $0 < \lambda_1 \leq \nu \leq \lambda_2$  such that  $\nu \leq \sqrt{\lambda_1 \lambda_2 / 2}$ , and define the event

$$
\mathcal {E} := \left\{\Lambda \succeq \lambda_ {1} P + \lambda_ {2} (I - P) \right\} \cap \left\{\| P \Lambda (1 - P) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \nu \right\}
$$

Then, with probability  $1 - \delta$ , if  $\mathcal{E}$  holds,

$$
\| \widehat {\Theta} _ {T} - \Theta_ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \leq \frac {1 2 m p \kappa_ {1}}{\lambda_ {1}} \log \frac {3 m d \kappa_ {1}}{\delta} + \left(\frac {\nu}{\lambda_ {1}}\right) ^ {2} \cdot \frac {4 8 m (d - p) \kappa_ {2}}{\lambda_ {2}} \log \frac {3 m d \kappa_ {2}}{\delta}.
$$

where  $\kappa_{1}:= \max_{1\leq j\leq p}v_{j}^{\top}\Lambda v_{j} / \lambda_{1}$  and  $\kappa_{2}:= \max_{p + 1\leq j\leq d}v_{j}^{\top}\Lambda v_{j} / \lambda_{2}$ .

In the best case, the cross term bound  $\nu$  is taken to be equal to  $\lambda_{1}$ , in which case the bound reads

$$
\| \widehat {\Theta} _ {T} - \Theta_ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \leq \widetilde {\mathcal {O}} \left(\frac {m p \kappa_ {1}}{\lambda_ {1}} + \frac {m (d - p) \kappa_ {2}}{\lambda_ {2}}\right) = \widetilde {\mathcal {O}} \left(m \mathrm {t r} (\lambda_ {1} \mathsf {P} + \lambda_ {2} (I - \mathsf {P})) ^ {- 1}\right),
$$

which is what would typically expect from linear regression which independent samples. The main obstacle in proving Lemma E.3 is that two PSD matrices  $A \preceq B$  do not necessarily satisfy  $A^2 \preceq B^2$ , hence, we require a more careful argument which incurs dependence on the term  $\| \mathsf{P}\Lambda(1 - \mathsf{P}) \|_{\mathrm{op}}$  which is upper bounded by the parameter  $\nu$  in the lemma statement.

Lemma E.4 (Covariance Lower Bound). Suppose that  $\mathbf{z}_t\mid \mathcal{F}_{t - 1}\sim \mathcal{N}(\overline{\mathbf{z}}_t,\Sigma_t)$ , where  $\overline{\mathbf{z}}$  and  $\Sigma_t\in \mathbb{R}^d$  are  $\mathcal{F}_{t - 1}$ -measurable and  $\Sigma_t\succeq \Sigma\succ 0$ . Let  $\mathcal{E}$  be any event for which  $\overline{\Lambda}_T\coloneqq \mathbb{E}[\Lambda \mathbb{I}(\mathcal{E})]$  satisfies  $\mathrm{tr}(\overline{\Lambda}_T)\leq TJ$  for some  $J\geq 0$ . Then, for

$$
T \geq \frac {2 0 0 0}{9} \left(2 d \log \frac {1 0 0}{3} + d \log \frac {J}{\lambda_ {\min} (\Sigma)}\right),
$$

it holds that, for  $\Lambda_0\coloneqq \frac{9T}{1600}\Sigma$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left[ \left\{\Lambda \not \geq \frac {9 T}{1 6 0 0} \Sigma \right\} \cap \mathcal {E} \right] \leq 2 \exp \left(- \frac {9}{2 0 0 0 (d + 1)} T\right).
$$

# E.2 Basic Concentration Bounds

Here we state some useful concentration bounds for Gaussian distributions.

Lemma E.5 (Proposition 1.1 in Hsu et al. [2012]). Let  $\mathbf{g} \sim \mathcal{N}(0, I_d)$  be an isotropic Gaussian vector, and let  $A$  be a symmetric matrix. Then,

$$
\mathbb {P} \left[ \left| \mathbf {g} ^ {\top} A \mathbf {g} - \operatorname {t r} (A) \right| > 2 t ^ {1 / 2} \| A \| _ {\mathrm {F}} + 2 t \| A \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \right] \leq 2 e ^ {- t}.
$$

By replacing the Frobenius and operator norms in the above inequality with the Hilbert-Schmidt norm, we obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 6. Let  $A\succeq 0$ , and let  $\mathbf{g}\sim \mathcal{N}(0,I_d)$ . Then, with probability  $1 - \delta$  for any  $\delta < 1 / e$

$$
\mathbf {g} ^ {\top} A \mathbf {g} \lesssim \operatorname {t r} (A) \log \frac {1}{\delta} = \mathbb {E} [ \mathbf {g} ^ {\top} A \mathbf {g} _ {t} ] \log \frac {1}{\delta}.
$$

# E.3 Proofs from Appendix E.1

# E.3.1 Proof of Lemma E.2

We assume without loss of generality that  $\sigma^2 = 1$ . Let  $\pmb{e}_t = \mathbf{y}_t - \Theta_\star \mathbf{z}_t$ . Let  $\mathbf{X} \in \mathbb{R}^{Td}$  denote the matrix whose rows are  $\pmb{e}_t$ , and  $\pmb{E}^{(i)} \in \mathbb{R}^T$  denote the vector  $(e_{1,i},\dots,e_{T,i})$ , where  $e_{t,i}$  is the  $i$ -th coordinate of  $\pmb{e}_t$ . Let  $\lambda_{\mathrm{min}} \coloneqq \lambda_{\mathrm{min}}(\Lambda_0)$ . Then,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Theta}} _ {T} - \boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \left\| \boldsymbol {\Lambda} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\mathbf {X}} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\mathbf {E}} ^ {(i)} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \leq \lambda_ {\min } (\boldsymbol {\Lambda}) ^ {- 1} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \left\| \boldsymbol {\Lambda} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {X} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {E} ^ {(i)} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \lambda_ {\min } (\boldsymbol {\Lambda}) ^ {- 1} \left\| \mathbf {X} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {E} ^ {(i)} \right\| _ {\boldsymbol {\Lambda} ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \\ \leq \frac {3}{2} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \lambda_ {\min } (\boldsymbol {\Lambda}) ^ {- 1} \left\| \mathbf {X} ^ {\top} \mathbf {E} ^ {(i)} \right\| _ {(\boldsymbol {\Lambda} + \frac {1}{2} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {0}) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last line holds for  $\Lambda_0 \preceq \Lambda$ . Invoking Lemma E.1, we have that with probability at least  $1 - \delta$ , it holds for any fixed  $i \in [m]$  that

$$
\left\| \mathbf {X} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {E} ^ {(i)} \right\| _ {(\boldsymbol {\Lambda} + \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {0}) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \leq 2 \log \left\{\frac {1}{\delta} \det ((\frac {\boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {0}}{2}) ^ {- 1 / 2} (\frac {\boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {0}}{2} + \boldsymbol {\Lambda}) (\frac {\boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {0}}{2}) ^ {- 1 / 2}) \right\}.
$$

Since  $\Lambda_0 \preceq \Lambda$ , we have  $\frac{\Lambda_0}{2} + \Lambda \leq \frac{3}{2}\Lambda$ , when the above can be bounded by

$$
\left\| \mathbf {X} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {E} ^ {(i)} \right\| _ {(\boldsymbol {\Lambda} + \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {0}) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \leq 2 \log \left\{\frac {1}{\delta} \det  (3 (\boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {0}) ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {0} ^ {- 1 / 2}) \right\}.
$$

Union bounding over  $i \in [m]$  and summing the bound concludes.

# E.4 Proof of Lemma E.3

We begin with a linear algebraic lemma lower bounding the square of a PSD matrix in the Lowner order.

Lemma E.6. Let  $X = \begin{bmatrix} X_{11} & X_{12} \\ X_{12}^{\top} & X_{22} \end{bmatrix} \succ 0$ . Then, for any parameter  $\alpha > 0$ ,

$$
X ^ {2} \succeq \left[ \begin{array}{c c} (1 - \alpha_ {1}) X _ {1 1} ^ {2} + (1 - \alpha_ {2} ^ {- 1}) X _ {1 2} X _ {1 2} ^ {\top} & 0 \\ 0 & (1 - \alpha_ {2}) X _ {2 2} ^ {2} + (1 - \alpha_ {1} ^ {- 1}) X _ {1 2} ^ {\top} X _ {1 2} \end{array} \right]
$$

Proof of Lemma E.6. We begin by expanding

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left[ \begin{array}{c c} X _ {1 1} & X _ {1 2} \\ X _ {1 2} ^ {\top} & X _ {2 2} \end{array} \right] ^ {2} = \left[ \begin{array}{c c} X _ {1 1} ^ {2} + X _ {1 2} X _ {1 2} ^ {\top} & X _ {1 1} X _ {1 2} + X _ {1 2} X _ {2 2} \\ X _ {2 2} X _ {1 2} ^ {\top} + X _ {1 2} ^ {\top} X _ {1 1} & X _ {2 2} ^ {2} + X _ {1 2} ^ {\top} X _ {1 2} \end{array} \right] \\ = \left[ \begin{array}{c c} X _ {1 1} ^ {2} + X _ {1 2} X _ {1 2} ^ {\top} & 0 \\ 0 & X _ {2 2} ^ {2} + X _ {1 2} ^ {\top} X _ {1 2} \end{array} \right] + \left[ \begin{array}{c c} 0 & X _ {1 1} X _ {1 2} \\ X _ {1 2} ^ {\top} X _ {1 1} & 0 \end{array} \right] + \left[ \begin{array}{c c} 0 & X _ {1 2} X _ {2 2} \\ X _ {2 2} X _ {1 2} ^ {\top} & 0 \end{array} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

Now, for any vector  $v = (v_{1}, v_{2})$ , and any  $\alpha_{1} > 0$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\langle v, \left[ \begin{array}{c c} 0 & X _ {1 1} X _ {1 2} \\ X _ {1 2} ^ {\top} X _ {1 1} \end{array} \right] v \right\rangle = 2 v _ {1} ^ {\top} X _ {1 1} X _ {1 2} v _ {2} \\ \geq - 2 \| v _ {1} ^ {\top} X _ {1 1} \| \| X _ {1 2} v _ {2} \| \\ = - 2 \cdot \alpha_ {1} ^ {1 / 2} \| v _ {1} ^ {\top} X _ {1 1} \| \cdot \alpha_ {1} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| X _ {1 2} v _ {2} \| \\ \geq - \alpha_ {1} \| v _ {1} ^ {\top} X _ {1 1} \| ^ {2} - \alpha_ {1} ^ {- 1} \| X _ {1 2} v _ {2} \| ^ {2} \\ = \left\langle v, \left[ \begin{array}{c c} - \alpha_ {1} X _ {1 1} ^ {2} & 0 \\ 0 & - \alpha_ {1} ^ {- 1} X _ {1 2} ^ {\top} X _ {1 2} \end{array} \right] v \right\rangle . \\ \end{array}
$$

Similarly, for any  $v$  and  $\alpha_{2} > 0$ , we have

$$
\left\langle v, \left[ \begin{array}{c c} 0 & X _ {1 2} X _ {2 2} \\ X _ {2 2} X _ {1 2} ^ {\top} & \end{array} \right] v \right\rangle \geq \left\langle v, \left[ \begin{array}{c c} - \alpha_ {2} ^ {- 1} X _ {1 2} X _ {1 2} ^ {\top} & 0 \\ 0 & - \alpha_ {2} X _ {2 2} ^ {2} \end{array} \right] v \right\rangle .
$$

Thus, for any  $\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2} > 0$

$$
X ^ {2} \succeq \left[ \begin{array}{c c} (1 - \alpha_ {1}) X _ {1 1} ^ {2} + (1 - \alpha_ {2} ^ {- 1}) X _ {1 2} X _ {1 2} ^ {\top} & 0 \\ 0 & (1 - \alpha_ {2}) X _ {2 2} ^ {2} + (1 - \alpha_ {1} ^ {- 1}) X _ {1 2} ^ {\top} X _ {1 2} \end{array} \right]
$$

![](images/e89680a36d47daf3440f3229c750afe329d7a08e790e1b7130076c7ec4c8d725.jpg)

The important case of Lemma E.6 is when the matrix  $X$  in question can be lower bounded in terms of the weighted sum of two complementary projection matrices.

Lemma E.7. Let  $\mathsf{P}$  be an orthogonal projection matrix, let  $X\succeq 0$ , and suppose that there exist positive constants  $\lambda_1\leq \nu \leq \lambda_2$  be such that  $X\succeq \lambda_{1}(I - \mathsf{P}) + \lambda_{2}\mathsf{P}$ , and  $\| \mathsf{P}X(1 - \mathsf{P})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq \nu$ . Then, if  $\nu \leq \sqrt{\lambda_1\lambda_2} /2$ ,

$$
X ^ {2} \succeq \frac {1}{4} \lambda_ {1} ^ {2} \mathsf {P} + \frac {\lambda_ {1} ^ {2} \lambda_ {2} ^ {2}}{1 6 \nu^ {2}} (I - \mathsf {P})
$$

Proof of Lemma E.7. Denote the number of rows/columns of  $X$  by  $d$ . By an orthonormal change of basis, we may assume that  $\mathsf{P}$  is the projection onto the first  $k = \dim(\mathrm{range}(\mathsf{P}))$  canonical basis vectors. Writing  $X$  and  $\mathsf{P}$  in this basis we have

$$
X = \left[ \begin{array}{l l} X _ {1 1} & X _ {1 2} \\ X _ {1 2} ^ {\top} & X _ {2 2} \end{array} \right] \succeq \lambda_ {1} (I - \mathsf {P}) + \lambda_ {2} \mathsf {P} = \left[ \begin{array}{c c} \lambda_ {1} I _ {d - p} & 0 \\ 0 & \lambda_ {2} I _ {k} \end{array} \right]. \tag {E.2}
$$

It suffices to show that, in this basis

$$
X ^ {2} \succeq \left[ \begin{array}{c c} \frac {\lambda_ {1} ^ {2}}{4} I _ {p} & 0 \\ 0 & \frac {\lambda_ {2} ^ {2} \lambda_ {1} ^ {2}}{1 6 \nu^ {2}} I _ {d - p} \end{array} \right]. \tag {E.3}
$$

From Equation (E.2),  $X_{11} \succeq \lambda_1 I_p$ ,  $X_{22} \succeq \lambda_2 I_{p - d}$ , and  $\| X_{12}\|_{\mathrm{op}} = \| \mathsf{P}X(1 - \mathsf{P})\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ . Hence, hence the parameters  $\nu \geq \lambda_1$  in the lemma satisfies  $\nu \geq \| X_{12}\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ . Thus,

$$
X ^ {2} \succeq \left[ \begin{array}{c c} \{(1 - \alpha_ {1}) + (1 - \alpha_ {2} ^ {- 1}) (\nu / \lambda_ {1}) ^ {2} \} \cdot \lambda_ {1} ^ {2} I _ {p} & 0 \\ 0 & \{(1 - \alpha_ {2}) + (1 - \alpha_ {1} ^ {- 1}) (\nu / \lambda_ {2}) ^ {2} \} \cdot \lambda_ {2} ^ {2} I _ {d - p} \end{array} \right].
$$

Set  $\alpha_{1} = \frac{1}{2}$ , and take  $\alpha_{2}$  to satisfy  $(1 - \alpha_{2}^{-1})(\nu /\lambda_{1})^{2} = -1 / 4$ . Then, we have the following string of implications

$$
1 - \alpha_ {2} ^ {- 1} = - \lambda_ {1} ^ {2} / 4 \nu^ {2} \quad \mathrm {i m p l i e s} \quad \alpha_ {2} ^ {- 1} = \frac {4 \nu^ {2} + \lambda_ {1} ^ {2}}{\nu^ {2}} \quad \mathrm {i m p l i e s}
$$

$$
\alpha_ {2} = \frac {4 \nu^ {2}}{4 \nu^ {2} + \lambda_ {1} ^ {2}} \quad \mathrm {i m p l i e s} \quad 1 - \alpha_ {2} = \frac {\lambda_ {1} ^ {2}}{4 \nu^ {2} + \lambda_ {1} ^ {2}} \geq \frac {\lambda_ {1} ^ {2}}{8 \nu^ {2}},
$$

where in the last line we use  $\nu \geq \lambda_{1}$ . For this choice, and using  $\nu \leq \sqrt{\lambda_1\lambda_2} / 2$

$$
X ^ {2} \succeq \left[ \begin{array}{c c} \frac {\lambda_ {1} ^ {2}}{4} I _ {p} & 0 \\ 0 & \{\frac {\lambda_ {1} ^ {2}}{8 \nu^ {2}} - \frac {\nu^ {2}}{\lambda_ {2} ^ {2}} \} \cdot \lambda_ {2} ^ {2} I _ {d - p} \end{array} \right] \succeq \left[ \begin{array}{c c} \frac {\lambda_ {1} ^ {2}}{4} I _ {p} & 0 \\ 0 & \frac {\lambda_ {2} ^ {2} \lambda_ {1} ^ {2}}{1 6 \nu^ {2}} I _ {d - p} \end{array} \right],
$$

as needed.

![](images/ffea1467bf7fc4a82860027e8e8784bbea31f2fdce776238437b06e47c9ec854.jpg)

We are now in a position to prove our desired lemma.

Concluding the proof of Lemma E.3. Let's establish the following notation:

- We assume without loss of generality that  $\sigma^2 = 1$ .  
- Let  $\pmb{e}_t = \mathbf{y}_t - \Theta_\star \mathbf{z}_t$ . Let  $\pmb{E} \in \mathbb{R}^{Td}$  denote the matrix whose rows are  $\pmb{e}_t$ , and  $\pmb{E}^{(i)} \in \mathbb{R}^T$  denote the vector  $(e_{1,i}, \ldots, e_{T,i})$ , where  $\pmb{e}_{t,i}$  is the  $i$ -th coordinate of  $\pmb{e}_t$ .  
- Recall that  $v_{1,\dots,p}$  denote a basis for the range of the projection operator  $\mathsf{P}$ , and  $v_{p+1,\dots,d}$  complete this basis to form an orthonormal basis for  $\mathbb{R}^d$ .

By the assumptions that  $\Lambda \succeq \lambda_1(I - \mathsf{P}) + \lambda_2\mathsf{P}$ , and  $\nu \geq \lambda_{1} \vee \| \mathsf{P}\mathbf{A}(1 - \mathsf{P})\|$ , and  $\nu \leq \sqrt{\lambda_1\lambda_2} / 2$ , Lemma E.7 implies that

$$
\boldsymbol {\Lambda} ^ {2} \succeq \frac {1}{4} \lambda_ {1} ^ {2} \mathsf {P} + \frac {\lambda_ {1} ^ {2} \lambda_ {2} ^ {2}}{1 6 \nu^ {2}} (I - \mathsf {P})
$$

so after inversion,

$$
\left(\mathbf {X} _ {T} ^ {\top} \mathbf {X} _ {T}\right) ^ {- 2} \preceq 4 \lambda_ {- 1} ^ {2} \mathsf {P} + \frac {1 6 \nu^ {2}}{\lambda_ {1} ^ {2} \lambda_ {2} ^ {2}} (I - \mathsf {P}).
$$

Hence, we can render

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Theta}} _ {T} - \boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \left\| (\mathbf {X} ^ {\top} \mathbf {X}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {X} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {E} ^ {(i)} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \left\langle \mathbf {X} ^ {\top} \mathbf {\boldsymbol {E}} ^ {(i)}, (\mathbf {X} ^ {\top} \mathbf {X}) ^ {- 2} \mathbf {X} ^ {\top} \mathbf {\boldsymbol {E}} ^ {(i)} \right\rangle \\ \leq \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \left\langle \mathbf {X} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {E} ^ {(i)}, \left(4 \lambda_ {1} ^ {- 2} \mathsf {P} + \frac {1 6 \nu^ {2}}{\lambda_ {1} ^ {2} \lambda_ {2} ^ {2}} (I - \mathsf {P})\right) \mathbf {X} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {E} ^ {(i)} \right\rangle \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} 4 \lambda_ {1} ^ {- 2} \| \mathsf {P} \mathbf {X} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {E} ^ {(i)} \| ^ {2} + \frac {1 6 \nu^ {2}}{\lambda_ {1} ^ {2} \lambda_ {2} ^ {2}} \| (I - \mathsf {P}) \mathbf {X} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {E} ^ {(i)} \| ^ {2} \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \frac {4}{\lambda_ {1} ^ {2}} \left(\sum_ {j = 1} ^ {p} \left\langle v _ {j}, \mathbf {X} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {E} ^ {(i)} \right\rangle^ {2}\right) + \frac {1 6 \nu^ {2}}{\lambda_ {1} ^ {2} \lambda_ {2} ^ {2}} \left(\sum_ {j = p + 1} ^ {d} \left\langle v _ {j}, \mathbf {X} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {E} ^ {(i)} \right\rangle^ {2}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

For an index  $j$ , let  $\lambda[j]$  equal  $\lambda_1$  if  $j \leq p$ , and  $\lambda_2$  if  $p + 1 \leq d \leq d$ , and define the vector  $\mathbf{X}_j = \mathbf{X}v_j$ . Then,  $\left\langle v_j, \mathbf{X}^\top \mathbf{E}^{(i)} \right\rangle^2$  can be bounded as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\langle v _ {j}, \mathbf {X} ^ {\top} \mathbf {E} ^ {(i)} \right\rangle^ {2} = \| \mathbf {X} _ {j} ^ {\top} \mathbf {E} ^ {(i)} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ = \| \mathbf {X} _ {j} \| ^ {2} \cdot \frac {\left\| \mathbf {X} _ {j} ^ {\top} \mathbf {E} ^ {(i)} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{\| \mathbf {X} _ {j} \| _ {2} ^ {2}} \\ \leq \| \mathbf {X} _ {j} \| ^ {2} \cdot \frac {3}{2} \frac {\left\| \mathbf {X} _ {j} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {E} ^ {(i)} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{\| \mathbf {X} _ {j} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2} \lambda [ j ]}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where in the last inequality we use that  $\| \mathbf{X}_j\| _2^2 = v_j^\top \pmb {\Lambda}v_j\geq v_j^\top (\lambda_1(1 - \mathsf{P}) + \lambda_2\mathsf{P})v_j = \lambda [j]$

By the scalar-valued self normalized tail inequality Lemma E.1, it holds with probability  $1 - \delta$  that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\langle v _ {j}, \mathbf {X} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {E} ^ {(i)} \right\rangle^ {2} \leq 3 \| \mathbf {X} _ {j} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \log \frac {\frac {1}{2} \lambda [ j ] + \| \mathbf {X} _ {j} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{\frac {1}{2} \lambda_ {j} [ j ] \delta} \\ \leq 3 \lambda [ j ] \kappa [ j ] \log \frac {\frac {1}{2} \lambda [ j ] + \lambda [ j ] \kappa [ j ]}{\frac {1}{2} \lambda_ {j} [ j ] \delta} \\ \leq 3 \lambda [ j ] \kappa [ j ] \log \frac {3 \kappa [ j ]}{\delta} \\ = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} 3 \lambda_ {1} \boldsymbol {\kappa} _ {1} \log \frac {3 \boldsymbol {\kappa} _ {1}}{\delta} & j \leq d \\ 3 \lambda_ {2} \boldsymbol {\kappa} _ {2} \log \frac {3 \boldsymbol {\kappa} _ {2}}{\delta} & j \geq d \end{array} \right. \\ \end{array}
$$

where we set  $\kappa[j] := \frac{v_j^\top \Lambda v_j}{\lambda[j]} \geq 1$ , and note that  $\kappa_1 := \max_{1 \leq j \leq d} \kappa[j]$  and  $\kappa_2 := \max_{j > p} \kappa[j]$ . Hence, taking a union bound over all  $dm$  coordinates

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \widehat {\Theta} _ {T} - \Theta_ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \\ \leq \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \frac {4}{\lambda_ {1} ^ {2}} \left(\sum_ {j = 1} ^ {p} 3 \lambda_ {1} \kappa_ {1} \log \frac {3 d m \kappa_ {1}}{\delta}\right) + \frac {1 6 \nu^ {2}}{\lambda_ {1} ^ {2} \lambda_ {2} ^ {2}} \left(\sum_ {j = p + 1} ^ {d} 3 \lambda_ {2} \kappa_ {2} \log \frac {3 d m \kappa_ {2}}{\delta}\right) \\ \leq \frac {1 2 m p \kappa_ {1}}{\lambda_ {1}} \log \frac {3 m d \kappa_ {1}}{\delta} + \left(\frac {\nu}{\lambda_ {1}}\right) ^ {2} \cdot \frac {4 8 m (p - d) \kappa_ {2}}{\lambda_ {2}} \log \frac {3 m d \kappa_ {2}}{\delta}. \\ \end{array}
$$

# E.4.1 Proof of Lemma E.4

By the Paley-Zygmund inequality (specifically, the variant in Simchowitz et al. [2018, Equation 3.12]), one can easily show that the sequence  $(\mathbf{z}_t)$  satisfies the  $(1,\Sigma ,\frac{3}{10})$ -block martingale small ball property [Simchowitz et al., 2018, Definition 2.1]. Then, for any matrix  $\Lambda_{+}\succeq 0$ , Simchowitz et al. [2018, Section D.2] (correcting the section for a lost normalization factor of  $\mathrm{T}$ ) shows that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left[ \left\{\Lambda \not \geq \frac {T}{1 6} (\frac {3}{1 0}) ^ {2} \Sigma \right\} \cap \{\Lambda \preceq \Lambda_ {+} \} \right] \leq \exp \left(- \frac {1}{1 0} T (\frac {3}{1 0}) ^ {2} + 2 d \log (\frac {1 0 0}{3}) + \log \det  \Lambda_ {+} (T \Sigma) ^ {- 1}\right) \\ \leq \exp \left(- \frac {9 T}{1 0 0 0} + 2 d \log \left(\frac {1 0 0}{3}\right) + d \log \frac {\| \Lambda_ {+} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{T \lambda_ {\min } (\Sigma)}\right). \tag {E.4} \\ \end{array}
$$

Now, notice that if we select  $\Lambda_{+} = \frac{\mathrm{tr}(\overline{\Lambda}_{T})}{\delta} I$ , the bound  $\| \Lambda \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq \mathrm{tr}(\Lambda)$  for  $\Lambda \succeq 0$  and an application of Markov's inequality show that,  $\mathbb{P}[\{\Lambda \not\succ \Lambda_{+}\} \cap \mathcal{E}] \leq \delta$ . Hence, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left[ \boldsymbol {\Lambda} \not \geq \frac {T}{1 6} (\frac {3}{1 0}) ^ {2} \right] \leq \inf  _ {\delta > 0} \exp \left(- \frac {9 T}{1 0 0 0} + 2 d \log (\frac {1 0 0}{3}) + d \log \frac {\operatorname {t r} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\Lambda}}} _ {T})}{T \lambda_ {\min } (\Sigma) \delta}\right) + \delta \\ \leq \inf _ {\delta > 0} \delta^ {- d} \exp \left(- \frac {9 T}{1 0 0 0} + 2 d \log \left(\frac {1 0 0}{3}\right) + d \log \frac {\operatorname {t r} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\Lambda}}} _ {T})}{T \lambda_ {\min} (\Sigma) \delta}\right) + \delta . \\ \end{array}
$$

Note that balancing  $a\delta^{-d} = \delta$  selects  $\delta = a^{1 / d + 1}$ , giving that the above is at most

$$
\left. 2 \exp \left(- \frac {1}{d + 1} \left(\frac {9 T}{1 0 0 0} - 2 d \log \left(\frac {1 0 0}{3}\right) - d \log \frac {\operatorname {t r} \left(\overline {{\boldsymbol {\Lambda}}} _ {T}\right)}{T \lambda_ {\min } (\Sigma)}\right)\right)\right).
$$

We conclude by bounding  $\mathrm{tr}(\overline{\Lambda}_T) \leq JT$  by assumption and applying some elementary algebra.

# Part II

# Proof of Upper and Lower Bounds

# F Proofs for Lower Bound (Section 4)

# F.1 Proof of Lemma 4.1

Observe that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max  \left\{\| A _ {e} - A _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}, \| B _ {e} - B _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \right\} \leq \max  \left\{\| A _ {e} - A _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {F}}, \| B _ {e} - B _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \right\} \\ \leq \max  \left\{\| K _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 / 2}, 1 \right\} \| \Delta \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ \leq \sqrt {m n} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {p a c k}} \max  \{\| K _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 / 2}, 1 \} \leq \sqrt {\| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}} \sqrt {m n} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {p a c k}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality is by Lemma B.8. This proves the first point of the lemma. Next, if  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}}^2 \leq \frac{1}{\|P_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}}} C_{\mathrm{safe}}^2 (A_\star, B_\star) / nm$ , then,

$$
\max  \left\{\| A _ {e} - A _ {\star} \| _ {\text {o p}}, \| B _ {e} - B _ {\star} \| _ {\text {o p}} \right\} \leq \frac {1}{C _ {\text {s a f e}} (A _ {\star} , B _ {\star})} \leq (1 - 2 ^ {1 / 5}),
$$

which implies  $\Psi_{e} \leq 2^{1/5} \max \{1, \|A_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}, \|B_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\}$ . Moreover Theorem 5 yields

$$
\| P _ {e} - P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq 1. 0 8 5 \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq 2 ^ {1 / 5} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}.
$$

For the next point, Theorem 12 bounds the error of the Taylor approximation, and implies that for some polynomial  $\mathfrak{p}$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| - \left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B _ {\star} ^ {\top} P _ {\star} B _ {\star}\right) ^ {- 1} \cdot \Delta^ {\top} P _ {\star} A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star} + K _ {\star} - K _ {e} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \\ \leq \mathfrak {p} \left(\| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}\right) \max  \left\{\| A _ {e} - A _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}, \| B _ {e} - B _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \right\} ^ {2} \max  \left\{\| A _ {e} - A _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {F}}, \| B _ {e} - B _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \right\} ^ {2} \\ \leq \mathfrak {p} \left(\| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}\right) \max  \left\{\| A _ {e} - A _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {F}}, \| B _ {e} - B _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \right\} ^ {4} \\ \leq (n m) ^ {2} \underbrace {\| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \mathfrak {p} (\| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}})} _ {:= \mathfrak {p} _ {2} (\mathfrak {p} (\| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}))} \epsilon_ {\text {p a c k}} ^ {4}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Finally, point 4 follows by bounding

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| K _ {\star} - K _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \leq \| - (R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B _ {\star} ^ {\top} P _ {\star} B _ {\star}) ^ {- 1} \cdot \Delta^ {\top} P _ {\star} A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star} + K _ {\star} - K _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} + \| (R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B _ {\star} ^ {\top} P _ {\star} B _ {\star}) ^ {- 1} \cdot \Delta^ {\top} P _ {\star} A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ \leq m n \epsilon_ {\text {p a c k}} ^ {2} \mathfrak {p} _ {2} \left(\| P _ {\star} \| _ {\text {o p}}\right) + \| \left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B _ {\star} ^ {\top} P _ {\star} B _ {\star}\right) ^ {- 1} \cdot \Delta^ {\top} P _ {\star} A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ \leq m n \epsilon_ {\text {p a c k}} ^ {2} \mathfrak {p} _ {2} \left(\| P _ {\star} \| _ {\text {o p}}\right) + \| \Delta \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\text {o p}} \| A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star} \| _ {\text {o p}} \\ \leq m n \epsilon_ {\text {p a c k}} ^ {2} \mathfrak {p} _ {2} \left(\| P _ {\star} \| _ {\text {o p}}\right) + \| \Delta \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\text {o p}} ^ {3 / 2} \tag {Lemma B.8} \\ \leq m n \epsilon_ {\text {p a c k}} ^ {2} \mathfrak {p} _ {2} (\| P _ {\star} \| _ {\text {o p}}) + \epsilon_ {\text {p a c k}} \sqrt {m n} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\text {o p}} ^ {3 / 2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

By taking  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}}^2 \leq 1 / mn\mathrm{poly}(\| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}})$ , the expression above can be made to be at most  $2mn\epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}}^2 \| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}^3$ .

# F.2 Proof of Lemma 4.3

Our strategy is to relate  $\mathrm{Regret}_T[\pi; A_e, B_e]$  and  $\mathrm{K-Err}_e[\pi]$  to the benchmark inducted by following the true optimal policy  $\pi_\star = \pi_\star(A, B)$  which minimizes  $\mathbb{E}_{A_e, B_e, \pi} \sum_{t=1}^T c(\mathbf{x}_t, \mathbf{u}_t)$  over all possible policies  $\pi$ .

To begin, consider an arbitrary stabilizable system  $(A,B)$ . Let  $K_{\infty} \coloneqq K_{\infty}(A,B)$  and  $P_{\infty} = P_{\infty}(A,B)$ . For  $T$  fixed and a control policy  $\pi$ , let

$$
\operatorname {K - E r r} [ \pi ] := \mathbb {E} _ {A, B, \pi} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \| \mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {\infty} (A, B) \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \right].
$$

We define the  $Q$ -functions and value functions associated with the LQR problem as follows.

$$
\mathbf {Q} _ {t; T} (x, u) := \mathbb {E} _ {A, B, \pi_ {\star}} \left[ \sum_ {s = t} ^ {T} c (\mathbf {x} _ {s}, \mathbf {u} _ {s}) \mid \mathbf {x} _ {t} = x, \mathbf {u} _ {t} = u \right] \qquad \mathbf {V} _ {t; T} (x) := \inf _ {u} \mathbf {Q} _ {t; T} (x, u),
$$

where  $\mathbb{E}_{A,B,\pi_{\star}(A,B)}[\cdot |\mathbf{x}_t = x,\mathbf{u}_t = u]$  denotes that the state at time  $t$  is  $\mathbf{x}_t = x$ , inputs is  $\mathbf{u}_t = u$ , and all future inputs are according to the policy  $\pi_{\star}(A,B)$ . Note then that  $\pi_{\star}$  always perscribes the action  $\mathbf{u}_t \coloneqq \arg \min_{t\in T} \mathbf{Q}_{t;T}(\mathbf{x}_t,u)$  at time  $t$ . We can now characterize the form of the  $\mathbf{Q}_{t;T}$  and  $\pi_{\star}$  using the following lemma.

Lemma F.1 (Optimal Finite-Horizon Controllers [Bertsekas, 2005]). Define the elements

$$
P _ {t + 1} := R _ {\mathbf {x}} + A ^ {\top} P _ {t} A - A ^ {\top} P _ {t} B \Sigma_ {t} ^ {- 1} B ^ {\top} P _ {t} A,
$$

$$
\Sigma_ {t + 1} := R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P _ {t} B,
$$

$$
K _ {t + 1} := - \Sigma_ {t + 1} ^ {- 1} B ^ {\top} P _ {t} A,
$$

with the convention that  $P_0 = R_{\mathbf{x}}$ . Then,  $\mathbf{V}_{t;T}(x) = x^{\top}P_{T - t}x$ , and  $\mathbf{Q}_{t;T}(x,u) - \mathbf{V}_{t;T}(x) = \| u - K_{T - t}x\|_{\Sigma_{T - t}}^2$ , and  $(\pi_{\star})_{t;T}(\mathbf{x}_t) = K_{T - t}\mathbf{x}_t$ .

For completeness, we prove the lemma in Section F.2.1. Having defined the true optimal policy, we that the regret is lower bounded as follows.

Lemma F.2. Fix a system  $A, B$ , and suppose that  $\mathrm{Regret}_T[\pi; A, B] \leq T \mathcal{J}_{A,B}^\star$ . Then,

$$
\operatorname {R e g r e t} _ {T} [ \pi ; A, B ] \geq \frac {1}{2} \mathrm {K - E r r} [ \pi ] - \mathcal {J} _ {A, B} ^ {\star} \left(2 T \left(\max  _ {t \geq T / 2} \eta_ {t}\right) + \sum_ {t \geq 0} \eta_ {t}\right),
$$

where we define the errors  $\eta_t \coloneqq \|\Sigma_t^\top(K_\infty - K_t)R_{\mathbf{x}}^{-1/2}\|_2^2$ .

Proof of Lemma F.2. We compare both the cost under  $\pi$  and the cost under a comparator to  $\mathbf{V}_{1:T}(0)$ , the value of the optimal policy starting at  $\mathbf{x}_1 = 0$ .

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {R e g r e t} _ {T} [ \pi ; A, B ] = \mathbb {E} _ {A, B, \pi} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} c _ {t} (\mathbf {x} _ {t}, \mathbf {u} _ {t}) \right] - \mathbf {V} _ {1; T} (0) - \left(T \mathcal {J} _ {A, B} ^ {\star} K _ {\infty} - \mathbf {V} _ {1; T} (0)\right) \\ \geq \mathbb {E} _ {A, B, \pi} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} c _ {t} (\mathbf {x} _ {t}, \mathbf {u} _ {t}) \right] - \mathbf {V} _ {1; T} (0) - (T \mathbb {E} _ {A, B, K _ {\infty}} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} c _ {t} (\mathbf {x} _ {t}, \mathbf {u} _ {t}) \right] - \mathbf {V} _ {1; T} (0)), \\ \end{array}
$$

where we use the fact that the infinite horizon regret induced by  $K_{\star}$  on a finite time horizon  $T$  is upper bounded by  $T$ -times the infinite horizon cost (this can be verified by direct computation).

Next, we use the performance difference lemma, which states that for any policy  $\pi'$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {A, B, \pi^ {\prime}} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} c _ {t} (\mathbf {x} _ {t}, \mathbf {u} _ {t}) \right] - \mathbf {V} _ {0; T} (0) = \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \mathbb {E} _ {A, B, \pi^ {\prime}} [ \mathbf {Q} _ {t; T} (\mathbf {x} _ {t}, \mathbf {u} _ {t}) - \mathbf {V} _ {t; T} (\mathbf {x} _ {t}) ] \\ = \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \mathbb {E} _ {A, B, \pi^ {\prime}} \left[ \| \mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {T - t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| _ {\Sigma_ {T - t}} ^ {2} \right]. \quad (\text {L e m m a F . 1}) \\ \end{array}
$$

Therefore,

$$
\mathrm {R e g r e t} _ {T} [ \pi ; A, B ] = \underbrace {\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \mathbb {E} _ {A , B , \pi} \left[ \| \mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {T - t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| _ {\Sigma_ {T - t}} ^ {2} \right]} _ {\mathrm {(p o l i c y s u b o p t i m a l i t y)}} - \underbrace {\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \mathbb {E} _ {A , B , K _ {\infty}} \left[ \| \mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {T - t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| _ {\Sigma_ {T - t}} ^ {2} \right]} _ {\mathrm {(c o m p a r a t o r s u b o p t i m a l i t y)}}.
$$

Comparator Suboptimality. We begin with two claims.

Claim F.3.  $\| (K_{\infty} - K_{T - t})\mathbf{x}_t\|_{\Sigma_{T - t}}^2\leq \eta_{T - t}\cdot \mathbf{x}_t^\top R_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{x}.$

Proof. We have that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(K _ {\infty} - K _ {T - t}\right) \mathbf {x} _ {t} \right\| _ {\Sigma_ {T - t}} ^ {2} = \left\| \Sigma_ {T - t} ^ {1 / 2} \left(K _ {\infty} - K _ {T - t}\right) R _ {\mathbf {x}} ^ {- 1 / 2} R _ {\mathbf {x}} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \right\| ^ {2} \\ \leq \left\| \Sigma_ {T - t} ^ {1 / 2} \left(K _ {\infty} - K _ {T - t}\right) R _ {\mathbf {x}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \left\| R _ {\mathbf {x}} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} := \eta_ {T - t} \cdot \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {x}} \mathbf {x} _ {t}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Claim F.4.  $\mathbb{E}_{A,B,K_{\infty}}[\sum_{t = 1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_t^\top R_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{x}_t]\leq T\mathcal{J}_{A,B}^{\star}$

Proof. We have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {A, B, K _ {\infty}} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {x}} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \right] \leq \mathbb {E} _ {A, B, K _ {\infty}} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \left(R _ {\mathbf {x}} + K _ {\infty} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} K _ {\infty}\right) \mathbf {x} _ {t} \right] \\ = \operatorname {t r} \left(\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \sum_ {s = 0} ^ {t} \left(\left(A + B K _ {\infty}\right) ^ {s}\right) ^ {\top} \left(R _ {\mathbf {x}} + K _ {\infty} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} K _ {\infty}\right) \left(\left(A + B K _ {\infty}\right) ^ {s}\right)\right) \\ \leq \operatorname {t r} \left(\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \sum_ {s = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(\left(A + B K _ {\infty}\right) ^ {s}\right) ^ {\top} \left(R _ {\mathbf {x}} + K _ {\infty} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} K _ {\infty}\right) \left(\left(A + B K _ {\infty}\right) ^ {s}\right)\right) \\ = T \operatorname {t r} \left(P _ {\infty} (A, B)\right) = T \mathcal {J} _ {A, B} ^ {\star}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last equalities are by Lemma B.6.

Invoking these two claims, we have

$$
\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \mathbb {E} _ {A, B, K _ {\infty}} \left[ \| \mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {T - t; T} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| _ {\Sigma_ {t; T}} ^ {2} \right] \leq \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \mathbb {E} _ {A, B, K _ {\infty}} \eta_ {t} \left[ \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {x}} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \right] \leq \mathcal {J} _ {A, B} ^ {\star} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \eta_ {T - t} \leq \mathcal {J} _ {A, B} ^ {\star} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {\infty} \eta_ {t}.
$$

Policy Suboptimality. We first make the following claim.

Claim F.5. Let  $(\mathcal{X},\langle \cdot ,\cdot \rangle_{\mathcal{X}})$  denote an inner product space with induced norm  $\| \cdot \|_{\mathcal{X}}$  . Then for any  $x,y\in \mathcal{X}$ $\| x + y\|_{\mathcal{X}}^2\geq \frac{1}{2}\| x\|_{\mathcal{X}}^2 -\| y\|_{\mathcal{X}}^2.$

Proof. We have  $\| x + y \|_{\mathcal{X}}^2 = \| x \|_{\mathcal{X}}^2 + \| y \|_{\mathcal{X}}^2 + 2 \langle x, y \rangle_{\mathcal{X}}$ . Note that, for any  $\alpha > 0$ , we have  $|2 \langle x, y \rangle_{\mathcal{X}}| = |2 \langle \alpha^{1/2} x, \alpha^{-1/2} y \rangle_{\mathcal{X}}| \leq \alpha \| x \|_{\mathcal{X}}^2 + \alpha^{-1} \| y \|_{\mathcal{X}}^2$ . Setting  $\alpha = \frac{1}{2}$ , we have  $|2 \langle x, y \rangle_{\mathcal{X}}| \leq \frac{1}{2} \| x \|_{\mathcal{X}}^2 + 2 \| y \|_{\mathcal{X}}^2$ . Hence  $\| x + y \|_{\mathcal{X}}^2 = \| x \|_{\mathcal{X}}^2 + \| y \|_{\mathcal{X}}^2 + 2 \langle x, y \rangle_{\mathcal{X}} \geq \| x \|_{\mathcal{X}}^2 + \| y \|_{\mathcal{X}}^2 - (\frac{1}{2} \| x \|_{\mathcal{X}}^2 + 2 \| y \|_{\mathcal{X}}^2) = \frac{1}{2} \| x \|_{\mathcal{X}}^2 - \| y \|_{\mathcal{X}}^2$ .

We can now lower bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {A, B, \pi} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \| \mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {T - t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| _ {\Sigma_ {T - t}} ^ {2} \right] \\ \geq \mathbb {E} _ {A, B, \pi} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \| \mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {T - t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| _ {\Sigma_ {T - t}} ^ {2} \right] \\ \geq \mathbb {E} _ {A, B, \pi} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \frac {1}{2} \| \mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {\infty} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| _ {\Sigma_ {T - t}} ^ {2} - \| (K _ {T - t} - K _ {\infty}) \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| _ {\Sigma_ {T - t}} ^ {2} \right] \tag {Claim F.5} \\ \geq \mathbb {E} _ {A, B, \pi} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \frac {1}{2} \| \mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {\infty} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| _ {2} ^ {2} - \| (K _ {T - t} - K _ {\infty}) \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| _ {\Sigma_ {T - t}} ^ {2} \right]. \quad (\Sigma_ {T - t} \succeq R _ {\mathbf {u}} \succeq I) \\ \end{array}
$$

The expression above is equal to

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \frac {1}{2} \mathrm {K - E r r} [ \pi ] - \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \mathbb {E} _ {A, B, \pi} \left[ \| (K _ {T - t} - K _ {\infty}) \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| _ {\Sigma_ {T - t}} ^ {2} \right] \\ \geq \frac {1}{2} \mathrm {K - E r r} [ \pi ] - \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \eta_ {t} \mathbb {E} _ {A, B, \pi} \left[ \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {x}} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \right] (Claim F.3) \\ \geq \frac {1}{2} \mathrm {K - E r r} [ \pi ] - 2 \max  _ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \eta_ {T - t} \mathcal {J} _ {A, B} ^ {\star} (Claim F.6) \\ \geq \frac {1}{2} \mathrm {K - E r r} [ \pi ] - 2 \mathcal {J} _ {A, B} ^ {\star} \max  _ {t \geq T / 2} \eta_ {t}, \quad (\text {C l a i m F . 6}) \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality uses the following claim.

Claim F.6. If  $\mathrm{Regret}_{A,B,T}[\pi] \leq T\mathcal{J}_{A,B}^{\star}$  (in particular, under Assumption 3), then for any  $\tau \leq T$  and  $Q \preceq R_{\mathbf{x}}$ , we have  $\sum_{t=1}^{\tau} \mathbb{E}_{A,B,\pi}\left[\mathbf{x}_t^\top Q\mathbf{x}_t\right] \leq 2T\mathcal{J}_{A,B}^{\star}$ .

The claim is stated for an arbitrary matrix  $Q$  so that it can be specialized where necessary.

Proof. We have  $\sum_{t=1}^{\tau} \mathbb{E}_{A,B,\pi}\left[\mathbf{x}_t^\top Q\mathbf{x}_t\right] \leq \sum_{t=1}^{T} \mathbb{E}_{A,B,\pi}\left[\mathbf{x}_t^\top R_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{x}_t\right] \leq \sum_{t=1}^{T} \mathbb{E}_{A,B,\pi}\left[\mathbf{x}_t^\top R_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{x}_t + \mathbf{u}_t^\top R_{\mathbf{u}}\mathbf{u}\right] = \operatorname{Regret}_{A,B,T}[\pi] + T\mathcal{I}_{A,B}^\star \leq 2T\mathcal{I}_{A,B}^\star.$

Combining the comparator suboptimality and policy suboptimality bounds completes the proof of Lemma F.2.

The next lemma shows that the error sequence  $\eta_t$  has geometric decrease.

Lemma F.7 (Bound on  $\eta_t$ ). Let  $(A, B)$  be stabilizable. Then, for  $\eta_t$  defined above, we have

$$
\eta_ {t} \leq \left(1 + \frac {1}{\nu}\right) ^ {- t}, \quad w h e r e \quad \nu = 2 \| P _ {\infty} (A, B) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \Psi (A, B) ^ {2}.
$$

Proof of Lemma F.7. Since  $R_{\mathbf{x}} \succeq I$

$$
\eta_ {t} \leq \frac {1}{\lambda_ {\min} (R _ {\mathbf {x}})} \bigg \| \Sigma_ {t; T} ^ {1 / 2} (K _ {\infty} - K _ {T - t}) \bigg \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \leq \bigg \| \Sigma_ {t; T} ^ {1 / 2} (K _ {\infty} - K _ {T - t}) \bigg \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}.
$$

Next, observe that from Lemma F.1 we have

$$
\left\| \Sigma_ {T - t} ^ {1 / 2} (K _ {\infty} - K _ {T - t}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} = \sup _ {\| x \| \leq 1} \| (K _ {\infty} - K _ {T - t}) x \| _ {\Sigma_ {T - t}} ^ {2} = \sup _ {\| x \| \leq 1} [ \mathbf {Q} _ {t; T} (x, K _ {\infty} x) - \mathbf {V} _ {T; t} (x) ].
$$

Since  $\mathbf{Q}_{t;T}(x, K_{\infty}x)$  is a finite horizon Q-function for a stationary process with non-negative rewards, we have  $\mathbf{Q}_{t;T}(x,u) \leq \mathbf{Q}_{\infty}(x,u)$ . Therefore, the above is

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \sup  _ {\| x \| \leq 1} \left[ \mathbf {Q} _ {\infty} (x, K _ {\infty} x) - \mathbf {V} _ {T; t} (x) \right] \\ = \sup  _ {\| x \| \leq 1} \left[ \mathbf {V} _ {\infty} (x) - \mathbf {V} _ {T - t} (x) \right] \\ = \sup  _ {\| x \| \leq 1} \left[ x ^ {\top} P _ {\infty} x - x ^ {\top} P _ {t; T} x \right] \\ \mathbf {\eta} = \left\| P _ {\infty} - P _ {T - t} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we use that  $P_{\infty}$  is the value function for the infinite horizon process (Lincoln and Rantzer [2006, Proposition 1]). By reparametrizing, we have verified that

$$
\eta_ {t} \leq \| P _ {\infty} - P _ {t} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}},
$$

To conclude, we apply Lemma F.8, which implies that  $\| P_{\infty} - P_{t;T}\| _2 \leq \left(1 + \frac{1}{\nu}\right)^{-(T - t + 1)}$ , where  $\nu$  is as in the lemma statement:

Lemma F.8 (Dean et al. [2018], Lemma E.6). Consider the Riccati recursion

$$
P _ {t + 1} = R _ {\mathbf {x}} + A ^ {\top} P _ {t} A - A ^ {\top} B P _ {t} (R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P _ {t} B) ^ {- 1} B ^ {\top} P _ {t} A,
$$

where  $R_{\mathbf{x}}$  and  $R_{\mathbf{u}}$  are positive definite and  $P_0 = 0$ . When  $P_{\infty}$  is the unique solution of the DARE, we have

$$
\left\| P _ {t} - P _ {\infty} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \left\| P _ {\infty} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \left(1 + \frac {1}{\nu}\right) ^ {- t}, \tag {F.1}
$$

where  $\nu = 2\| P_{\infty}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot \left(\frac{\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{min}}(R_{\mathbf{x}})}\vee \frac{\|B\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{min}}(R_{\mathbf{u}})}\right)$

We can take  $\nu \leq 2\| P_{\infty}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\max \{\| A\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2},\| B\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\} \leq 2\Psi (A,B)\| P_{\infty}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}$ , as  $R_{\mathbf{x}},R_{\mathbf{u}}\succeq I$

We can now conclude the proof of Lemma 4.3.

Proof of Lemma 4.3. From Lemma F.2, we have the lower bound

$$
\mathrm {R e g r e t} _ {T} [ \pi ; A _ {e}, B _ {e} ] \geq \frac {1}{2} \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e} [ \pi ] - \mathcal {J} _ {e} \left(2 T \left(\max _ {t \geq T / 2} \eta_ {t}\right) + \sum_ {t \geq 0} \eta_ {t}\right),
$$

Recall  $\nu := 2\| P_e\|_{\mathrm{op}}\Psi_e^2$  from Lemma F.7, and that  $\eta_t \leq \| P_e\|_{\mathrm{op}}(1 + \nu^{-1})^t \leq \exp(-t / \nu)$ . Therefore

$$
\sum_ {t \geq 0} \eta_ {t} \leq 2 \| P _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \Psi_ {e} ^ {2},
$$

Hence, if  $T \geq 2\nu \log(2T)$ , we have that

$$
2 T \left(\max  _ {t \geq T / 2} \eta_ {t}\right) \leq \| P _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \| P _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2},
$$

where we use  $P_{\infty}(\cdot ,\cdot)\succeq I$  (Lemma B.5). Hence, for such  $T$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {R e g r e t} _ {T} [ \pi ; A, B ] \geq \frac {1}{2} \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e} [ \pi ] - 3 \| P _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \Psi_ {e} ^ {2} \\ \geq \frac {1}{2} \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e} [ \pi ] - J _ {e} 3 \| P _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \Psi_ {e} ^ {2} \\ \geq \frac{1}{2}\mathrm{K - Err}_{e}[\pi ] - d_{\mathbf{x}}\underbrace{3\|P_{e}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}\Psi_{e}^{2}}_{= \gamma_{e}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Since  $\nu \geq 1$ , the condition  $T \geq 2\nu \log(2T)$  holds as long as  $T \geq c' \nu^2 = c' \| P_e \|_{\mathrm{op}}^2 \Psi_e^4 c'$ . Reparametrizing in terms of  $P_\star, \Psi_\star$  in view of Lemma 4.1 concludes the proof.

# F.2.1 Proof of Lemma F.1

We first recall a standard expression for the value function Bertsekas [2005, Section 4.1]:

$$
\mathbf {V} _ {t} (x) = \| x \| _ {P _ {T - t}} ^ {2} + \sum_ {s = t + 1} ^ {T} \mathrm {t r} (P _ {T - s}).
$$

To obtain the expression for the  $\mathbf{Q}_t$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {Q} _ {t} (x, u) = c (x, u) + \mathbb {E} _ {\mathbf {w} _ {t}} [ \mathbf {V} _ {t + 1} (A x + B u + \mathbf {w} _ {t}) ] \\ = c (x, u) + \left(A x + B u\right) ^ {\top} P _ {T - (t + 1)} (A x + B u) + \mathbb {E} [ \mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {\top} P _ {T - (t + 1)} \mathbf {w} _ {t} ] + \sum_ {s = t + 2} ^ {T} \operatorname {t r} (P _ {T - s}) \\ = c (x, u) + \left(A x + B u\right) ^ {\top} P _ {T - (t + 1)} (A x + B u) + \sum_ {s = t + 1} ^ {T} \operatorname {t r} \left(P _ {T - s}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Note that  $\mathbf{V}_t(x) \coloneqq \min_u \mathbf{Q}_t(x, u)$ , and  $\mathbf{Q}_t(x, u)$  is a quadratic function. We can compute

$$
\begin{array}{l} \arg \min _ {u} \mathbf {Q} _ {t} (x, u) = \arg \min _ {u} c (x, u) + (A x + B u) ^ {\top} P _ {T - (t + 1)} (A x + B u) + \beta_ {t} \\ = \arg \min  _ {u} x ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {x}} x + u ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} u + (A x + B u) ^ {\top} P _ {T - (t + 1)} (A x + B u) + \beta_ {t} \\ = \underset {u} {\arg \min } u ^ {\top} (R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P _ {t + 1; T} B) u + 2 u ^ {\top} B ^ {\top} P _ {T - (t + 1)} A x \\ = - \left(R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B ^ {\top} P _ {T - (t + 1)} B\right) ^ {- 1} B ^ {\top} P _ {t + 1; T} A x \\ = K _ {t; T} x. \\ \end{array}
$$

Moreover, since  $\mathbf{Q}_t(x,u)$  is quadratic in  $u$  with quadratic form  $\Sigma_{T - t} = (R_{\mathbf{u}} + B^{\top}P_{T - (t + 1)}B)$  and the gradient  $\nabla_u\mathbf{Q}_t(x,u)$  vanishes at the minimizer  $u = K_{t;T}x$ , we have

$$
\mathbf {Q} _ {t} (x, u) - \mathbf {V} _ {t} (x) = \mathbf {Q} _ {t} (x, u) - \mathbf {Q} _ {t} (x, K _ {t; T} x) = \| u - K _ {t; T} x \| _ {\Sigma_ {T - t}} ^ {2}.
$$

# F.3 Proof of Lemma 4.4

Again, let us begin proving the lemma for an arbitrary stabilizable  $(A,B)$ , and then specialize to the packing instances  $(A_e,B_e)$ . For a fixed policy  $\pi$ , and let all probabilities and expectations be under the law  $\mathbb{P}_{\pi;A,B}$ . Our strategy follows from Arias-Castro et al. [2012]. Let  $K_{\infty} = K_{\infty}(A,B)$  and let  $\pmb{\delta}_t \coloneqq \mathbf{u}_t - K_{\infty}\mathbf{x}_t$ , and note that  $\mathrm{K - Err}[\pi] = \mathbb{E}_{A,B,\pi}[\sum_{t=1}^{\frac{T}{2}}\|\pmb{\delta}_t\|_2^2]$ . Define the covariance matrix

$$
\mathbf {\Lambda} _ {\frac {T}{2}} := \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\frac {T}{2}} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top}.
$$

For some constant  $c > 0$  to be chosen at the end of the proof, consider a 'thresholded' least squares estimator defined as follows:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \widehat {K} _ {\mathrm {L S}} := \mathbb {I} \left\{\boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {\frac {T}{2}} \succeq c \frac {T}{2} I \right\} \cdot \left(\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\frac {T}{2}} \mathbf {u} _ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {\frac {T}{2}} ^ {- 1} \\ = \mathbb {I} \left\{\boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {\frac {T}{2}} \succeq c \frac {T}{2} I \right\} \left(\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\frac {T}{2}} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {\frac {T}{2}} ^ {- 1} + \mathbb {I} \left\{\boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {\frac {T}{2}} \succeq c \frac {T}{2} I \right\} \left(\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\frac {T}{2}} K _ {\infty} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {\frac {T}{2}} ^ {- 1} \\ = \mathbb {I} \left\{\boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {\frac {T}{2}} \succeq c \frac {T}{2} I \right\} K _ {\infty} + \mathbb {I} \left\{\boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {\frac {T}{2}} \succeq c \frac {T}{2} I \right\} \left(\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\frac {T}{2}} \delta_ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {\frac {T}{2}} ^ {- 1}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Hence, introducing the matrices  $\mathbf{X} := \left[\mathbf{x}_1 \mid \mathbf{x}_2 \mid \ldots \mathbf{x}_{\frac{T}{2}}\right]$ , and  $\pmb{\Delta} := \left[\delta_1 \mid \delta_2 \mid \ldots \delta_{\frac{T}{2}}\right]$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left[ \| \widehat {K} _ {\mathrm {L S}} - K _ {\infty} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \right] = \| K _ {\infty} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \mathbb {P} \left[ \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {\frac {T}{2}} \ngeq c \frac {T}{2} I \right] + \mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbb {I} \left\{\boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {\frac {T}{2}} \succeq c \frac {T}{2} I \right\} \left\| \left(\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\frac {T}{2}} \cdot \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {\frac {T}{2}} ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {F} ^ {2} \right] \\ = \| K _ {\infty} \| _ {F} ^ {2} \mathbb {P} \left[ \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {\frac {T}{2}} \not \succeq c \frac {T}{2} I \right] + \mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbb {I} \left\{\mathbf {X X} ^ {\top} \succeq c \frac {T}{2} I \right\} \| (\boldsymbol {\Delta} \mathbf {X} ^ {\top}) (\mathbf {X X} ^ {\top}) ^ {- 1} \| _ {F} ^ {2} \right] \\ = \| K _ {\infty} \| _ {F} ^ {2} \mathbb {P} \left[ \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {\frac {T}{2}} \ngeq c \frac {T}{2} I \right] + \mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbb {I} \left\{\mathbf {X X} ^ {\top} \succeq c \frac {T}{2} I \right\} \| \boldsymbol {\Delta} \mathbf {X} ^ {\dagger} \| _ {F} ^ {2} \right] \\ \leq \| K _ {\infty} \| _ {F} ^ {2} \mathbb {P} \left[ \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {\frac {T}{2}} \ngeq c \frac {T}{2} I \right] + \mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbb {I} \left\{\mathbf {X X} ^ {\top} \succeq c \frac {T}{2} I \right\} \| \boldsymbol {\Delta} \| _ {F} ^ {2} \| \mathbf {X} ^ {\dagger} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \right] \\ \leq \| K _ {\infty} \| _ {F} ^ {2} \mathbb {P} \left[ \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {\frac {T}{2}} \not \gtrsim c \frac {T}{2} I \right] + \frac {1}{c \frac {T}{2}} \mathbb {E} \left[ \| \boldsymbol {\Delta} \| _ {F} ^ {2} \right] \\ = \| K _ {\infty} \| _ {F} ^ {2} \mathbb {P} \left[ \Lambda_ {\frac {T}{2}} \not \geq c \frac {T}{2} I \right] + \frac {2}{c T} K - E r r [ \pi ] \\ \leq \mathcal {J} _ {\infty} (A, B) \mathbb {P} \left[ \Lambda_ {\frac {T}{2}} \not \simeq c \frac {T}{2} I \right] + \frac {2}{c T} \mathrm {K - E r r} [ \pi ]. \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second-to-last line follows since  $\| \pmb{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2} = \sum_{t = 1}^{\frac{T}{2}}\|\pmb{\delta}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}$ , and the last line uses by Lemma B.8 which bounds  $K_{\infty}^{\top}K_{\infty} \preceq P_{\infty}(A,B)$ , so that  $\| K_{\infty}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2} \leq \operatorname{tr}(P_{\infty}(A,B)) = \mathcal{J}_{A,B}^{\star}$ .

In order to conclude the proof, we need to select show that, for some constant  $c$  sufficiently small and  $\frac{T}{2}$  sufficiently large,  $\mathbb{P}\left[\Lambda_{\frac{T}{2}} \not\simeq c \frac{T}{2} I\right]$  is negligible. Let us now apply Lemma E.4. Let  $\mathcal{F}_t$  denote the filtration generated by  $(\mathbf{x}_s, \mathbf{u}_s)_{s \leq t}$  and  $\mathbf{u}_{t+1}$ . Observe that  $\mathbf{x}_t \mid \mathcal{F}_{t-1} \sim \mathcal{N}(\overline{\mathbf{x}}_t, I)$ , where  $\overline{\mathbf{x}}_t$  is  $\mathcal{F}_{t-1}$ -measurable.

Let us now specialize to an instance  $(A,B) = (A_e,B_e)$ . We can then bound

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \operatorname {t r} \left(\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) \right] \leq \mathbb {E} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {x}} \mathbf {x} _ {t} + \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} \mathbf {u} \right] \leq 2 \mathcal {J} _ {A, B} ^ {\star} T,
$$

by the Assumption 3 and Claim F.6. Hence,  $\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{E}[\Lambda_{T / 2}])\leq \frac{T}{2}\cdot (4\mathcal{J}_{A_e,B_e}^\star)$ . Therefore, if

$$
T \geq \frac {2 0 0 0}{9} \left(2 d _ {\mathbf {x}} \log \frac {1 0 0}{3} + d _ {\mathbf {x}} \log 4 \mathcal {J} _ {A _ {e}, B _ {e}} ^ {\star}\right),
$$

we have that

$$
\mathbb {P} \left[ \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {T / 2} \not \geq \frac {9 (T / 2)}{1 6 0 0} \right] \leq 2 \exp \left(- \frac {9}{2 0 0 0 (d _ {\mathbf {x}} + 1)} T\right).
$$

In particular, there exists a universal constants  $c, c_{\mathrm{LS}}$  such that (recalling  $\mathcal{J}_{A_e,B_e}^\star = \operatorname{tr}(P_\infty(A_e,B_e))$

$$
T \geq c d _ {\mathbf {x}} \log \left(1 + d _ {\mathbf {x}} \| P _ {\infty} \left(A _ {e}, B _ {e}\right) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}\right) \geq c d _ {\mathbf {x}} \log \left(1 + \mathcal {J} _ {A _ {e}, B _ {e}} ^ {\star}\right).
$$

then for a universal constant  $c_{\mathrm{LS}}$  , we have

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \| \widehat {K} _ {\mathrm {L S}} - K _ {\infty} \| _ {F} ^ {2} \right] \leq \gamma_ {\mathrm {l s}} + \frac {1}{T c _ {\mathrm {L S}}} \mathrm {K - E r r} [ \pi ].
$$

Moreover, for  $(A_e, B_e)$ , we can upper bound  $\| P_e\|_{\mathrm{op}} \lesssim \| P_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}}$  (and amend  $c$  accordingly) using Lemma 4.1, concluding the proof.

# F.4 Proof of Lemma 4.5

Let  $\tau = T / 2$ . Recall that our packing consists of systems  $(A_e, B_e)$  indexed by sign-vectors  $e \in \{-1, 1\}^{[n] \times [m]}$ :

$$
(A _ {e}, B _ {e}) := (A _ {\star} - \Delta_ {e} K _ {\star}, B _ {\star} + \Delta_ {e}), \quad \mathrm {w h e r e} \Delta_ {e} = \epsilon \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} e _ {i, j} w _ {i} v _ {j} ^ {\top}.
$$

To keep notation compact, let  $q \coloneqq (i,j)$  denote a stand-in for the double indices  $(i,j)$ , with  $q_1 = i$  and  $q_2 = j$ . Given an indexing vector  $e \in \{-1,1\}^{[n] \times [m]}$ ,  $e^c$  denote the vector consisting of coordinates of  $e$  other than  $(q_1,q_2)$ . For  $a \in \{-1,1\}$ , we set

$$
\Delta_ {a, q, e _ {q} ^ {c}} := \epsilon \left(a e _ {q _ {1}, q _ {2}} + \sum_ {q ^ {\prime} \neq q} e _ {q _ {1} ^ {\prime}, q _ {2} ^ {\prime}} w _ {q _ {1} ^ {\prime}} v _ {q _ {2} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\top}\right).
$$

and define  $A_{a,q,e_q^c}$ ,  $B_{a,q,e_q^c}$  analogously, let  $\mathbb{P}_{a,q,e_q^c}$  denote the law of the first  $\tau = T / 2$  rounds under  $\mathbb{P}_{A_{a,q,e_q^c},B_{a,q,e_q^c},\mathsf{Alg}}[\cdot]$ .

We now consider an indexing vector  $\pmb{e}$  drawn uniformly from  $\{-1,1\}^{[n]\times [m]}$ . We will then let  $\mathbb{P}_{a,q}$  denote the law  $\mathbb{P}_{e_q^c}[\mathbb{P}_{a,q,e_q^c}]$ , marginalizing over the entries  $e_q^c$ . Our proof now follows from the argument in Arias-Castro et al. [2012]. We note then that, for any  $q$  and any  $\widehat{e}$  that depends only on the first  $\tau = T / 2$  time steps, we can bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {e}} \mathbb {E} _ {A _ {\boldsymbol {e}}, B _ {\boldsymbol {e}}} \left[ | \boldsymbol {e} _ {q} - \widehat {e} _ {q} | \right] = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {e} _ {q} \stackrel {\mathrm {u n i f}} {\sim} \{- 1, 1 \}} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {e} _ {q} ^ {c}} \mathbb {E} _ {A _ {\boldsymbol {e} _ {q}, q, \boldsymbol {e} _ {q} ^ {c}}, B _ {\boldsymbol {e} _ {q}, q, \boldsymbol {e} _ {q} ^ {c}}} \left[ | \boldsymbol {e} _ {q} - \widehat {e} _ {q} | \right] \\ = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {e} _ {q} \stackrel {\text {u n i f}} {\sim} \{- 1, 1 \}} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {e} _ {q}, q} \left[ | \boldsymbol {e} _ {q} - \widehat {e} _ {q} | \right] \geq \frac {1}{2} \left(1 - \operatorname {T V} \left(\mathbb {P} _ {- 1, q}, \mathbb {P} _ {1, q}\right)\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Hence, by Cauchy Schwarz,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left[ \sum_ {q} | e _ {q} - \widehat {e} _ {q} | \right] \geq \sum_ {q} \frac {1}{2} (1 - \operatorname {T V} (\mathbb {P} _ {0, q}, \mathbb {P} _ {1, q})) \\ \geq \frac {n m}{2} \sum_ {q} \left(1 - \sqrt {\frac {1}{n m} \sum_ {q} \mathrm {T V} (\mathbb {P} _ {0 , q} , \mathbb {P} _ {1 , q}) ^ {2}}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Moreover, by Jensen's inequality followed by a symmetrized Pinsker's ienquality,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {T V} \left(\mathbb {P} _ {0, q}, \mathbb {P} _ {1, q}\right) ^ {2} \leq \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {e} _ {q} ^ {c}} \left[ \operatorname {T V} \left(\mathbb {P} _ {- 1, q, \boldsymbol {e} _ {q} ^ {c}}, \mathbb {P} _ {1, q, \boldsymbol {e} _ {q} ^ {c}}\right) ^ {2} \right] \\ \leq \frac {1}{2} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {e} _ {q} ^ {c}} \left[ \frac {\mathrm {K L} (\mathbb {P} _ {- 1 , q , \boldsymbol {e} _ {q} ^ {c}} , \mathbb {P} _ {1 , q , \boldsymbol {e} _ {q} ^ {c}})}{2} + \frac {\mathrm {K L} (\mathbb {P} _ {1 , q , \boldsymbol {e} _ {q} ^ {c}} , \mathbb {P} _ {- 1 , q , \boldsymbol {e} _ {q} ^ {c}})}{2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

We now require the following lemma to compute the relevant KL-divergences, which we prove below.

Lemma F.9. Let  $\Delta^{(0)},\Delta^{(1)}\in \mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathbf{x}}\times d_{\mathbf{u}}}$ ,  $\tau \in \mathbb{N}$ , and let  $A_{\star},B_{\star}$  be the nominal systems defined above. For  $i\in \{0,1\}$ , let  $\mathbb{P}_i$  denote the law of the first  $\tau$  iterates under  $\mathbb{P}_{A_\star -\Delta^{(i)}K_\star,B_\star +\Delta^{(i)},\mathsf{Alg}}[\cdot ]$ . Then,

$$
\operatorname {K L} \left(\mathbb {P} _ {0}, \mathbb {P} _ {1}\right) = \frac {1}{2} \operatorname {t r} \left(\left(\Delta^ {(0)} - \Delta^ {(1)}\right) \Lambda_ {\tau} \left(\Delta^ {(0)}\right) \left(\Delta^ {(0)} - \Delta^ {(1)}\right) ^ {\top}\right).
$$

where we have defined the matrix

$$
\Lambda_ {\tau} (\Delta) := \mathbb {E} _ {A _ {\star} - \Delta K _ {\star}, B _ {\star} + \Delta , \mathsf {A l g}} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} \left(\mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {\star} \mathbf {x} _ {t}\right) \left(\mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {\star} \mathbf {x} _ {t}\right) ^ {\top} \right].
$$

We can now compute

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathrm {K L} (\mathbb {P} _ {- 1, q, \boldsymbol {e} _ {q} ^ {c}}, \mathbb {P} _ {1, q, \boldsymbol {e} _ {q} ^ {c}}) = \mathrm {t r} ((\Delta_ {1, q, \boldsymbol {e} _ {q} ^ {c}} - \Delta_ {- 1, q, \boldsymbol {e} _ {q} ^ {c}}) ^ {\top} \Lambda_ {\tau} (\Delta_ {- 1, q, \boldsymbol {e} _ {q} ^ {c}}) (\Delta_ {1, q, \boldsymbol {e} _ {q} ^ {c}} - \Delta_ {- 1, q, \boldsymbol {e} _ {q} ^ {c}})) \\ = 2 \epsilon^ {2} \mathrm {t r} (u _ {q _ {1}} w _ {q _ {2}} ^ {\top} \Lambda_ {\tau} (\Delta_ {- 1, q, e _ {q} ^ {c}}) w _ {q _ {2}} u _ {q _ {1}} ^ {\top}) \\ = 2 \epsilon^ {2} w _ {q _ {2}} ^ {\top} \Lambda_ {\tau} (\Delta_ {- 1, q, e _ {q} ^ {c}}) w _ {q _ {2}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Hence, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathrm {T V} (\mathbb {P} _ {0, q}, \mathbb {P} _ {1, q})) ^ {2} \leq \frac {1}{2} \cdot 2 \epsilon^ {2} w _ {q _ {2}} ^ {\top} \left(\mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {e} _ {q} ^ {c}} \left[ \frac {\Lambda_ {T} (\Delta_ {0 , q , e _ {q} ^ {c}}) + \Lambda_ {T} (\Delta_ {1 , q , e _ {q} ^ {c}})}{2} \right]\right) w _ {q _ {2}} \\ = \epsilon^ {2} w _ {q _ {2}} ^ {\top} \left(\mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {e}} \left[ \Lambda_ {\tau} (\Delta_ {\boldsymbol {e}}) \right]\right) w _ {q _ {2}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Hence, since  $\{w_{j}\}$  for an orthonormal basis,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {q} \mathrm {T V} (\mathbb {P} _ {0, q}, \mathbb {P} _ {1, q})) ^ {2} = \sum_ {q} \epsilon w _ {q _ {2}} ^ {\top} \left(\mathbb {E} _ {e} \left[ \Lambda_ {\tau} (\Delta_ {e}) \right]\right) w _ {q _ {2}} \\ = \epsilon \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {n} w _ {j} ^ {\top} \left(\mathbb {E} _ {e} \left[ \Lambda_ {\tau} (\Delta_ {e}) \right]\right) w _ {j} \\ \leq m \epsilon^ {2} \mathrm {t r} (\mathbb {E} _ {e} [ \Lambda_ {\tau} (\Delta_ {e} ])). \\ \end{array}
$$

We simplify further as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathrm {t r} (\mathbb {E} _ {e} [ \Lambda_ {T} (\Delta_ {e} ]) = \mathbb {E} _ {e} [ \mathrm {t r} (\Lambda_ {T} (\Delta_ {e}) ] \\ = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {e}} \left[ \right. \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbb {E} _ {A _ {\star} - \Delta_ {e} K _ {\star}, B _ {\star} + \Delta_ {e}, \mathrm {A l g}} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} \left(\mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {\star} \mathbf {x} _ {t}\right)\left(\mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {\star} \mathbf {x} _ {t}\right) ^ {\top} \right]\right)\left. \right)\left. \right] \\ = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {e}} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {A _ {e}, B _ {e}, \mathrm {A l g}} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} \operatorname {t r} \left((\mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {\star} \mathbf {x} _ {t}) (\mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {\star} \mathbf {x} _ {t}) ^ {\top}\right) \right] \right] \\ = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {e}} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {A _ {e}, B _ {e}, \mathrm {A l g}} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} \| \mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {\star} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| ^ {2} \right] \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

Therefore, we conclude

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \sum_ {q} | \pmb {e} _ {q} - \widehat {\pmb {e}} _ {q} | \right] \geq \frac {n m}{2} \sum_ {q} \left(1 - \sqrt {\frac {\epsilon^ {2}}{n} \mathbb {E} _ {e} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {A _ {e} , B _ {e} , \mathrm {A l g}} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} \| \mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {\star} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| ^ {2} \right] \right]}\right).
$$

This concludes the proof of the proposition.

# F.4.1 Proof of Lemma F.9

By convexity of KL and Jensen's inequality, one can see that the KL under a randomized algorithm  $\mathsf{Alg}_{\mathrm{rand}}$  is upper bounded by the largest KL divergence attained by one of the deterministic algorithms corresponding to a realization of its random seeds. Hence, we may assume without loss of generality that  $\mathsf{Alg}$  is deterministic.

By first conditioning the performance of  $\mathsf{Alg}$  on its random seed, then integrating the KL combu Note that by we may assume that  $\mathsf{Alg}$  is deterministic. Let  $\mathcal{F}_{t - 1}$  denote the filtration generated by  $(\mathbf{x}_{1:t - 1},\mathbf{u}_{1:t - 1})$

$$
\mathrm {K L} (\mathbb {P} _ {0}, \mathbb {P} _ {1}) = \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} \mathbb {E} _ {A (\Delta^ {(0)}), B (\Delta^ {(0)}), \mathsf {A l g}} [ \mathrm {K L} (\mathbb {P} _ {0} (\mathbf {x} _ {t}, \mathbf {u} _ {t} \mid \mathcal {F} _ {t - 1}), \mathbb {P} _ {\Delta_ {2}, T} (\mathbf {x} _ {t}, \mathbf {u} _ {t} \mid \mathcal {F} _ {t - 1}) ],
$$

where  $\mathbb{P}_0(\mathbf{x}_t,\mathbf{u}_t\mid \mathcal{F}_{t - 1})$  denotes the conditional probability law. Note that  $\mathbf{u}_t$  is deterministic given  $\mathcal{F}_{t - 1}$ . Moreover,  $\mathbf{x}_t\mid \mathcal{F}_{t - 1}$  has the distribution of  $\mathcal{N}((A - \Delta^{(i)}K_{\star})\mathbf{x}_t + (B + \Delta^{(i)})\mathbf{u}_t,I)$  under  $\mathbb{P}_i(\cdot \mid \mathcal{F}_{t - 1})$ . Hence, using the standard formula for Gaussian KL,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathrm {K L} \big (\mathbb {P} _ {i} (\mathbf {x} _ {t}, \mathbf {u} _ {t} \mid \mathcal {F} _ {t - 1}), \mathbb {P} _ {i} (\mathbf {x} _ {t}, \mathbf {u} _ {t} \mid \mathcal {F} _ {t - 1}) \big) \\ = \frac {1}{2} \| (A - \Delta^ {(0)} K _ {\star}) \mathbf {x} _ {t} + (B + \Delta^ {(0)}) - (A - \Delta^ {(1)} K _ {\star}) \mathbf {x} _ {t} + (B + \Delta^ {(1})) \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ = \frac {1}{2} \| (\Delta^ {(0)} - \Delta^ {0}) (\mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {\star} \mathbf {x} _ {t}) \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ = \frac {1}{2} \mathrm {t r} ((\Delta^ {(0)} - \Delta^ {1}) ^ {\top} (\mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {\star} \mathbf {x} _ {t}) (\mathbf {u} _ {t} - K _ {\star} \mathbf {x} _ {t}) ^ {\top} (\Delta^ {(0)} - \Delta^ {1})). \\ \end{array}
$$

The lemma now follows from summing from  $t = 1,\dots ,\tau$  and taking expectations.

# F.5 Proof of Lemma 4.6

We have  $\mathbb{I}(e_{i,j}\neq \widehat{e}_{i,j}(\widehat{K})) = \mathbb{I}(e_{i,j}\widehat{e}_{i,j}(\widehat{K})\neq 1) = \mathbb{I}(e_{i,j}w_i^\top (\widehat{K} -K_\star)v_j\leq 0)$ . Define the Taylor approximation error matrix  $\Delta_{2,e}\coloneqq K_{\star} - (R_{\mathbf{u}} + B_{\star}^{\top}P_{\star}B_{\star})^{-1}(\Delta_{e}A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}P_{\star}) - K_{e}$ . We then have

$$
\begin{array}{l} e _ {i, j} w _ {i} ^ {\top} (\widehat {K} - K _ {\star}) v _ {j} \geq e _ {i, j} w _ {i} ^ {\top} (K _ {e} - K _ {\star}) v _ {j} - | w _ {i} ^ {\top} (\widehat {K} - K _ {\star}) v _ {j} | \\ \geq e _ {i, j} w _ {i} ^ {\top} (R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B _ {\star} ^ {\top} P _ {\star} B _ {\star}) ^ {- 1} (\Delta_ {e} A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star} P _ {\star}) v _ {j} - | w _ {i} ^ {\top} \Delta_ {2, e} v _ {j} | - | w _ {i} ^ {\top} (\widehat {K} - K _ {\star}) v _ {j} | \\ = e _ {i, j} \underbrace {\frac {\sigma_ {j} (A _ {\mathrm {c l} , \star} P _ {\star})}{\sigma_ {i} (R _ {\mathbf {u}} + B _ {\star} ^ {\top} P _ {\star} B _ {\star})}} _ {\leq \nu_ {m}} w _ {i} ^ {\top} \Delta_ {e} v _ {j} - \left(| w _ {i} ^ {\top} \Delta_ {2, e} v _ {j} | + | w _ {i} ^ {\top} (\widehat {K} - K _ {\star}) v _ {j} |\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where we use the definition of  $w_{i}$  and  $v_{j}$ , as less as  $\sigma_{j}(A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}P_{\star})\geq \sigma_{j}(A_{\mathrm{cl},\star})$  since  $P_{\star}\succeq I$ . Since  $\{w_{i^{\prime}}\}$  and  $\{v_{j^{\prime}}\}$  form an orthonormal basis, we have  $w_{i}^{\top}\Delta_{e}v_{j} = w_{i}^{\top}\sum_{i^{\prime} = 1}^{n}\sum_{j^{\prime} = 1}^{m}(\epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}}e_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}w_{i^{\prime}}v_{j^{\prime}}^{\top})v_{j} = \epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}}e_{i,j}$ . Hence,

$$
e _ {i, j} w _ {i} ^ {\top} (\widehat {K} - K _ {\star}) v _ {j} \geq \nu_ {m} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {p a c k}} - \left(| w _ {i} ^ {\top} \Delta_ {2, e} v _ {j} | + | w _ {i} ^ {\top} (\widehat {K} - K _ {\star}) v _ {j} |\right).
$$

It follows that for any  $u\in (0,1)$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {I} \left(e _ {i, j} w _ {i} ^ {\top} (\widehat {K} - K _ {\star}) v _ {j} \leq 0\right) \leq \mathbb {I} \left(| w _ {i} ^ {\top} (\widehat {K} - K _ {\star}) v _ {j} | \geq \sqrt {u} \nu_ {m} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {p a c k}}\right) + \mathbb {I} \left(| w _ {i} ^ {\top} \Delta_ {2, e} v _ {j} | \geq (1 - \sqrt {u}) \nu_ {m} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {p a c k}}\right) \\ \leq \frac {| w _ {i} ^ {\top} (\widehat {K} - K _ {\star}) v _ {j} | ^ {2}}{u \nu_ {m} ^ {2} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {p a c k}} ^ {2}} + \frac {| w _ {i} ^ {\top} \Delta_ {2 , e} v _ {j} |}{(1 - \sqrt {u}) ^ {2} \nu_ {m} ^ {2} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {p a c k}} ^ {2}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Since  $w_{i},v_{j}$  form an orthonormal basis, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} d _ {\mathrm {h a m}} \left(e _ {i, j}, \widehat {e} _ {i, j} (\widehat {K})\right) = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \mathbb {I} \left(e _ {i, j} w _ {i} ^ {\top} \left(\widehat {K} - K _ {\star}\right) v _ {j} \leq 0\right) \\ \leq \frac {\| \widehat {K} - K \| _ {\mathrm {F}}}{u \nu_ {m} ^ {2} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {p a c k}} ^ {2}} + \frac {\| \Delta_ {2 , e} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}}{(1 - \sqrt {u}) ^ {2} \nu_ {m} ^ {2} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {p a c k}} ^ {2}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Finally, since  $\| \Delta_{2,e}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^2\leq (nm)^2\epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}}^4\mathfrak{p}_2(\| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}})^2$  by Lemma 4.1, we have that for  $u = 1 / \sqrt{2}$  and for  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{pack}}^2\leq \frac{1}{20nm}\mathfrak{p}_2(\| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}})\leq \frac{1}{nm} (1 - 1 / \sqrt{2})\sqrt{20} /\mathfrak{p}_2(\| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}})$  that the above is at most

$$
d _ {\mathrm {h a m}} (e _ {i, j}, \widehat {e} _ {i, j} (\widehat {K})) \leq \frac {2 \| \widehat {K} - K \| _ {\mathrm {F}}}{\nu_ {m} ^ {2} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {p a c k}} ^ {2}} - \frac {n m}{2 0}.
$$
