# F.6 Proof of Lemma 4.7

Introduce the shorthand  $\mathrm{K - Err}_e\coloneqq \mathrm{K - Err}_{T / 2}[\pi ;A_e,B_e]$ . We then have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {e}} \mathrm {K} _ {\star} \mathrm {- E r r} _ {\boldsymbol {e}} [ \pi ] = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {e}} \mathbb {E} _ {A _ {\boldsymbol {e}}, B _ {\boldsymbol {e}}, \pi} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \| \mathbf {x} _ {t} - K _ {\star} \mathbf {u} _ {t} \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \leq 2 \mathbb {E} _ {\pmb {e}} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {A _ {\pmb {e}}, B _ {\pmb {e}}, \pi} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \| \mathbf {x} _ {t} - K _ {\pmb {e}, \infty} \mathbf {u} _ {t} \| ^ {2} + \| (K _ {\pmb {e}, \infty} - K _ {\star}) \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| ^ {2} \right] \right] \\ = 2 \mathbb {E} _ {e} \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e} [ \pi ] + 2 \mathbb {E} _ {e} \operatorname {t r} \left(\left(K _ {e, \infty} - K _ {\star}\right) ^ {\top} \mathbb {E} _ {A _ {e}, B _ {e}, \pi} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right] \left(K _ {e, \infty} - K _ {\star}\right)\right) \\ \leq 2 \mathbb {E} _ {e} \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e} [ \pi ] + 2 \left(\max  _ {e} \| K _ {e} - K _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}\right) \cdot \mathbb {E} _ {e} \left\| \mathbb {E} _ {A _ {e}, B _ {e}, \pi} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right] \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq 2 \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {e}} \operatorname {K - E r r} _ {\boldsymbol {e}} [ \pi ] + 4 n m \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3} \epsilon_ {\text {p a c k}} ^ {2} \cdot \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {e}} \left\| \mathbb {E} _ {A _ {\boldsymbol {e}}, B _ {\boldsymbol {e}}, \pi} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right] \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}, \tag {F.2} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality uses Lemma 4.1.

Lemma F.10. Suppose  $\epsilon$  is sufficiently small. Given matrices  $A_{e},B_{e}$  and optimal controller  $K_{e}$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbb {E} _ {A _ {e}, B _ {e}, \pi} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right] \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq (3 / 2) T \| P _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + 2 J _ {e} \| B _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}. \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e} [ \pi ] \\ \leq 2 T \left\| P _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + 3 \mathcal {J} _ {\star} \Psi_ {\star} ^ {2}. \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e} [ \pi ], \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality uses Lemma 4.1.

In particular, note that by Assumption 3 and Lemma 4.3, we have the bound

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {e}} [ \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {\boldsymbol {e}} [ \pi ] ] \leq 2 \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {e}} \mathbb {E} \mathrm {R e g r e t} _ {\boldsymbol {e}} [ \pi ] + \gamma_ {\mathrm {e r r}} \leq 2 \gamma_ {\mathrm {e r r}} T \leq \frac {T}{3 d _ {\mathbf {x}} \Psi_ {\star} ^ {3}}.
$$

Then, noting  $\mathcal{J}_{\star} \leq d_{\mathbf{x}} \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}$ , we can bound  $\mathbb{E}_e \left\| \mathbb{E}_{A_e, B_e, \pi} \left[ \sum_{t=1}^{T/2} \mathbf{x}_t \mathbf{x}_t^\top \right] \right\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq 3T \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}$ . Combining with Eq. (F.2), we have

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {e} \mathrm {K} _ {\star} \mathrm {- E r r} _ {e} [ \pi ] \leq 2 \mathbb {E} _ {e} \mathrm {K} \mathrm {- E r r} _ {e} [ \pi ] + 4 n m T \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {4} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {p a c k}} ^ {2}.
$$

# F.6.1 Proof of Lemma F.10

Let  $\mathbf{x}_t$  denote the sequence induced by playing the algorithm  $\pi$ . Recalling the notation  $\delta_t = \mathbf{u}_t - B_eK_e\mathbf{x}_t$ , we then have

$$
\mathbf {x} _ {t} = A _ {e} \mathbf {x} _ {t - 1} + \mathbf {u} _ {t} + \mathbf {w} _ {t} = (A _ {e} + B _ {e} K _ {e}) \mathbf {x} _ {t - 1} + B _ {e} \pmb {\delta} _ {t} + \mathbf {w} _ {t}. \qquad \mathrm {(F . 3)}
$$

We further define the comparison sequence

$$
\overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} := (A _ {e} + B _ {e} K _ {e}) \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t - 1} + \mathbf {w} _ {t} \qquad \mathrm {(F . 4)}
$$

in which we play the optimal infinite-horizon inputs for  $(A_e, B_e)$ . As shorthand, let  $\mathbb{E}_e[\cdot] := \mathbb{E}_{A_e, B_e, \pi}[\cdot]$ , and recall that  $\mathrm{K-Err}_e := \mathrm{K-Err}_{T/2}[\pi; A_e, B_e]$ . We can bound the desired operator norm of the algorithms

$$
\left\| \mathbb {E} _ {e} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right] \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \left\| \mathbb {E} _ {e} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right] \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \left\| \mathbb {E} _ {e} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} ^ {\top} - \mathbf {x} _ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right] \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}.
$$

It therefore suffices to establish the bounds

$$
\left\| \mathbb {E} _ {e} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right] \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq T \| P _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \tag {F.5}
$$

$$
\left\| \mathbb {E} _ {e} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} ^ {\top} - \mathbf {x} _ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right] \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {1}{2} T \| P _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + 2 J _ {e} \| B _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e}. \tag {F.6}
$$

Let us first prove Equation (F.5). We can compute

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbb {E} _ {e} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right] \right\| \leq \left\| \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \sum_ {s = 0} ^ {- 1} (A _ {e} + B _ {e} K _ {e}) ^ {s} \left((A _ {e} + B _ {e} K _ {e}) ^ {s}\right) ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq \frac {T}{2} \| \mathsf {d l y a p} ((A _ {e} + B _ {e} K _ {e}) ^ {\top}, I) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \frac {T}{2} \| \mathsf {d l y a p} ((A _ {e} + B _ {e} K _ {e}), I) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {T}{2} \| P _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last two steps are by Lemma B.5.

Next, we prove Equation (F.6). By Jensen's inequality, the triangle inequality, and Cauchy-Schwarz, we can bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbb {E} _ {e} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} ^ {\top} - \mathbf {x} _ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right] \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \mathbb {E} _ {e} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \| \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} ^ {\top} - \mathbf {x} _ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \right] \\ \leq \mathbb {E} _ {e} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} 2 \| \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} - \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| \| \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} \| + \| \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} - \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \leq 2 \sqrt {\mathbb {E} _ {e} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \| \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} \| ^ {2} \right]} \sqrt {\mathbb {E} _ {e} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \| \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} - \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| ^ {2} \right]} + \mathbb {E} _ {e} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \| \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} - \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

From Equations (F.3) and (F.4), we have that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} - \mathbf {x} _ {t} = \left(A _ {e} + B _ {e} K _ {e}\right) \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t - 1} + \mathbf {w} _ {t} - \left(\left(A _ {e} + B _ {e} K _ {e}\right) \mathbf {x} _ {t - 1} + B _ {e} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} + \mathbf {w} _ {t}\right) \\ = \left(A _ {e} + B _ {e} K _ {e}\right) \left(\overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t - 1} - \mathbf {x} _ {t - 1}\right) - B _ {e} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \\ = - \sum_ {s = 1} ^ {t} \left(A _ {e} + B _ {e} K _ {e}\right) ^ {t - s} B _ {e} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {s}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Therefore, we have that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \left\| \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} - \mathbf {x} _ {t} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \leq \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \sum_ {s = 1} ^ {t} \left\| A _ {e} + B _ {e} K _ {e}\right) ^ {t - s} B _ {e} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {s} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \leq \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} ^ {\top} \left(B _ {e} ^ {\top} \sum_ {s = 0} ^ {\infty} (A _ {e} + B _ {e} K _ {e}) ^ {s \top} (A _ {e} + B _ {e} K _ {e}) ^ {s}\right) B _ {e} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} ^ {\top} \\ = \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \delta_ {t} ^ {\top} \left(B _ {e} ^ {\top} \mathrm {d l y a p} \left(A _ {e} + B _ {e} K _ {e}, I\right) B _ {e} \delta_ {t} \right. \\ \leq \| B _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \| P _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \| _ {2} ^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we use Lemma B.5 in the last inequality. Taking expectations, we have

$$
\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \| \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} - \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \leq \| B _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \| P _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e}.
$$

This yields

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbb {E} _ {e} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} ^ {\top} - \mathbf {x} _ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right] \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq 2 \sqrt {\mathbb {E} _ {e} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T / 2} \| \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} _ {t} \| ^ {2} \right] \| B _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \| P _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e} + \| B _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \| P _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e}} \\ \leq 2 \sqrt {T / 2 \cdot J _ {e} \| B _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \| P _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e}} + \| B _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \| P _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e} \\ = \sqrt {2 T \cdot J _ {e} \| B _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \| P _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e}} + \| B _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \| P _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where use the bound that  $\sum_{t=1}^{T/2} \mathbb{E}[\|\overline{\mathbf{x}}_t\|^2] \leq (T/2)J_e$  using similar arguments to Lemma F.6. The above can be bounded by

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \frac {1}{2} T \| P _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + J _ {e} \| B _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e} + + \| B _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \| P _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e} \\ \leq \frac {1}{2} T \| P _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + 2 J _ {e} \| B _ {e} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \mathrm {K - E r r} _ {e}, \\ \end{array}
$$

since  $J_{e} = \operatorname{tr}(P_{e})$ .

# F.7 Additional Corollaries of Theorem 1

For scaled identity systems, we can remove the requirement that  $d_{\mathbf{u}} \leq (1 - \Omega(1)) d_{\mathbf{u}}$ .

Corollary 7 (Scaled Identity System). Suppose that  $A_{\star} = (1 - \gamma)I$  for  $\gamma \in (0,1)$ , that  $B_{\star} = U^{\top}$  where  $U$  has orthonormal columns, and  $R_{\mathbf{x}}, R_{\mathbf{u}} = I$ . Then, for  $T \geq c_1\gamma^{-p}\left(d_{\mathbf{u}}d_{\mathbf{x}} \vee \frac{d_{\mathbf{x}}(1 - \gamma)^{-4}}{d_{\mathbf{u}}^2}\right) \vee c_1d_{\mathbf{x}}\log (1 + d_{\mathbf{x}}\gamma^{-1})$ ,

$$
\mathcal {R} _ {A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}, T} \left(\sqrt {d _ {\bf u} ^ {2} d _ {\bf x} / T}\right) \gtrsim \gamma^ {- 4} (1 - \gamma) ^ {2} \sqrt {d _ {\bf u} ^ {2} d _ {\bf x} T}.
$$

Proof of Corollary 7. By the same arguments as in Corollary 1, we have  $\Psi_{\star} \leq 1$  and  $\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq \gamma^{-1}$ . To conclude, let us lower bound  $\sigma_{\min}(A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}) \gtrsim 1 - \gamma$ , which yields  $\nu_{d_{\mathbf{x}}} \gtrsim \frac{1 - \gamma}{\gamma}$ . Reparameterize  $a = (1 - \gamma)$ . Then for  $A_{\star} = aI$  and  $B_{\star} = U^{\top}$ . Then, we can see that the DARE decouples into scalar along the columns of  $U$  and their orthogonal complement. That is, if  $p, k$  is the solution to

$$
(1 - a ^ {2}) p = - p ^ {2} a ^ {2} (1 + p) ^ {- 1} + 1, \quad k = - (1 + p) ^ {- 1} p a, \tag {F.7}
$$

then  $A_{\mathrm{cl},\star} = (A_{\star} - kUU^{\top}) = (a - k)UU^{\top} + a(I - UU^{\top})$  , so that

$$
\sigma_ {\min } \left(A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star}\right) \geq \min  \{a, a - k \} = \min  \left\{a, \frac {a}{1 + p} \right\} = \frac {a}{1 + p}.
$$

To conclude, we solve (F.7) and show that  $p$  is bounded above by a universal constant. For scalar  $(a,b)$ , the solution to the DARE is

$$
(1 - a ^ {2}) p + p ^ {2} (1 - a ^ {2}) = - p ^ {2} a ^ {2} + (1 + p) \quad \text {a n d t h u s} \quad - a ^ {2} p + p ^ {2} - 1 = 0.
$$

The solution  $p$  is then given by

$$
p = \frac {a ^ {2} \pm \sqrt {a ^ {4} + 4}}{2} \leq \frac {1 + \sqrt {5}}{2},
$$

as needed.

# G Proofs for Upper Bound (Section 5)

# G.1 Proof of Lemma 5.1 (Correctness of Perturbations)

On the event  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}}$  of Lemma 5.5, the condition defining  $k_{\mathrm{safe}}$  yields

$$
\left\| \left[ \widehat {A} _ {k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}} - A _ {\star} \mid \widehat {B} _ {k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}} - B _ {\star} \right] \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \leq \operatorname {C o n f} _ {k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}} \leq 1 / 3 C _ {\mathrm {s a f e}} (\widehat {A} _ {k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}}, \widehat {B} _ {k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}}).
$$

By the continuity of  $C_{\mathrm{safe}}$  given by Theorem 11, we then have that, for any  $(\widehat{A}, \widehat{B}) \in B_{\mathrm{safe}}$ ,

$$
\left\| \left[ \widehat {A} - A _ {\star} \mid \widehat {B} - B _ {\star} \right] \right\| _ {\text {o p}} ^ {2} \leq C _ {\text {s a f e}} (A _ {\star}, B _ {\star}).
$$

In particular, the projection step ensures that the above holds for any  $(\widehat{A}_k,\widehat{B}_k)$ . Let us now go point by point. Theorem 5 then implies that

1.  $P_{k} \preceq \frac{21}{20} P_{\star}$ , and thus  $J_{k} \lesssim \mathcal{J}_{\star}$ .  
2.  $\mathcal{J}_k - \mathcal{J}_{\star} = \mathcal{J}_{A_{\star},B_{\star}}[K_{\infty}(\widehat{A}_k,\widehat{B}_k)] - \mathcal{J}_{A_{\star},B_{\star}}^{\star}\leq C_{\mathrm{est}}(A_{\star},B_{\star})\epsilon_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.$  
3. By Lemma B.8,

$$
\| \underbrace {K _ {\infty} (\widehat {A} _ {k} , \widehat {B} _ {k})} _ {:= \widehat {K} _ {k}} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \leq \| \mathrm {d l y a p} (A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} \widehat {K} _ {k}, R _ {\mathbf {x}} + \widehat {K} _ {k} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} \widehat {K} _ {k}) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \| P _ {k} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {2 1}{2 0} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}.
$$

The next two points of the lemma follow from Theorem 8.

For the last point, recall that

$$
\sigma_ {\mathrm {i n}} ^ {2} = \sqrt {d _ {\mathbf {x}}} \| P _ {\infty} (\widehat {A} _ {k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}}, \widehat {B} _ {k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}}) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {9 / 2} \max \{1, \| \widehat {B} _ {k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \} \sqrt {\log \frac {\| P _ {\infty} (\widehat {A} _ {k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}} ,} \widehat {B} _ {k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}}) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\delta}}.
$$

Since  $\mathsf{Conf}_{k_{\mathrm{safe}}}\lesssim 1$ , we have  $\max \{1,\| \widehat{B}_{k_{\mathrm{safe}}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\} \asymp \Psi_{B_\star}$ . Let us show  $\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\asymp \| P_{\infty}(\widehat{A}_{k_{\mathrm{safe}}},\widehat{B}_{k_{\mathrm{safe}}})\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ . By Lemma B.6,  $P_{\infty}(\widehat{A}_{k_{\mathrm{safe}}},\widehat{B}_{k_{\mathrm{safe}}})\preceq P_{k_{\mathrm{safe}}}$ , which is  $\preceq P_{\star}$  by point 1 of this lemma. On the other hand,  $\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim \| P_{\infty}(\widehat{A}_{k_{\mathrm{safe}}},\widehat{B}_{k_{\mathrm{safe}}})\|_{\mathrm{op}}$  by Theorem 11.

# G.2 Proof of Main Regret Decomposition (Lemma 5.2)

We establish Lemma 5.2 by establishing a more general regret decomposition for arbitrary feedback controllers  $K$ , noise-input variances  $\sigma_{u}$ , and control costs  $R_{1}, R_{u}$ . This will allow us to reuse the same computations for similar calculations in the initial estimation phase (Lemma 5.5), and for covariance matrix upper bounds as well.

Definition G.1 (Control Evolution Distribution). We define the law  $\mathcal{D}(K, \sigma_u, x_1)$  to denote the law of the following dynamical system evolution:  $\mathbf{x}_1 = x_1$ , and for  $t \geq 2$ , the system evolves according to the following distribution:

$$
\mathbf {x} _ {t} = A _ {\star} \mathbf {x} _ {t - 1} + \mathbf {w} _ {t}, \quad \mathbf {u} _ {t} = K \mathbf {x} _ {t} + \sigma_ {u} \mathbf {g} _ {t}, \tag {G.1}
$$

where  $\mathbf{w}_t\sim \mathcal{N}(0,I_{d_{\mathbf{x}}})$  and  $\mathbf{g}_t\sim \mathcal{N}(0,I_{d_{\mathbf{u}}})$

We begin with the following characterization, proven in Appendix G.6, of the quadratic forms that will arise in our regret bounds. Note that we use arbitrary cost matrices  $R_{1}, R_{2} \succeq 0$ .

Lemma G.1. Let  $K$  be a stabilizing controller, and let  $(\mathbf{x}_t,\mathbf{u}_t)_{t\geq 1}$  denote the linear dynamical system described by the evolution of the law  $\mathcal{D}(K,\sigma_u,x_1)$ . For cost matrices  $R_{1},R_{2}\succeq 0$ , define the random variable

$$
\operatorname {C o s t} \left(R _ {1}, R _ {2}; x _ {1}, t, \sigma_ {u}\right) := \sum_ {s = 1} ^ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {1} \mathbf {x} _ {t} + \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {2} \mathbf {u} _ {t} = \overline {{\mathbf {g}}} ^ {\top} \Lambda_ {\overline {{\mathbf {g}}}} \overline {{\mathbf {g}}} + x _ {1} ^ {\top} \Lambda_ {\mathbf {x} _ {1}} x _ {1} + 2 \overline {{\mathbf {g}}} ^ {\top} \Lambda_ {\text {c r o s s}} x _ {1}.
$$

Further, define  $R_{K} = R_{1} + K^{\top}R_{2}K$ ,  $A_{K} = A_{\star} + B_{\star}K$ ,  $P_{K} = \mathsf{d}\mathsf{|}\mathsf{y}\mathsf{a}\mathsf{p}(A_{K},R_{K})$ , and  $J_{K} := \operatorname{tr}(P_{K})$ .

1. In expectation, we have

$$
\mathbb {E} [ \mathrm {C o s t} (R _ {1}, R _ {2}; x _ {1}, t, \sigma_ {u}) ] \leq t J _ {K} + 2 \sigma_ {u} ^ {2} t d _ {\mathbf {u}} \left(\| R _ {2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \| B _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \| P _ {K} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}\right) + x _ {1} ^ {\top} P _ {K} x _ {1}
$$

2. Set  $d_{\mathrm{eff}} \coloneqq \min \{d_{\mathbf{u}}, \mathrm{rank}(R_1) + \mathrm{rank}(R_2)\}$ . With a probability  $1 - \delta$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {C o s t} \left(R _ {1}, R _ {2}; x _ {1}, t, \sigma_ {u}\right) \leq t J _ {K} + 2 \sigma_ {u} ^ {2} d _ {\text {e f f}} t \left(\| R _ {2} \| _ {\text {o p}} + \| B _ {\star} \| _ {\text {o p}} ^ {2} \| P _ {K} \| _ {\text {o p}}\right) \\ + \mathcal {O} \left(\sqrt {d t \log \frac {1}{\delta}} + \log \frac {1}{\delta}\right) \left((1 + \sigma_ {u} ^ {2} \| B _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}) \| R _ {K} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| A _ {K} \| _ {\mathcal {H} _ {\infty}} ^ {2} + \sigma_ {u} ^ {2} \| R _ {2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}\right) \\ + 2 x _ {1} ^ {\top} P _ {K} x _ {1}. \\ \end{array}
$$

3. More crudely, we can also bound, with probability  $1 - \delta$

$$
\operatorname {C o s t} \left(R _ {1}, R _ {2}; x _ {1}, t, \sigma_ {u}\right) \lesssim t \log \frac {1}{\delta} \left(J _ {K} + 2 \sigma_ {u} ^ {2} d _ {\text {e f f}} \left(\| R _ {2} \| _ {\text {o p}} + \| B _ {\star} \| _ {\text {o p}} ^ {2} \| P _ {K} \| _ {\text {o p}}\right)\right) + 2 x _ {1} ^ {\top} P _ {K} x _ {1}. \tag {G.2}
$$

Let us now apply the above lemma to our present setting. For  $k \geq k_{\mathrm{safe}}$ , define the terms

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {C o s t} _ {\text {n o i s e}, k} := d _ {\mathbf {u}} \left(\| R _ {\mathbf {u}} \| _ {\text {o p}} + \| B _ {\star} \| _ {\text {o p}} ^ {2} \| P _ {k} \| _ {\text {o p}}\right) \\ \mathsf {C o s t} _ {\mathrm {c o n c}, k} := \left((1 + \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \| B _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}) \| R _ {\mathbf {x}} + \widehat {K} _ {k} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} \widehat {K} _ {k} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| A _ {\mathrm {c l}, k} \| _ {\mathcal {H} _ {\infty}} ^ {2}\right) + \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \| R _ {\mathbf {u}} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

By Lemma G.1 and the fact  $\mathcal{J}_{\star} \leq J_{k}$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}}} ^ {T} (\mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {x}} \mathbf {x} _ {t} + \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} \mathbf {u} _ {t} - \mathcal {I} _ {\star}) \lesssim \sum_ {k = k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}} ^ {k _ {\mathrm {f i n}}} \tau_ {k} (J _ {k} - \mathcal {I} _ {\star}) + \tau_ {k} \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \mathrm {C o s t} _ {\mathrm {n o i s e}, k} \\ + \sum_ {k = k _ {\text {s a f e}}} ^ {k _ {\text {f i n}}} (\sqrt {\tau_ {k} d \log (1 / \delta)} + \log (1 / \delta) \operatorname {C o s t} _ {\text {c o n c}, k} + \sum_ {k = k _ {\text {s a f e}}} ^ {k _ {\text {f i n}}} \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} ^ {\top} P _ {k} \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Let us first bound the  $\mathsf{Cost}_{\mathrm{noise},k}$ -terms. Since  $1 \leq \| P_k\|_{\mathrm{op}} \lesssim \| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}$  on event  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}}$  (Lemma 5.1) and  $\| R_{\mathbf{u}}\|_{\mathrm{op}} = 1$ , we have

$$
\operatorname {C o s t} _ {\text {n o i s e}, k} \leq d _ {\mathbf {u}} \left(\| R _ {\mathbf {u}} \| _ {\text {o p}} + \| B _ {\star} \| _ {\text {o p}} ^ {2} \| P _ {k} \| _ {\text {o p}}\right) \lesssim d _ {\mathbf {u}} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\text {o p}}.
$$

Since  $\sigma_k^2 \leq \sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^2 \tau_k^{-1/2}$  and  $\|P_k\|_{\mathrm{op}} \lesssim \|P_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ , we then obtain

$$
\sum_ {k = k _ {\text {s a f e}}} ^ {k _ {\text {f i n}}} \tau_ {k} \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \mathsf {C o s t} _ {\text {n o i s e}, k} \lesssim \sqrt {T} d _ {\mathbf {u}} \sigma_ {\text {i n}} ^ {2} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\text {o p}}.
$$

Next, let us bound

$$
\mathsf {C o s t} _ {\mathrm {c o n c}, k} := \left((1 + \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \| B _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}) \| R _ {\mathbf {x}} + \widehat {K} _ {k} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} \widehat {K} _ {k} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| A _ {\mathrm {c l}, k} \| _ {\mathcal {H} _ {\infty}} ^ {2}\right) + \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \| R _ {\mathbf {u}} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}.
$$

Observe that  $R_{\mathbf{x}} + \widehat{K}_k^\top R_{\mathbf{u}}\widehat{K}_k \preceq \mathsf{d}\mathsf{lyap}[A_{\mathrm{cl},k},R_{\mathbf{x}} + \widehat{K}_k^\top R_{\mathbf{u}}\widehat{K}_k] = P_k$ . On the good event  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}}$ , we have  $\|P_k\|_{\mathrm{op}} \lesssim \|P_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}}, \|A_{\mathrm{cl},k}\|_{\mathcal{H}_\infty} \lesssim \|A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}\|_{\mathcal{H}_\infty} \leq \|P_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3/2}$  (Lemma 5.1), and by definition,  $\|B_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2 \leq \Psi_{B_\star}^2$ . Thus, the above is at most (again taking  $R_{\mathbf{u}} = I$ )

$$
\mathsf {C o s t} _ {\mathrm {c o n c}, k} \lesssim \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {4} + \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \left(\| R _ {\mathbf {u}} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {4}\right) \leq \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {4} \left(1 + \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \sigma_ {k} ^ {2}\right).
$$

Therefore,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}} ^ {k _ {\mathrm {f i n}}} (\sqrt {\tau_ {k} d \log (1 / \delta)} + \log (1 / \delta)) \mathsf {C o s t} _ {\mathrm {c o n c}, k} \lesssim \sqrt {T d \log (1 / \delta)} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {4} + \log (T) \log (1 / \delta) \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {4} \\ + \sigma_ {\mathrm {i n}} ^ {2} \log (T) \log (1 / \delta) \sqrt {d} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {4}. \\ \leq \sqrt {T d \log (1 / \delta)} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {4} + \log^ {2} \frac {1}{\delta} (1 + \sqrt {d} \sigma_ {\mathrm {i n}} ^ {2} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2}) \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {4}. \\ \end{array}
$$

where we use  $\log (T)\leq \log (1 / \delta)$ . Finally, we have the bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = k _ {\text {s a f e}}} ^ {k _ {\text {f i n}}} \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} ^ {\top} P _ {k} \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} \lesssim \log T \max  _ {k \leq \log T} \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} ^ {\top} P _ {k} \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} \\ \leq \log T _ {\max  _ {k \leq \log T} \| \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \| P _ {k} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}} \\ \lesssim \log T \max  _ {k \leq \log T} \| \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Hence, putting things together, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}}} ^ {T} (\mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {x}} \mathbf {x} _ {t} + \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} \mathbf {u} _ {t} - \mathcal {I} _ {\star}) \lesssim \sum_ {k = k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}} ^ {k _ {\mathrm {f i n}}} \tau_ {k} (J _ {k} - \mathcal {I} _ {\star}) + \log T \max _ {k \leq \log_ {T}} \| \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ + \sqrt {T} \left(d _ {\mathbf {u}} \sigma_ {\mathrm {i n}} ^ {2} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}\right) + \sqrt {d \log (1 / \delta)} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {4}) \\ + \log^ {2} \frac {1}{\delta} (1 + \sqrt {d} \sigma_ {\mathrm {i n}} ^ {2} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2}) \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {4}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Reparameterizing  $\delta \gets \frac{\delta}{6T}$  and taking a union bound preserves the above inequality up to constants (since  $\log T \leq \log \frac{1}{\delta}$ ), and reduces the failure probability across all episodes to  $\delta / 6$ .

# G.3 Bounding the States: Lemma 5.3

Since  $\mathsf{dlyap}[A_{\mathrm{cl},\star}]\coloneqq \mathsf{dlyap}(A_{\mathrm{cl},\star},I)\succeq I$  , we bound the right hand side of

$$
\left\| \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \leq \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} ^ {\top} \mathrm {d l y a p} \left[ A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star} \right] \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}}.
$$

To bound the right hand side, we manipulate the following quantity.

$$
\left\| \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star} ] ^ {1 / 2} \prod_ {i = j} ^ {k - 1} A _ {\mathrm {c l}, i} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \sqrt {\left\| \left(\prod_ {i = j} ^ {k - 1} A _ {\mathrm {c l} , i} ^ {\tau_ {i}}\right) ^ {\top} \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star} ] \left(\prod_ {i = j} ^ {k - 1} A _ {\mathrm {c l} , i} ^ {\tau_ {i}}\right) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}.
$$

By Lemma 5.1, we have that for all  $i \geq k_{\mathrm{safe}}$  on event  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}}$ ,

$$
A _ {\mathrm {c l}, i} ^ {\top} \mathsf {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star} ] A _ {\mathrm {c l}, i} \preceq (1 - \frac {1}{2 \| \mathsf {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l} , \star} ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}) \mathsf {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star} ]
$$

This yields that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star} ] ^ {1 / 2} \prod_ {i = j} ^ {k - 1} A _ {\mathrm {c l}, i} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \sqrt {\left(\prod_ {i = j} ^ {k - 1} \left(1 - \frac {1}{2 \| \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l} , \star} ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}\right) ^ {\tau_ {i}}\right) ^ {2} \| \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star} ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}} \\ = \left(1 - \frac {1}{2 \| \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l} , \star} ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}\right) ^ {\sum_ {i = j} ^ {k - 1} \tau_ {i}} \sqrt {\| \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l} , \star} ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}} \\ = \left(1 - \frac {1}{2 \| \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l} , \star} ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}\right) ^ {\tau_ {k - 1}} \sqrt {\| \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l} , \star} ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

For  $k > k_{\mathrm{safe}}$ , define the vector  $\pmb{e}_k \coloneqq \mathbf{x}_{\tau_k} - A_{\mathrm{cl},k-1}^{\tau_{k-1}} \mathbf{x}_{\tau_{k-1}}$ . Now, we can write

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} = \boldsymbol {e} _ {k} + A _ {\operatorname {c l}, k - 1} ^ {\tau_ {k - 1}} \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k - 1}} \\ = \boldsymbol {e} _ {k} + A _ {\operatorname {c l}, k - 1} ^ {\tau_ {k - 1}} \left(\boldsymbol {e} _ {k - 1} + A _ {\operatorname {c l}, k - 2} ^ {\tau_ {k - 2}} \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k - 2}}\right) \\ = \sum_ {j = k _ {\text {s a f e}} + 1} ^ {k} \left(\prod_ {i = j} ^ {k - 1} A _ {\text {c l}, i} ^ {\tau_ {i}}\right) \boldsymbol {e} _ {j} + \left(\prod_ {i = k _ {\text {s a f e}}} ^ {k - 1} A _ {\text {c l}, i} ^ {\tau_ {i}}\right) \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k _ {\text {s a f e}}}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Thus,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star} ] ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} \| _ {2} \lesssim \max  _ {k _ {\text {s a f e}} \leq j \leq k} \| e _ {j} \| _ {2} \sqrt {\| \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l} , \star} ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}} \left(1 + k \left(1 - \frac {1}{2 \| \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l} , \star} ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}\right) ^ {\tau_ {k - 1}}\right) \\ + \| \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k _ {\text {s a f e}}}} \| _ {2} \sqrt {\| \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l} , \star} ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}. \\ \lesssim \max  _ {k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}} \leq j \leq k} \| e _ {j} \| _ {2} \sqrt {\| d l y \mathsf {a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l} , \star} ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}} \| d l y \mathsf {a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l} , \star} ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \| \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}}} \| _ {2} \sqrt {\| d l y \mathsf {a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l} , \star} ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}} \\ = \max  _ {k _ {\text {s a f e}} \leq j \leq k} \| \boldsymbol {e} _ {j} \| _ {2} \| \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star} ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3 / 2} + \| \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k _ {\text {s a f e}}}} \| _ {2} \sqrt {\| \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l} , \star} ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

where above we have used the inequality  $\max_{k\geq 1}k(1 - \rho)^k\lesssim \frac{1}{\rho}$ , together with  $\tau_k - 1\geq k$ . We begin with the following technical claim:

Lemma G.2. Let  $\mathbf{x}_t$  denote the  $t$ -th iterate from the control law  $\mathcal{D}(K, x_1, \sigma_u)$  (Definition G.1). Then, with probability at least  $1 - \delta$ ,

$$
\left\| \mathbf {x} _ {t} - A _ {K} ^ {t - 1} x _ {1} \right\| \leq \mathcal {O} \left(\sqrt {J _ {K} (1 + \sigma_ {u} ^ {2} \| B _ {\star} \| _ {2} ^ {2}) \log \frac {1}{\delta}}\right).
$$

Since  $\delta < 1 / T$ ,  $\sigma_k^2 \leq 1$ , and  $\mathcal{J}_k \lesssim \mathcal{J}_{\star}$ , a union bound and reparametrization of  $\delta$  implies that, the following holds with probability  $1 - \delta / 8$ :

$$
\forall k \leq k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}: \tau_ {k} \leq T, \| \pmb {e} _ {k} \| _ {2} \lesssim \alpha_ {1} := \sqrt {\mathcal {J} _ {\max} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \log \frac {1}{\delta}} \| \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}}} \| _ {2} \leq \alpha_ {0} \sqrt {\mathcal {J} _ {0} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \log \frac {1}{\delta}}
$$

Concluding, this implies the crude bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} \| _ {2} \leq \sqrt {\mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} ^ {\top} \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l} , \star} ] \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}}} \lesssim \sqrt {\| \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l} , \star} ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}} (\alpha_ {0} + \alpha_ {1} \| \| \mathrm {d l y a p} [ A _ {\mathrm {c l}, \star} ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}) \leq \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3 / 2} (\alpha_ {0} + \alpha_ {1}) \\ \lesssim \sqrt {\Psi_ {B _ {\star}} (\mathcal {J} _ {0} + \mathcal {J} _ {\star}) \log (1 / \delta)} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3 / 2} \\ \lesssim \sqrt {\Psi_ {B _ {\star}} \mathcal {J} _ {0} \log (1 / \delta)} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3 / 2}. \\ \end{array}
$$
