# G.4 Proof of Estimation Bound (Lemma 5.4)

Our strategy is two invoke the two-scale estimation bound Lemma E.3, which we restate here

Lemma E.3 (Two-Scale OLS Estimate). Consider martingale least squares setting of Definition E.1. Let  $\mathsf{P} \in \mathbb{R}^{d \times d}$  denote a projection matrix onto a subspace of dimension  $p$ , and fix an orthonormal basis of  $\mathbb{R}^d$ ,  $v_1, \ldots, v_d$ , such that  $v_1, \ldots, v_p$  form an orthonormal basis for the range of  $\mathsf{P}$ . Further, fix positive constants  $0 < \lambda_1 \leq \nu \leq \lambda_2$  such that  $\nu \leq \sqrt{\lambda_1 \lambda_2 / 2}$ , and define the event

$$
\mathcal {E} := \left\{\Lambda \succeq \lambda_ {1} P + \lambda_ {2} (I - P) \right\} \cap \left\{\| P \Lambda (1 - P) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \nu \right\}
$$

Then, with probability  $1 - \delta$ , if  $\mathcal{E}$  holds,

$$
\| \widehat {\Theta} _ {T} - \Theta_ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \leq \frac {1 2 m p \kappa_ {1}}{\lambda_ {1}} \log \frac {3 m d \kappa_ {1}}{\delta} + \left(\frac {\nu}{\lambda_ {1}}\right) ^ {2} \cdot \frac {4 8 m (d - p) \kappa_ {2}}{\lambda_ {2}} \log \frac {3 m d \kappa_ {2}}{\delta}.
$$

where  $\kappa_{1} := \max_{1 \leq j \leq p} v_{j}^{\top} \Lambda v_{j} / \lambda_{1}$  and  $\kappa_{2} := \max_{p+1 \leq j \leq d} v_{j}^{\top} \Lambda v_{j} / \lambda_{2}$ .

To instantiate the lemma, consider the following orthogonal projection operators:

Definition G.2 (Round-wise projections). Recall  $d = d_{\mathbf{x}} + d_{\mathbf{u}}$ . Given  $v \in \mathbb{R}^d$ , let  $v = (v^{x}, v^{u})$  denote its decomposition along the  $x$  and  $u$  directions. For a given round  $k \geq k_{\mathrm{safe}}$ , let

-  $\mathcal{V}_k\coloneqq \{v\in \mathbb{R}^d:v^x +\widehat{K}_kv^u = 0\}$ , and let  $\mathcal{V}_k^\perp$  denotes it orthogonal complement.  
-  $\mathsf{P}_k$  denote the orthogonal projection onto  $\nu_{k}$ , and let  $\mathsf{P}_k^\perp \coloneqq (I - \mathsf{P}_k)$  denote the projection on  $\nu_{k}^{\perp}$ .  
- Let  $v_{k,1}, \ldots, v_{k,d_{\mathbf{u}}}$  denote an eigenbasis of  $\mathsf{P}_k$ , and  $v_{k,d_{\mathbf{u}} + 1}, \ldots, v_{k,d}$  denote an eigenbasis of  $\mathsf{P}_k^\perp$ . In particular, note that for any  $0 < c_1 < c_2$ ,  $v_{k,1}, \ldots, v_{k,d}$  is an eigenbasis (in descending order) for  $c_1 \mathsf{P}_k + c_2 \mathsf{P}_k^\perp$ .

To apply Lemma E.3, take

$$
m \leftarrow d _ {\mathbf {x}}, \quad p \leftarrow d _ {\mathbf {u}}, \quad d \leftarrow d _ {\mathbf {x}} + d _ {\mathbf {u}}, \quad v _ {i} \leftarrow v _ {i, k}, \quad \boldsymbol {\Lambda} \leftarrow \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k}, \quad \mathrm {P} \leftarrow \mathrm {P} _ {k} \tag {G.3}
$$

Hence, on the event

$$
\mathcal {E} _ {\mathrm {c o v}} (\nu , \alpha_ {1}, \alpha_ {2}, \lambda_ {1}, \lambda_ {2}) := \left\{ \begin{array}{l} \Lambda_ {k} \succeq \lambda_ {1} \mathsf {P} _ {k} + \lambda_ {2} \mathsf {P} \\ \max  _ {1 \leq i \leq d _ {\mathbf {u}}} v _ {i, k} ^ {\top} \Lambda_ {k} v _ {i, k} \leq \kappa_ {1} \lambda_ {1} \\ \max  _ {d _ {\mathbf {u}} + 1 \leq i \leq d} v _ {i, k} ^ {\top} \Lambda_ {k} v _ {i, k} \leq \kappa_ {2} \lambda_ {2} \\ \| \mathsf {P} _ {k} \Lambda_ {k} (I - \mathsf {P} _ {k}) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \nu \end{array} \right\}, \quad \nu \leq \sqrt {\lambda_ {1} \lambda_ {2} / 2}, \tag {G.4}
$$

then, it holds that

$$
\left\| A _ {\star} - \widehat {A} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + \left\| B _ {\star} - \widehat {B} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \lesssim \frac {d _ {\mathbf {x}} d _ {\mathbf {u}} \kappa_ {1}}{\lambda_ {1}} \log (\kappa_ {1} d / \delta) + (\nu / \lambda_ {1}) ^ {2} \frac {d _ {\mathbf {x}} ^ {2} \kappa_ {2}}{\lambda_ {2}} \log (\kappa_ {1} d / \delta), \text {w . p .} 1 - \delta / 2 4 \tag {G.5}
$$

The first step in our bound will be to lower bound the relevant, centered covariances, thereby

Lemma G.3 (Round-wise covariance lower bound). Let  $k \geq k_{\mathrm{safe}} + 1$ , at let  $t \in \{\tau_k, \dots, \tau_{k+1} - 1\}$ . Then, on  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}}$ . If  $\sigma_k^2$  satisfies  $\sigma_k^2 \leq \frac{1}{6.2 \| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}}$ , we have that

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ (\mathbf {z} _ {t} - \mathbb {E} [ \mathbf {z} _ {t} \mid \mathcal {F} _ {t - 1} ]) (\mathbf {z} _ {t} - \mathbb {E} [ \mathbf {z} _ {t} \mid \mathcal {F} _ {t - 1} ]) ^ {\top} \right] \succeq \Gamma_ {k} := \frac {\sigma_ {k} ^ {2}}{6 . 2 \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}} \mathsf {P} _ {k} + \frac {1}{2} \mathsf {P} _ {k} ^ {\perp}.
$$

See Section G.4.1 for the proof. We now convert the above bound into a Löwner lower bound, then conclude by giving an upper bound on  $\tau_{k_{\mathrm{safe}}}$ . We rely on the following guarantee  $\Lambda_{k}$ . To state the bound, we introduce a following shorthand which allows us to abbreviate statements of the form "  $k$  such that  $\tau_{k}$  is sufficiently large".

Definition G.3. We say that the condition  $f(x) \gtrsim_{\star} g(x)$  is met if it holds that  $f \geq Cg$  for a sufficiently large, but unspecified universal constant  $C$ .

Note that  $\gtrsim_{\star}$  differs from  $\gtrsim$  in the following respect:  $f(x)\gtrsim_{\star}g(x)$  may require a large constant say  $C = 1000$ ; in construct,  $f(x)\gtrsim g(x)$  holds whenever  $f(x)\geq cg(x)$  for any constant, even say  $c = 1 / 1000$ . We also define

$$
\tau_ {\mathrm {l s}} := d \max  \left\{1, \frac {d _ {\mathbf {u}}}{d _ {\mathbf {x}}} \right\} \left(\| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3} \mathcal {P} _ {0} + \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 1} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {6}\right) \log \frac {d \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\delta} \tag {G.6}
$$

With this setup in place,

Lemma G.4. . The following bounds hold simultaneously with probability  $1 - \delta / 24$ , if  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{bound}} \cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}}$  holds:

1. For  $\tau_{k} \gtrsim_{\star} \sqrt{\log(d / \delta)}$ , we have

$$
\max  _ {i \in \{1, \dots , d _ {\mathbf {u}} \}} v _ {k, i} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k} v _ {k, i} \lesssim \tau_ {k} \sigma_ {k} ^ {2}
$$

2. Suppose that  $\tau_{k}\geq \| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}\mathcal{J}_{0}\vee \Psi_{B_{\star}}^{4}\sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^{4}$ . Then,

$$
\max  _ {i \in [ d ]} v _ {k, i} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k} v _ {k, i} \lesssim \tau_ {k} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \log (d / \delta).
$$

3. If  $\tau_{k} \gtrsim \tau_{\mathrm{ls}}$ , then the above two conditions hold,  $\sigma_{k}^{2}$  satisfies the conditions of Lemma G.3 and  $\Lambda_{k} \succeq c\tau_{k}\Gamma_{k}$  for some universal constant  $c > 0$ .

The proof is deferred to Appendix G.4.2. Next, we [ms: text and sign post] Appendix G.4.3

Lemma G.5. Suppose that  $\tau_{k} \geq \tau_{\mathrm{ls}}$ . Then, with probability  $1 - \delta / 8$ , the following upper bound holds on the events  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}} \cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{bound}}$ :

$$
\left\| \mathrm {P} _ {k} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k} (I - \mathrm {P} _ {k}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \nu_ {k} := C _ {\nu} \tau_ {k} \sigma_ {k} ^ {2}
$$

where  $C_\nu$  is a universal constant.

In light of the above lemmas, we see that the event  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{cov}}$  in Equation (G.4) holds with probability  $1 - 2\delta /24$  with the substitutions

$$
\kappa_ {1} \lesssim \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}, \quad \kappa_ {2} \lesssim \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \log (d / \delta)
$$

$$
\nu_ {k} \leftarrow C _ {\nu} \tau_ {k} \sigma_ {k} ^ {2}, \quad \lambda_ {1} \leftarrow \frac {\tau_ {k} \sigma_ {k} ^ {2}}{6 . 2 \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}} \quad \lambda_ {2} = \frac {1}{2} \tau_ {k}
$$

One can verify that for  $\tau_{k} \gtrsim \tau_{\mathrm{ls}}$ , we have  $\nu_{k} \leq \sqrt{\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}/2}$ . Hence, applying Equation (G.5), it holds with probability  $1 - \delta/8$  (on  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}} \cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{bound}}$ ), it holds that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| A _ {\star} - \widehat {A} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + \left\| B _ {\star} - \widehat {B} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \\ \lesssim \frac {\left(d _ {\mathbf {x}} d _ {\mathbf {u}}\right) \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}}{\tau_ {k} \sigma_ {k} ^ {2}} \log (d \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} / \delta) + \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3} \frac {d _ {\mathbf {x}} ^ {2}}{\tau_ {k}} \log (d \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} / \delta) ^ {2}, \\ \lesssim \frac {d _ {\mathbf {x}} d _ {\mathbf {u}} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}}{\sigma_ {\mathrm {i n}} ^ {2} \tau_ {k} ^ {1 / 2}} \log \left(\frac {d \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\delta}\right) + \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3} \frac {d _ {\mathbf {x}} ^ {2}}{\tau_ {k}} \log \left(\frac {d \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\delta}\right) ^ {2}, \tag {G.7} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last line uses  $\tau_k\sigma_k^2 = \sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^2\sqrt{\tau_k}$  for  $\tau_{k}\gtrsim_{\star}\tau_{\mathrm{ls}}$  (see Lemma 5.1) and  $1 / \delta \geq T\geq \tau_k\gtrsim_\star \tau_{\mathrm{ls}}$ .

# G.4.1 Proof of Lemma G.3

Define

$$
\Sigma_ {k} = \left[ \begin{array}{c} I _ {d _ {\mathbf {x}}} \\ \widehat {K} _ {k} ^ {\top} \end{array} \right] \left[ \begin{array}{c c} I _ {d _ {\mathbf {x}}} & \widehat {K} _ {k} \end{array} \right] + \left[ \begin{array}{c c} 0 & 0 \\ 0 & \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} I \end{array} \right] = \left[ \begin{array}{c c} I _ {d _ {\mathbf {x}}} & \widehat {K} _ {k} \\ \widehat {K} ^ {\top} & \widehat {K} _ {k} ^ {\top} \widehat {K} _ {k} + \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} I \end{array} \right].
$$

We see that for  $k \geq k_0 + 1$  and  $t \geq \tau_k$ , we have that  $\mathbf{z}_t \mid \mathcal{F}_{t-1} \sim \mathcal{N}(\overline{\mathbf{z}}_t, \Sigma_k)$ , where  $\overline{\mathbf{z}}_t$  and  $\Sigma_t$  are  $\mathcal{F}_{t-1}$ -measurable. Our goal will now be to lower bound  $\Sigma_k \asymp \gamma_1 \mathsf{P}_k + \gamma_2 \mathsf{P}_k^\perp$ . To this end, let  $v \in S^{d-1}$  and write  $v = \mathsf{P}_k v + \mathsf{P}_k^\perp v := v_\parallel + v_\perp$ . Observe then that

$$
\left| v _ {\perp} ^ {\top} \Sigma_ {k} v _ {\parallel} \right| \leq \left| v _ {\perp} ^ {\top} \left[ \begin{array}{c} I _ {d _ {\mathbf {x}}} \\ \widehat {K} _ {k} ^ {\top} \end{array} \right] \left[ \begin{array}{c c} I _ {d _ {\mathbf {x}}} & \widehat {K} _ {k} \end{array} \right] v _ {\parallel} \right| + \left| v _ {\perp} ^ {\top} \left[ \begin{array}{c c} 0 & 0 \\ 0 & \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} I \end{array} \right] v _ {\parallel} \right| = \left| v _ {\perp} ^ {\top} \left[ \begin{array}{c c} 0 & 0 \\ 0 & \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} I \end{array} \right] v _ {\parallel} \right| \leq \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \| v _ {\perp} \| \| v _ {\parallel} \|,
$$

where we use that  $\left[ \begin{array}{cc}I_{d_{\mathbf{x}}} & \widehat{K}_k \end{array} \right]v_{\parallel} = 0$ . On the other hand, since  $v_{\perp}\in \mathsf{null}\left(\left[ \begin{array}{cc}I_{d_{\mathbf{x}}} & \widehat{K}_k \end{array} \right]\right)^{\perp}$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} v _ {\perp} ^ {\top} \Sigma_ {k} v _ {\perp} \geq v _ {\perp} ^ {\top} \left[ \begin{array}{c} I _ {d _ {\mathbf {x}}} \\ \widehat {K} _ {k} ^ {\top} \end{array} \right] \left[ \begin{array}{c c} I _ {d _ {\mathbf {x}}} & \widehat {K} _ {k} \end{array} \right] v _ {\perp} \\ = \| \left[ \begin{array}{c c} I _ {d _ {\mathbf {x}}} & \widehat {K} _ {k} \end{array} \right] v _ {\perp} \| ^ {2} \\ \geq \| v _ {\perp} \| ^ {2} \sigma_ {d _ {\mathbf {x}}} \left(\left[ \begin{array}{c c} I _ {d _ {\mathbf {x}}} & \widehat {K} _ {k} \end{array} \right]\right) ^ {2} = \| v _ {\perp} \| ^ {2} \lambda_ {\min } (I _ {d _ {\mathbf {x}}} + \widehat {K} _ {k} ^ {\top} \widehat {K} _ {k}) \geq \| v _ {\perp} \| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

We can therefore bound, for any  $\alpha > 0$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} v ^ {\top} \Sigma_ {k} v = v _ {\perp} ^ {\top} \Sigma_ {k} v _ {\perp} + 2 v _ {\perp} ^ {\top} \Sigma_ {k} v _ {\parallel} + v _ {\parallel} ^ {\top} \Sigma_ {k} v _ {\parallel} \\ \geq \left\| v _ {\perp} \right\| ^ {2} - 2 \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \left\| v _ {\perp} \right\| \left\| v _ {\parallel} \right\| + \lambda_ {\min } (\Sigma_ {k}) \left\| v _ {\perp} \right\| ^ {2} \\ \geq \| v _ {\perp} \| ^ {2} - \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} (\alpha \| v _ {\parallel} \| ^ {2} + \frac {1}{\alpha} \| v _ {\parallel} \| ^ {2}) + \lambda_ {\min } (\Sigma_ {k}) \| v _ {\perp} \| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Taking  $\alpha = \lambda_{\mathrm{min}}(\Sigma_k) / 2\sigma_k^2$  we have

$$
v ^ {\top} \Sigma_ {k} v \geq \| v _ {\perp} \| ^ {2} \underbrace {(1 - \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \cdot \frac {2 \sigma_ {k} ^ {2}}{\lambda_ {\operatorname* {m i n}} (\Sigma_ {k})})} _ {:= \gamma_ {1}} + \underbrace {\frac {1}{2} \lambda_ {\operatorname* {m i n}} (\Sigma_ {k})} _ {:= \gamma_ {2}} \| v _ {\perp} \| ^ {2}.
$$

Hence, we have show that, for  $\gamma_1,\gamma_2$  defined in the above display,  $\Sigma_{k}\succeq \gamma_{1}\mathsf{P}_{k}^{\perp} + \gamma_{2}\mathsf{P}_{k}$ . Let us now lower bound each of these quantities. From Dean et al. [2018, Lemma F.6], since  $\| \widehat{K}_k\| ^2\lesssim \| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}$  (Lemma 5.1), and  $\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq 1\geq \sigma_k^2$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lambda_ {\min } (\Sigma_ {k}) \geq \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \min  \left\{\frac {1}{2}, \frac {1}{2 \| \widehat {K} _ {k} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} + \sigma_ {k} ^ {2}} \right\} \geq \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \min  \left\{\frac {1}{2}, \frac {1}{2 . 1 \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \sigma_ {k} ^ {2}} \right\} \\ \geq \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \min  \left\{\frac {1}{2}, \frac {1}{3 . 1 \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}} \right\} = \frac {\sigma_ {k} ^ {2}}{6 . 2 \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Hence, for  $\sigma_k^2 \leq \frac{1}{6.2\|P_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}}}$ , we have  $\gamma_1 \geq \frac{1}{2}$ , and  $\gamma_2 \geq \frac{\sigma_k^2}{3.1\|P_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}}}$ .

# G.4.2 Proof of Lemma G.11

Proof. All union bounds will be absorbed into  $\delta$  factors, as  $\delta \leq 1 / T$  and  $T \geq d$ . We decompose  $v_{k,i} = v_{k,i}^{x} + v_{k,i}^{u}$  along its  $x$  and  $u$  coordinate. It suffices to show that each bound holds individually with probability  $1 - \delta$  for a fixed  $i$ , and  $k$ , since the union bound over  $k$  can be absorbed into the  $\delta$  factor (as  $\delta \leq 1 / T$ ), and dimension addressed by reparametrizing  $\delta \gets \delta / d$ .

Point 1: For  $i \in \{1, \dots, d_{\mathbf{u}}\}$ ,  $v_{k,i}$  lies in the vector space  $\nu_{k}$ . Therefore

$$
\begin{array}{l} v _ {k, i} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k} v _ {k, i} = \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {2 \tau_ {k} - 1} v _ {k, i} ^ {\top} \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {u} _ {t} \end{array} \right] \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {u} _ {t} \end{array} \right] ^ {\top} v _ {k, i} \\ = \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {2 \tau_ {k} - 1} v _ {k, i} ^ {\top} \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \\ \widehat {K} _ {k} \mathbf {x} _ {t} + \sigma_ {k} \mathbf {g} _ {t} \end{array} \right] \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \\ \widehat {K} _ {k} \mathbf {x} _ {t} + \sigma_ {k} \mathbf {g} _ {t} \end{array} \right] ^ {\top} v _ {k, i} \\ = \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {2 \tau_ {k} - 1} v _ {i} ^ {\top} \left[ \begin{array}{c} \\ \sigma_ {k} \mathbf {g} _ {t} \end{array} \right] \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {x} _ {t} + \sigma_ {k} \mathbf {g} _ {t} \end{array} \right] ^ {\top} v _ {k, i} \\ = \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {2 \tau_ {k} - 1} \left\langle v _ {k, i} ^ {u}, \mathbf {g} _ {t} \right\rangle^ {2} \sim \| v _ {k, i} ^ {u} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \cdot \chi^ {2} (\tau_ {k}). \\ \end{array}
$$

By standard  $\chi^2$ -concentration, the above is  $\lesssim \tau_k\| v_{k,i}^u\| _2^2\sigma_k^2\leq \tau_k\sigma_k^2$  for  $\tau_{k}\geq \sqrt{\log(1 / \delta)}$

Point 2: For arbitrary  $i$ , set  $R_{1} \coloneqq v_{k,i}^{x}(v_{k,i}^{x})^{\top}$  and  $R_{2} \coloneqq v_{k,i}^{u}(v_{k,u}^{u})^{\top}$ .

$$
v _ {k, i} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k} v _ {k, i} = \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {2 \tau_ {k} - 1} v _ {k, i} ^ {\top} \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {u} _ {t} \end{array} \right] \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {u} _ {t} \end{array} \right] ^ {\top} v _ {k, i} \leq 2 \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {2 \tau_ {k} - 1} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {1} \mathbf {x} _ {1} + \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\top} R _ {2} \mathbf {u} _ {t}.
$$

Thus, Lemma G.1 ensures that, with probability  $1 - \delta$ , we have that for the matrix  $P := \mathrm{dlyap}(A_{\star} + B_{\star}\widehat{K}_k,R_1 + \widehat{K}_k^\top R_2\widehat{K}_k)$ ,

$$
v _ {k, i} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k} v _ {k, i} \lesssim \tau_ {k} \log \frac {1}{\delta} \left(\mathrm {t r} (P) + 2 \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} d _ {\mathrm {e f f}} \left(\| R _ {2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \| B _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}\right)\right) + \| P \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} \| ^ {2},
$$

where  $d_{\mathrm{eff}} \leq \mathrm{rank}(R_1) + \mathrm{rank}(R_2) = 2$ . Since  $R_1 \preceq I \preceq R_{\mathbf{x}}$  and  $R_2 \preceq I = R_{\mathbf{u}}$ , we have  $R_1 + \widehat{K}_k^\top R_2 \widehat{K}_k \preceq R_{\mathbf{x}} + \widehat{K}_k^\top R_{\mathbf{u}} \widehat{K}_k$ , and thus (by Lemma B.5),  $P = \mathsf{d}\mathsf{l}\mathsf{y}\mathsf{a}\mathsf{p}(A_\star + B_\star \widehat{K}_k, R_1 + \widehat{K}_k^\top R_2 \widehat{K}_k) \preceq$

$\mathsf{dlyap}(A_{\star} + B_{\star}\widehat{K}_k,R_{\mathbf{x}} + \widehat{K}_k^\top R_{\mathbf{u}}\widehat{K}_k) = P_k$  . Moerover Lemma 5.1, we get  $\| P_k\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim \| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}$  . Moreover, since  $P$  can be shown to have rank at most 2,  $\operatorname {tr}(P)\lesssim \| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}$  . Finally,  $\| x_{\tau_k}\| _2\leq \sqrt{\mathcal{I}_0\log(1 / \delta)}\| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3 / 2}$  from Lemma G.3,

$$
\begin{array}{l} v _ {k, i} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k} v _ {k, i} \lesssim \tau_ {k} \log \frac {1}{\delta} \left(\| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \left(1 + \| B _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}\right)\right) + \mathcal {J} _ {0} \log (1 / \delta) \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {4} \\ \lesssim \tau_ {k} \log \frac {1}{\delta} \left(\| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2}\right) + \mathcal {J} _ {0} \log (1 / \delta) \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {4}. \\ \end{array}
$$

In particular, if  $\tau_{k} \geq \mathcal{J}_{0}\|P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}$ , and  $\sigma_{k} \leq 1 / \Psi_{B_{\star}}^{2}$  (for which it suffices  $\tau_{k} \leq \sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^{4}\Psi_{B_{\star}}^{4}$ ), we have

$$
v _ {k, i} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k} v _ {k, i} \lesssim \tau_ {k} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \log (1 / \delta).
$$

Point 3: Suppose now that  $\tau_{k} \geq \| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}\mathcal{J}_{0}\log (1 / \delta) \vee \Psi_{B_{\star}}^{4}\sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^{4}$ . Then, by using the expectation bound statement of Lemma G.1, and summing over indices  $i$ , we have

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \operatorname {t r} \left(\boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k}\right) \cap \mathcal {E} _ {\text {b o u n d}} \cap \mathcal {E} _ {\text {s a f e}} \right] \lesssim d \tau_ {k} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}.
$$

Hence, by Lemma E.4, if

$$
\tau_ {k} \gtrsim_ {\star} d \log \left\{\frac {d \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\sigma_ {\min} (\Gamma_ {k})} \right\}.
$$

then with probability  $1 - e^{-\tau_k / d}$  on  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{bound}}\cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}}$  , we have that  $\Lambda_{k}\gtrsim \tau_{k}\Gamma_{k}$  . Note that since  $\tau_{k}\geq \| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3}\mathcal{J}_{0}\log (1 / \delta)\geq d\log (1 / \delta)$  (since  $\mathcal{J}_0\geq d$  and  $\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq 1$  ), we have also  $1 - e^{-\tau_k / d}\geq 1 - \delta$

Now, if in addition  $\tau_{k} \gtrsim_{\star} \sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^{4} \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}$ , Lemma G.4.1 entails that  $\Gamma_{k} \gtrsim \frac{\sigma_{k}^{2}}{\|P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}} = \frac{\sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^{2}}{\sqrt{\tau_{k}} \|P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}}$  (note that for such  $k$ ,  $\sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^{2} / \sqrt{\tau_{k}} \leq 1$ ). With a few simplifications, we see then that if

$$
\tau_ {k} \gtrsim_ {\star} d \log \tau_ {k} + d \log \left\{\frac {d \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{1 \wedge \sigma_ {\mathrm {i n}} ^ {2}} \right\} \vee \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3} \mathcal {J} _ {0} \log (1 / \delta) \vee \sigma_ {\mathrm {i n}} ^ {4} (\max \{\Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {4}, \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \}),
$$

then with probability  $1 - \mathcal{O}(\delta)$ ,  $\Lambda_k \gtrsim \tau_k \Gamma_k$ . Since  $\tau_k \gtrsim_\star d \log \tau_k$  for  $\tau_k \gtrsim d \log d$ , we need simply  $\tau_k \gtrsim_\star d \log \left\{\frac{d \| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}}{1 \wedge \sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^2}\right\} \vee \| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}^3 \mathcal{J}_0 \log (1 / \delta) \vee \sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^4 (\max \{\Psi_{B_\star}^4, \| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}^2\})$  to ensure  $\Lambda_k \gtrsim \tau_k \Gamma_k$  with probability  $1 - \mathcal{O}(\delta)$ . Shrinking  $\delta$  by a constant reduces the failure probability to  $1 - \delta$ . Lastly, using  $\sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^2 \geq 1$  by definition, and  $\sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^2 \lesssim \sqrt{d_{\mathbf{x}}} \| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{9/2} \Psi_{B_\star} \sqrt{\log \frac{\| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}}{\delta}}$  by Lemma 5.1, we can bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} d \log \left\{\frac {d \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{1 \wedge \sigma_ {\mathrm {i n}} ^ {2}} \right\} \vee \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3} \mathcal {J} _ {0} \log (1 / \delta) \vee \sigma_ {\mathrm {i n}} ^ {4} (\Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {4} \vee \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}) \\ \lesssim d \log d \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3} \mathcal {J} _ {0} \log (1 / \delta) + d _ {\mathbf {x}} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {9} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \log \frac {\| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\delta} (\Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {4} \vee \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}) \\ \lesssim d \left(\| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3} \mathcal {P} _ {0} + \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 1} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {6}\right) \log \frac {d \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\delta} := \tau_ {\mathrm {l s}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

where in the last line we use  $\Psi_{B_\star}, \| P_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}} \geq 1$ ,  $d_{\mathbf{x}} \leq d$ , and  $\mathcal{P}_0 = \mathcal{J}_0 / d_{\mathbf{x}}$ .

# G.4.3 Proof of Lemma G.5

Consider a fixed epoch  $k$ , and for  $t \in \{\tau_k, \tau_k + 1, \dots, \tau_{k+1} - 1\}$ , introduce the contribution from all previous steps:

$$
\mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} := \mathbb {E} [ \mathbf {x} _ {t} \mid \mathcal {F} _ {\tau_ {k}} ] = A _ {\mathrm {c l}, k} ^ {t - \tau_ {k}} \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} l
$$

where  $(\mathcal{F}_t)$  is the filtration generated by  $\mathbf{x}_{1:t-1}, \mathbf{w}_{1:t-1}, \mathbf{u}_{1:t-1}$ , and where the equality follows from direct computation. Further, introduce the difference

$$
\check {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} := \mathbf {x} _ {t} - \mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t}, \tag {G.8}
$$

The important observation here is

Observation G.1.  $\check{\mathbf{x}}_t$  is a linear form in the jointly standard-normal Gaussian vector  $\overline{\mathbf{g}} := (\mathbf{w}_t, \mathbf{g}_t : \tau_k \leq t \leq \tau_{k+1} - 1)$ .

We then have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathsf {P} _ {k} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k} (I - \mathsf {P} _ {k}) = \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \mathsf {P} _ {k} \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {u} _ {t} \end{array} \right] \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {u} _ {t} \end{array} \right] ^ {\top} (I - \mathsf {P} _ {k}) \\ = \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \mathsf {P} _ {k} \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \\ \widehat {K} _ {k} \mathbf {x} _ {t} + \sigma_ {k} \mathbf {g} _ {t} \end{array} \right] \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \\ \widehat {K} _ {k} \mathbf {x} _ {t} + \sigma_ {k} \mathbf {g} _ {t} \end{array} \right] ^ {\top} (I - \mathsf {P} _ {k}) \\ \stackrel {(i)} {=} \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \mathsf {P} _ {k} \left[ \begin{array}{c} 0 \\ \sigma_ {k} \mathbf {g} _ {t} \end{array} \right] \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \\ \widehat {K} _ {k} \mathbf {x} _ {t} + \sigma_ {k} \mathbf {g} _ {t} \end{array} \right] ^ {\top} (I - \mathsf {P} _ {k}) \\ \stackrel {(i i)} {=} \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \mathsf {P} _ {k} \left[ \begin{array}{c} 0 \\ \sigma_ {k} \mathbf {g} _ {t} \end{array} \right] \left[ \begin{array}{c} \breve {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} \\ \widehat {K} _ {k} \breve {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} \end{array} \right] ^ {\top} (I - \mathsf {P} _ {k}) + \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \mathsf {P} _ {k} \left[ \begin{array}{c} 0 \\ \sigma_ {k} \mathbf {g} _ {t} \end{array} \right] \left[ \begin{array}{c} \breve {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} \\ \widerel {\triangledown} K _ {k} \breve {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} \end{array} \right] ^ {\top} (I - \mathsf {P} _ {k}) \\ + \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \mathsf {P} _ {k} \left[ \begin{array}{c c} 0 & 0 \\ 0 & \mathbf {g} _ {t} \mathbf {g} _ {t} ^ {\top} \end{array} \right] (I - \mathsf {P} _ {k}), \\ \end{array}
$$

where here  $(i)$  follows since  $\mathsf{P}_k$  annihilates the subspace  $(x,u):u = Kx$ , and where  $(ii)$  decomposes using linearity. Since  $\| \mathsf{P}_k\|_{\mathrm{op}}\vee \| I - \mathsf{P}_k\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1$ , we can upper bound, we bound the operator norm of  $\| \mathsf{P}_k\pmb {\Lambda}_k(I - \mathsf{P}_k)\|_{\mathrm{op}}$  by three terms:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathsf {P} _ {k} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k} (I - \mathsf {P} _ {k}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \\ \sigma_ {k} \underbrace {\left\| \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \mathbf {g} _ {t} \left[ \check {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} \mid \hat {K} _ {k} \check {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} \right] \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}} _ {\text {t e r m} _ {1}} + \sigma_ {k} \underbrace {\left\| \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \mathbf {g} _ {t} \left[ \overset {\circ} {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} \mid \widehat {K} _ {k} \overset {\circ} {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} \right] \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}} _ {\text {t e r m} _ {2}} + \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \underbrace {\left\| \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \mathbf {g} _ {t} \mathbf {g} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}} _ {\text {t e r m} _ {3}}. \tag {G.9} \\ \end{array}
$$

Let us bound each term in order;

# Bounding term

Definition G.4 (Relevant  $\epsilon$ -nets). Let  $\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{left}}$  be an 1/4-net of the unit ball in  $\mathbb{R}_{\mathbf{u}}^{d}$ , and let  $\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{right}}$  be a 1/4-net of the unit ball in the subspace  $\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathbf{x}} + d_{\mathbf{u}}}$  spanned by  $(I - \mathsf{P}_k)$ , that is, spanned by vectors of the form  $(x,\widehat{K}_kx)$ . We use  $w$  to denote elements of  $\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{left}}$ , and  $v = (v_{1},v_{2})$  to denote elements of this net  $\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{right}}$ , partitioned into the  $\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathbf{x}}}$  and  $\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathbf{u}}}$  components.

A standard covering argument entails that

$$
\operatorname {term} _ {1} \lesssim \max  _ {w \in \mathcal {T} _ {\text {l e f t}}, v = (v _ {1}, v _ {2}) \in \mathcal {T} _ {\text {r i g h t}}} \operatorname {term} _ {1} (w, v) \quad \text {w h e r e} \operatorname {term} _ {1} (w, v) := \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \langle w, \mathbf {g} _ {t} \rangle \langle (\check {\mathbf {x}} _ {t}, \widehat {K} _ {k} \check {\mathbf {x}} _ {t}), v \rangle .
$$

From Observation G.1,  $\mathsf{term}_1(w,v)$  is a quadratic form in a Gaussian vector  $\overline{\mathbf{g}} = ((\mathbf{w}_t,\mathbf{g}_t):\tau_k\leq t\leq$ $\tau_{k + 1} - 1)$  encoding both input noise  $\mathbf{g}_t$  and process noise  $\mathbf{w}_t$ , and thus may express that for some matrix  $Q(w,v)$ , which without loss of generality we can choose to be symmetric, for which

$$
\operatorname {t e r m} _ {1} (w, v) = \overline {{\mathbf {g}}} ^ {\top} Q (w, v) \overline {{\mathbf {g}}}
$$

When clear from context, we abridge  $Q = Q(w,v)$ .

We shall apply the Hanson Wright inequality, which requires bounding  $\operatorname{tr}(Q)$ ,  $\|Q\|_{\mathrm{F}}$ , and  $\|Q\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ . We first see that  $\operatorname{tr}(Q) = 0$ , since  $\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{term}_1(w,v)] = 0$ , as the noise  $\mathbf{g}_t$  is independent of  $\check{\mathbf{x}}_t$ . Moreover, we can bound  $\|Q\|_{\mathrm{F}} \leq \sqrt{\operatorname{rank}(Q)} \|Q\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ . To bound  $\operatorname{rank}(Q)$ :

Claim G.6.  $\mathrm{rank}(Q)\leq \tau_k$  , and thus  $\| Q\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq \sqrt{\tau_k}\| Q\|_{\mathrm{op}}$

Proof of Claim G.6. Let  $X_{t}:\mathbb{R}^{\dim (\overline{\mathbf{g}})}\to \mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathbf{u}}}$  denote the projection matrix yielding  $X_{t}\overline{\mathbf{g}} = \mathbf{g}_{t}$ , and let  $Y_{t}\coloneqq \mathbb{R}^{\dim (\overline{\mathbf{g}})}\to \mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathbf{x}} + d_{\mathbf{u}}}$  denote the linear operator  $Y_{t}\overline{\mathbf{g}} = (\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{t},\widehat{K}_{k}\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{t})$ ; these are indeed linear by Observation G.1, namely that  $\dot{\mathbf{x}}_t = \mathbf{x}_t - \mathring{\mathbf{x}}_t$  is linear in the noise vector  $\overline{\mathbf{g}}$ .

Let  $\bar{X} \coloneqq X_{t}$  denote the matrix whose rows are  $X_{\tau_k}, \ldots, X_{\tau_{k+1}-1}$ , and let  $\bar{Y}$  be analogous. Then,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \overline {{\mathbf {g}}} ^ {\top} Q (w, v) \overline {{\mathbf {g}}} = \operatorname {t e r m} _ {1} (w, v) \\ = \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \mathbf {g} _ {t} ^ {\top} w v ^ {\top} \left(\check {\mathbf {x}} _ {t}, \widehat {K} _ {k} \check {\mathbf {x}} _ {t}\right), v = \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1}} \overline {{\mathbf {g}}} ^ {\top} X _ {t} ^ {\top} w v ^ {\top} Y _ {t} \overline {{\mathbf {g}}} \\ = \overline {{\mathbf {g}}} \bar {X} \mathrm {D i a g} _ {\tau_ {k} (w v ^ {\top})} \bar {Y} \overline {{\mathbf {g}}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\mathrm{Diag}_{\tau_k(wv^\top)}$  denotes the block-diagonal matrix consisting of  $\tau_{k}$  blocks of the rank-one terms  $w v^{\top}$ . Hence, the quadratic form  $Q$  can be rendered explicitly as  $Q = \bar{X}\mathrm{Diag}_{\tau_k}(wv^\top)\bar{Y}$ , which has rank at most  $\mathrm{rank}(\mathrm{Diag}_{\tau_k}(wv^\top)) = \tau_k\cdot \mathrm{rank}(wv^\top) = \tau_k$ .

Next, we bound  $\| Q\|_{\mathrm{op}}$

Claim G.7.  $\| Q\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim \| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{5 / 4}$

Proof. We construct a matrix  $\bar{Q} \succeq 0$  such that  $-\bar{Q} \preceq Q \preceq \bar{Q}$ . It follows that  $\|Q\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq \bar{Q}$ . Let us continue by constructing  $\bar{Q}$ . For any parameter  $\alpha > 0$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathsf {t e r m} _ {1} (w, v) := \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \langle w, \mathbf {g} _ {t} \rangle \langle (\check {\mathbf {x}} _ {t}, \widehat {K} _ {k} \check {\mathbf {x}} _ {t}), v \rangle \leq \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \frac {\alpha}{2} \mathbf {g} _ {t} ^ {\top} w w ^ {\top} \mathbf {g} _ {t} + \frac {1}{2 \alpha} (\check {\mathbf {x}} _ {t}, \widehat {K} _ {k} \check {\mathbf {x}} _ {t}) ^ {\top} v v ^ {\top} (\check {\mathbf {x}} _ {t}, \widehat {K} _ {k} \check {\mathbf {x}} _ {t}) ^ {\top} \\ \stackrel {(i)} {\leq} \frac {\alpha}{2} \underbrace {\sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \mathbf {g} _ {t} ^ {\top} w w ^ {\top} \mathbf {g} _ {t}} _ {:= \overline {{\mathbf {g}}} ^ {\top} \bar {Q} _ {1} (w, v) \overline {{\mathbf {g}}}} + \frac {1}{\alpha} \underbrace {\sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \check {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} ^ {\top} (v _ {1} v _ {1} ^ {\top} + \widehat {K} _ {k} v _ {2} v _ {2} \widehat {K} _ {k} ^ {\top}) \check {\mathbf {x}} _ {t}} _ {:= \overline {{\mathbf {g}}} ^ {\top} \bar {Q} _ {2} (w, v) \overline {{\mathbf {g}}}} \\ \stackrel {(i i)} {:=} \overline {{\mathbf {g}}} ^ {\top} \bar {Q} (w, v) \overline {{\mathbf {g}}} \\ \end{array}
$$

where in  $(i)$ , we use the decompose  $v = (v_{1}, v_{2})$  as in Definition G.4, and use the elementary inequality  $vv^{\top} \preceq \left[ \begin{array}{cc} v_{1}v_{1}^{\top} & 0 \\ 0 & v_{2}v_{2}^{\top} \end{array} \right]$ . In  $(ii)$ , we again invoke that  $\check{\mathbf{x}}_t$  is linear in  $\overline{\mathbf{g}}$  (Observation G.1).

Since the above inequality holds for any realization of the noise, we find that  $Q(w,v) \preceq \bar{Q}(w,v)$ , and a similar manipulation shows that  $Q(w,v) \succeq -\bar{Q}(w,v)$ . Noting that  $\| Q(w,v) \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq$

$\| \bar{Q} (w,v)\|_{\mathrm{op}} = \| \frac{\alpha}{2}\bar{Q}_1(w,v) + \frac{1}{\alpha}\bar{Q}_2(w,v)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq \frac{\alpha}{2}\| \bar{Q}_1(w,v)\|_{\mathrm{op}} + \frac{1}{\alpha}\| \bar{Q}_2(w,v)\|_{\mathrm{op}},$  we bound  $\| \bar{Q}_1(w,v)\|_{\mathrm{op}}$  and  $\| \bar{Q}_2(w,v)\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ . We have first that, deterministically,

$$
\overline {{\mathbf {g}}} ^ {\top} \bar {Q} _ {1} (w, v) \overline {{\mathbf {g}}} = \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \mathbf {g} _ {t} ^ {\top} w w ^ {\top} \mathbf {g} _ {t} \leq \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \| \mathbf {g} _ {t} \| ^ {2} \leq \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \| \mathbf {g} _ {t} \| ^ {2} + \| \mathbf {w} _ {t} \| ^ {2} = \| \overline {{\mathbf {g}}} \| ^ {2},
$$

so that  $\| \bar{Q}_1(w,v)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1$ . Moreover, by applying Lemma G.12 with  $x_{1} = 0$ ,  $R_{2} = 0$ ,  $\sigma_u = \sigma_k$ ,  $R_{1} = v_{1}v_{1}^{\top} + \widehat{K}_{k}v_{2}v_{2}\widehat{K}_{k}^{\top}$ , we can bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \bar {Q} _ {2} (w, v) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \lesssim \left(1 + \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \| B _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}\right) \| A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} \widehat {K} _ {k} \| _ {\mathcal {H} _ {\infty}} \| R _ {1} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \lesssim (1 + \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \| B _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}) \| A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} \widehat {K} _ {k} \| _ {\mathcal {H} _ {\infty}} (1 + \| \widehat {K} _ {k} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}). \\ \end{array}
$$

In particular, for  $\sigma_k \leq 1 / \Psi_{B_\star}^2$ , we have  $(1 + \sigma_k^2 \| B_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}^2) \lesssim 1$ , and and under the safe event  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}}$ , we have  $\| \widehat{K}_k \|_{\mathrm{op}}^2 \leq \frac{21}{20} \| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}$  and  $\| A_\star + B_\star \widehat{K}_k \|_{\mathcal{H}_\infty} \coloneqq \| A_{\mathrm{cl},k} \|_{\mathcal{H}_\infty} \leq \| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3/2}$ . Since  $\| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}} \geq 1$ , this allows us to bound

$$
\| \bar {Q} _ {2} (w, v) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \lesssim \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {5 / 2},
$$

provided again that  $\sigma_k \leq \Psi_{B_\star}$ . Hence, setting  $\alpha = \| P_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{5/4}$ ,

$$
\| Q (w, v) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {\alpha}{2} \| \bar {Q} _ {1} (w, v) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \frac {1}{\alpha} \| \bar {Q} _ {2} (w, v) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \lesssim \frac {\alpha}{2} + \frac {1}{\alpha} \| P _ {\star} ^ {5 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \lesssim \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {5 / 4}.
$$

![](images/bab24eea68437b8805098f42e748910353677c8f7fc4b876b7211e554dc49445.jpg)

Gathering the past two claims, we have  $\| Q\|_{\mathrm{op}} \lesssim \| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{5 / 4}$  and  $\| Q\|_{\mathrm{F}} \lesssim \sqrt{\tau_k}\| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{5 / 4}$ . Hence, the Hanson-Wright Inequality implies that, for some universal constant  $c > 0$ ,

$$
\mathbb {P} \left[ \mathbf {t e r m} _ {1} (w, v) \geq c \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {5 / 4} \left(\sqrt {\tau_ {k}} \log \left(1 / \delta_ {0}\right) + \log \left(1 / \delta_ {0}\right)\right) \right] \leq \delta_ {0}.
$$

Finally, taking a union bound over  $w \in \mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{left}}$  and  $v \in \mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{right}}$ , and noting that we may choose  $\log |\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{left}}||\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{right}}| \lesssim d_{\mathbf{x}} + d_{\mathbf{u}}$ , we have for  $\tau_{k+1} \geq d_{\mathbf{x}} + d_{\mathbf{u}} + \log(1 / \delta_0)$  and another universal constant  $c$  that

$$
\mathbb {P} \left[ \sup  _ {v \in \mathcal {T} _ {\text {l e f t}}, w \in \mathcal {T} _ {\text {r i g h t}}} \operatorname {t e r m} _ {1} (w, v) \geq c \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {5 / 4} \sqrt {\tau_ {k} \left(d _ {\mathbf {x}} + d _ {\mathbf {u}} + \log \left(1 / \delta_ {0}\right)\right)} \right] \leq \delta .
$$

Finally, by a standard bound on the cardinality of the minimal-size nets  $\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{left}}$  and  $\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{right}}$  (see e.g. ?, Section 4.2), this implies that for another constant  $c$ ,

$$
\mathbb {P} \left[ \operatorname {t e r m} _ {1} \geq c \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {5 / 4} \sqrt {\tau_ {k} \left(d _ {\mathbf {x}} + d _ {\mathbf {u}} + \log \left(1 / \delta_ {0}\right)\right)} \right] \leq \delta_ {0}.
$$

Bounding term2 Again, we use a covering argument. with  $\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{left}}$  be an 1/4-net of the unit ball in  $\mathbb{R}_{\mathbf{u}}^{d}$ , and let  $\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{right}}$  be a 1/4-net of the unit ball in the subspace  $\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathbf{x}} + d_{\mathbf{u}}}$  spanned by  $(I - \mathsf{P}_k)$ . Similarly, use

$$
\operatorname {term} _ {2} \lesssim \max  _ {w \in \mathcal {T} _ {\text {l e f t}}, v = (v _ {1}, v _ {2}) \in \mathcal {T} _ {\text {r i g h t}}} \operatorname {term} _ {2} (w, v) \quad \text {w h e r e} \operatorname {term} _ {2} (w, v) := \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \langle w, \mathbf {g} _ {t} \rangle \langle (\mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t}, \widehat {K} _ {k} \mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t}), v \rangle
$$

Since  $\mathring{\mathbf{x}}_t = A_{\mathrm{cl},k}^{t - \tau_k}\mathbf{x}_{\tau_k}$ , we see that  $(\mathbf{g}_t:t\geq \tau_k)$  are independent of the terms  $\langle (\mathbf{\dot{x}}_t,\widehat{K}_k\mathbf{\dot{x}}_t),v\rangle$ . Since  $\| v\| = 1$ ,  $\langle w,\mathbf{g}_t\rangle$  are then independent standard Gaussian random vectors. Hence, by standard Gaussian tail bound,

$$
\mathsf {t e r m} _ {2} (w, v) \leq \sqrt {2 \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {t} \langle (\mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} , \widehat {K} _ {k} \mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t}) , v \rangle^ {2} \log (1 / \delta_ {0})}, \mathrm {w . p .} 1 - \delta_ {0}).
$$

We can then bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {t} \langle (\mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t}, \widehat {K} _ {k} \mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t}), v \rangle^ {2} = v ^ {\top} \left[ \begin{array}{c} I \\ \widehat {K} _ {k} \end{array} \right] ^ {\top} \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} \mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} ^ {\top} \left[ \begin{array}{c} I \\ \widehat {K} _ {k} \end{array} \right] v \leq \left\| \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} \mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| v \| ^ {2} \left\| \left[ \begin{array}{c} I \\ \widehat {K} _ {k} \end{array} \right] \right\| ^ {2} \\ = \left(1 + \| \widehat {K} _ {k} \| ^ {2}\right) \left\| \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} \mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \left(1 + \| \widehat {K} _ {k} \| ^ {2}\right) \operatorname {t r} \left(\sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} \mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Furthermore, since  $\mathring{\mathbf{x}}_t = A_{\mathrm{cl},k}^{t - \tau_{k + 1}}\mathbf{x}_{\tau_k}$ , the linearity of trace yields

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {t r} \left(\sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} \mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) = \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} ^ {\top} \left(\sum_ {i = 0} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - \tau_ {k}} \left(A _ {\mathrm {c l}, k} ^ {i}\right) ^ {\top} \left(A _ {\mathrm {c l}, k} ^ {i}\right)\right) \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} ^ {\top} \\ \leq \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} ^ {\top} \left(\sum_ {i = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(A _ {\mathrm {c l}, k} ^ {i}\right) ^ {\top} \left(A _ {\mathrm {c l}, k} ^ {i}\right)\right) \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} ^ {\top} \\ = \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} ^ {\top} \mathrm {d l y a p} (A _ {\mathrm {c l}, k}, I) \mathbf {x} _ {\tau_ {k}} \leq (\sqrt {\Psi_ {B _ {\star}} \mathcal {J} _ {0} \log (1 / \delta)} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3 / 2}) ^ {2} \text {o n e v e n t} \mathcal {E} _ {\text {b o u n d}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the above uses Lemma 5.3. Moreover, on the event  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}}$ , from Lemma 5.1,  $\| P_k\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim \| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}$  and  $\| \widehat{K}_k\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2\lesssim \| P_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}$ . Hence, on  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{bound}}\cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}}$

$$
\mathrm {t r} (\sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} \mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {1 / 2} \lesssim \sqrt {\Psi_ {B _ {\star}} \mathcal {J} _ {0} \log (1 / \delta)} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {3 / 2}, (1 + \| \widehat {K} _ {k} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}) ^ {1 / 2} \lesssim \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {1 / 2}
$$

Hence, combining the above bounds

$$
\left(\sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {t} \langle (\mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t}, \widehat {K} _ {k} \mathring {\mathbf {x}} _ {t}), v \rangle^ {2}\right) ^ {1 / 2} \lesssim \sqrt {\Psi_ {B _ {\star}} \mathcal {J} _ {0} \log (1 / \delta)} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}
$$

and thus, on  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}}\cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{bound}}$  , the following holds with probability  $1 - \delta_0$  ..

$$
\mathsf {t e r m} _ {2} (w, v) \leq \sqrt {\Psi_ {B _ {\star}} \mathcal {J} _ {0} \log (1 / \delta) \log (1 / \delta_ {0})} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}
$$

Again, using a union bound over a standard cardinality bound on  $\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{left}}$  and  $\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{right}}$ , we obtain that with probability  $1 - \delta_0$

$$
\mathsf {t e r m} _ {2} \lesssim \max  _ {w \in \mathcal {T} _ {\text {l e f t}}, v = (v _ {1}, v _ {2}) \in \mathcal {T} _ {\text {r i g h t}}} \mathsf {t e r m} _ {2} (w, v) \leq \sqrt {\Psi_ {B _ {\star}} \mathcal {J} _ {0} \log (1 / \delta) (d _ {\mathbf {x}} + d _ {\mathbf {u}} + \log (1 / \delta_ {0}))} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\text {o p}} ^ {2}.
$$

Bounding term3 Recall that

$$
\mathsf {t e r m} _ {3} = \left\| \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {\tau_ {k + 1} - 1} \mathbf {g} _ {t} \mathbf {g} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}
$$

Observe that  $\mathbf{g}_t$  are independent and isotropic Gaussian random vectors. Noting that  $\tau_{k + 1} = 2\tau_k$ , a standard operator norm bound for Gaussian matrices,  $?$ , Theorem 5.39 yields that, with probability  $1 - \delta_0$ ,

$$
\mathsf {t e r m} _ {3} \lesssim (\sqrt {\tau_ {k}} + \sqrt {d _ {\mathbf {u}} + \log (1 / \delta_ {0})}) ^ {2}
$$

In particular, for  $\tau_k \geq d_{\mathbf{u}} + \log(1 / \delta_0)$ ,  $\mathrm{term}_3 \lesssim \sqrt{\tau_k}$ .

Combining the bounds Combining the above bounds (and shrinking  $\delta_0$  to absorb constants into union bounds as necessary), it holds that for  $\tau_k \geq d_{\mathbf{u}} + d_{\mathbf{x}} + \log(1 / \delta_0)$ , we have that with probability  $1 - \delta_0$ ,

$$
\mathsf {t e r m} _ {1} \lesssim \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {5 / 4} \sqrt {\tau_ {k} (d _ {\mathbf {x}} + d _ {\mathbf {u}} + \log (1 / \delta_ {0}))}
$$

$$
\mathrm {t e r m} _ {2} \lesssim \sqrt {\Psi_ {B _ {\star}} \mathcal {J} _ {0} \log (1 / \delta) (d _ {\mathbf {x}} + d _ {\mathbf {u}} + \log (1 / \delta_ {0}))} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}.
$$

$$
\mathrm {t e r m} _ {3} \lesssim \tau_ {k}
$$

In particular, if in addition it holds that  $\tau_{k} \geq \Psi_{B_{\star}} \mathcal{J}_0 \| P_{\star} \|_{\mathrm{op}}^{3/2} \log(1/\delta)$ , we find

$$
\mathsf {t e r m} _ {1} + \mathsf {t e r m} _ {2} \lesssim \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {5 / 4} \sqrt {\tau_ {k} (d _ {\mathbf {x}} + d _ {\mathbf {u}} + \log (1 / \delta_ {0}))}.
$$

Hence, from Equation (G.9), under the above conditions on  $\tau_{k}$ , and on  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}} \cap \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{bound}}$ , we have that, with probability  $1 - \delta_0$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathrm {P} _ {k} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k} (I - \mathrm {P} _ {k}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \sigma_ {k} (\text {t e r m} _ {1} + \text {t e r m} _ {2}) + \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \text {t e r m} _ {2} \\ \lesssim \sigma_ {k} \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {5 / 4} \sqrt {\tau_ {k} (d _ {\mathbf {x}} + d _ {\mathbf {u}} + \log (1 / \delta_ {0}))} + \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \tau_ {k}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Setting  $\delta_0 = \delta /16$  and absorbing gives

$$
\| \mathsf {P} _ {k} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k} (I - \mathsf {P} _ {k}) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \lesssim \left(\sqrt {\tau_ {k} \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \left(\| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {5 / 2} (d + \log (1 / \delta)) + \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \tau_ {k}\right)}\right),
$$

Note that our stipulated conditions on  $\tau_{k}$  hold for  $\tau_{k} \gtrsim_{\star} \tau_{\mathrm{ls}}$ . Moreover, when this holds, we have

$$
\sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \tau_ {k} \geq \min \{\tau_ {k}, \sigma_ {\mathrm {i n}} ^ {2} \tau_ {k} ^ {- 1 / 2} \} \geq \min \{\tau_ {\mathrm {l s}}, \sqrt {d _ {\mathbf {x}} \tau_ {\mathrm {l s}}} \} \geq \| P _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {5 / 2} (d + \log (1 / \delta))
$$

so that

$$
\left\| \mathsf {P} _ {k} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k} (I - \mathsf {P} _ {k}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \lesssim \tau_ {k} \sigma_ {k} ^ {2}
$$

# G.5 Proof of Lemma 5.5 ( $k < k_{\mathrm{safe}}$ )

We analyze the rounds  $k < k_{\mathrm{safe}}$ , which correspond to the rounds before the least-squares procedure produces a sufficiently close approximation to  $(A_{\star}, B_{\star})$  that we can safely implement certainty equivalent control.

In order to avoid directly conditioning on events  $\{k_{\mathrm{safe}} \leq (\ldots)\}$ , let us define the sequence  $\mathbf{z}_{t,0} := (\mathbf{x}_{t,0}, \mathbf{u}_{t,0})$  on the same probability space as  $(\mathbf{x}_t, \mathbf{u}_t)$  to denote the system driven by the same noise  $\mathbf{w}_t$ , and with the same random perturbations  $\mathbf{g}_t$ , but where the evolution is with respect to the dynamics

$$
\mathbf {x} _ {t, 0} = A _ {\star} + B _ {\star} \mathbf {u} _ {t, 0} \quad \mathbf {u} _ {t, 0} = K _ {0} \mathbf {x} _ {t, 0} + \mathbf {g} _ {t},
$$

that is, the dynamics defined by the distribution  $\mathcal{D}(K_0,\sigma_u^2 = 1,x_1 = 0)$ . Observe that, for any  $t < \tau_{k_{\mathrm{safe}}}$ , it holds that  $\mathbf{x}_{t,0} = \mathbf{x}_t$  and  $\mathbf{u}_{t,0} = \mathbf{u}_t$ , so it will suffice to reason about this sequence.

Proof that  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}}$  holds As above, to reason rigorously about probabilities, we introduce  $\widehat{A}_{k,0}$ ,  $\widehat{B}_{k,0}$  as the OLS estimators on the  $\mathbf{z}_{k,0} \coloneqq (\mathbf{x}_{k,0}, \mathbf{u}_{k,0})$  sequence, and define the covariance matrix

$$
\boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k, 0} := \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k}} ^ {2 \tau_ {k} - 1} \mathbf {z} _ {k, 0} \mathbf {z} _ {k, 0} ^ {\top}.
$$

We also define the induced confidence term:

$$
\mathsf {C o n f} _ {k, 0} = 6 \lambda_ {\min } (\boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k, 0}) ^ {- 1} \Big (d \log 5 + \log \left\{\frac {4 k ^ {2} \det (3 (\boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k , 0})}{\delta} \right\} \Big).
$$

Lemma G.8. The following event holds with probability  $1 - \delta$ :

$$
\mathcal {E} _ {\mathrm {c o n f}} := \left\{\forall k \leq k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}} w i t h \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k} \succeq I, \left\| \left[ \widehat {A} _ {k} - A _ {\star} \mid \widehat {B} _ {k} - B _ {\star} \right] \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \leq \mathsf {C o n f} _ {k} \right\}.
$$

Proof. Applying (E.1) in Lemma E.2 with  $\Lambda_0 = I$ , we see that for any fixed  $k$  for which  $\Lambda_{k,0} \succeq I$ ,  $\mathsf{Conf}_{k,0}$  is a valid  $\delta / 4k^2$ -confidence interval; that is  $\| [A_\star - \widehat{A}_{k,0} | B_\star - \widehat{B}_{k,0}] \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq \mathsf{Conf}_{k,0}$ . By a union bound, the confidence intervals are valid with probability  $1 - \delta / 2$ , simultaneously. Since the sequence  $\mathbf{x}_{t,0}$  coincides with  $\mathbf{x}_t$  for  $t \leq \tau_{k_{\mathrm{safe}}}$ , and  $\mathbf{u}_{t,0}$  with  $\mathbf{u}_t$  for  $t \leq \tau_{k_{\mathrm{safe}}} - 1$ , we see that  $\mathsf{Conf}_{k,0} = \mathsf{Conf}_k$  for all  $k \leq k_{\mathrm{safe}}$ .

Proof of Regret Bound We begin with the following regret bound.

Lemma G.9. For  $\delta < 1 / T$ , the following hold with probability  $1 - \delta$

$$
\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau_ {k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}}} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} _ {t, 0} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {x}} \mathbf {x} _ {t, 0} + \mathbf {u} _ {t, 0} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} \mathbf {u} _ {t, 0} \lesssim d \tau_ {k _ {\mathrm {s a f e}}} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \mathcal {P} _ {0} \log (\frac {1}{\delta}).
$$

Proof. It suffices to show that the  $(\mathbf{x}_{t,0},\mathbf{u}_{t,0})$  sequences satisfies the following bound:

$$
\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau_ {k _ {0}} - 1} \mathbf {x} _ {t, 0} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {x}} \mathbf {x} _ {t, 0} + \mathbf {u} _ {t, 0} ^ {\top} R _ {\mathbf {u}} \mathbf {u} _ {t, 0} \lesssim \tau_ {k _ {0}} \left(\mathcal {J} _ {0} (1 + \| B _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}) + \mathrm {t r} (R _ {\mathbf {u}})\right) \log (\frac {1}{\delta}),
$$

where the inequality suffices since  $\mathcal{P}_0\geq 1$  (indeed,  $\mathcal{I}_0\geq \mathcal{I}_{\star}\geq d$  by Lemma B.6), and thus  $\mathcal{J}_0(1 + \| B_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}^2) + \mathrm{tr}(R_{\mathbf{u}}) = d_{\mathbf{x}}\mathcal{P}_0(1 + \| B_\star \|_{\mathrm{op}}^2) + d_{\mathbf{u}}\leq d\mathcal{P}_0\Psi_\star.$

For the second, we have from Lemma G.12 and the fact that  $\mathbf{x}_1 = 0$  that there is a Gaussian quadratic form  $\overline{\mathbf{g}}^{\top}\Lambda_{\overline{\mathbf{g}}}\overline{\mathbf{g}}$  which is equal to  $\sum_{t=1}^{\tau_{k_0}-1}\mathbf{x}_{t,0}^{\top}R_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{x}_{t,0}$ , and where  $\mathrm{tr}(\Lambda_{\overline{\mathbf{g}}}) \leq \tau_{k_0}$  ( $\mathcal{J}_0(1 + \|B_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2) + \mathrm{tr}(R_{\mathbf{u}})$ ). The second bound now follows from the crude statement of Hanson Wright in Corollary 6. The last statement follows by a union bound, noting that we need to bound over  $k_{\max} = \log_2T \leq T \leq 1/\delta$ , rounds, and absorbing constants.

We conclude by arguing an upper bound on  $\tau_{k_{\mathrm{safe}}}$ . We rely on the following guarantee.

Lemma G.10. Suppose  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{safe}}$  holds. Then for all  $k < k_{\mathrm{safe}}$  for which  $\Lambda_k\succeq I$ , we must have that  $\mathrm{Conf}_k\gtrsim \epsilon_{\mathrm{safe}}$ , where  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{safe}} = \| P_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-10}$ .

Proof. For all  $k < k_{\mathrm{safe}}$  for which  $\Lambda_k \succeq I$ , we must have that  $\mathsf{Conf}_k > 1 / C_{\mathrm{safe}}(\widehat{A}_k, \widehat{B}_k)$ . If  $\mathsf{Conf}_k \leq c / C_{\mathrm{safe}}(A_\star, B_\star)^2$  for a sufficiently small  $c$ , then the same perturbation argument as in Theorem 11 entails that we have  $\mathsf{Conf}_k \leq 1 / 9C_{\mathrm{safe}}(\widehat{A}_k, \widehat{B}_k)^2$ , yielding a contradiction. Finally, we substitute in  $C_{\mathrm{safe}}(A_\star, B_\star)^2 \lesssim \| P_\star\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{10}$  by Equation (3.1).

Recall that we say  $f \gtrsim_{\star} f$  if “ $f \geq C g$ ” for a sufficiently large constant  $C$  (Definition G.3). In light of the above lemma, Part 2 will follow as soon as we can show that, for any  $\epsilon \in (0,1)$ ,

$$
\text {i f} \tau_ {k} \gtrsim_ {\star} \frac {d (1 + \| K _ {0} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2})}{\epsilon} \log \frac {\Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \mathcal {J} _ {0}}{\delta}, \quad \text {t h e n} \mathsf {C o n f} _ {k, 0} \leq \epsilon , \text {a n d} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k, 0} \succeq I \text {w . p .} 1 - \mathcal {O} (\delta). \quad \tag {G.10}
$$

We begin with a lower bound the matrices  $\Lambda_{k,0}$ :

Lemma G.11. for a sufficiently large constant  $C$ . Finally, set  $\tau_{\mathrm{min}} = d\log (1 + \Psi_{B_\star}\mathcal{J}_0)$ . Then, for any  $k$  such that  $\tau_k\gtrsim_\star \tau_{\mathrm{min}}\lor d\log (\frac{1}{\delta})$ , it holds that

$$
\mathbb {E} [ \mathrm {t r} (\pmb {\Lambda} _ {k, 0}) ] \lesssim \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} \mathcal {J} _ {0} \tau_ {k}, \quad \mathbb {P} \left[ \lambda_ {\min} (\pmb {\Lambda} _ {k, 0}) \gtrsim_ {\star} \frac {\tau_ {k}}{1 + \| K _ {0} \| _ {2} ^ {2}} \right] \leq \delta .
$$

The bound above is proven in Section G.5.1. We can now verify Eq. (G.10), concluding the proof of Part 2.

Proof of Eq. (G.10). Suppose that  $k$  is such that  $\tau_{k} \gtrsim_{\star} \tau_{\min} \vee d \log \left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)$ . Then, by the above lemma, and using  $\operatorname*{det}(cX) = c^{d} \operatorname*{det}(X)$  for  $X \in \mathbb{R}^{d \times d}$ , we have, with probability  $1 - \mathcal{O}(\delta)$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {C o n f} _ {k, 0} \lesssim \frac {1 + \| K _ {0} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{\tau_ {k}} (d + \log \frac {k ^ {2}}{\delta} + \log \det  ((\boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k, 0})) \\ \leq \frac {1 + \| K _ {0} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{\tau_ {k}} (d + \log \frac {k ^ {2}}{\delta} + d \log \mathrm {t r} ((\pmb {\Lambda} _ {k, 0})), \\ \end{array}
$$

where we use that  $X\succeq 0$ , we have  $\log \det (X) = \sum_{i = 1}^{d}\log \lambda_{i}(X)\leq d\log \operatorname {tr}(X)$ . By Markov's inequality, we have with probability  $1 - \delta$  that  $\mathrm{tr}((\pmb{\Lambda}_{k,0})\leq \mathbb{E}[\mathrm{tr}((\pmb{\Lambda}_{k,0})] / \delta \lesssim \Psi_{B_\star}^2\mathcal{J}_0\tau_k\leq \Psi_{B_\star}^2\mathcal{J}_0 / \delta$ , since  $\tau_{k}\leq T\leq 1 / \delta$ . Hence, with some elementary operators, we can bound

$$
\operatorname {C o n f} _ {k, 0} \lesssim \frac {d}{\tau_ {k}} \log \frac {\Psi_ {B _ {*}} ^ {2} \mathcal {J} _ {0}}{\delta}.
$$

Hence, for  $\tau_{k} \gtrsim_{\star} \frac{d}{\epsilon} \log \frac{\Psi_{B_{\star}}^{2} \mathcal{I}_{0}}{\delta}$ , we have with probability  $1 - \delta$  that we have  $\mathsf{Conf}_k \leq \epsilon$ .

# G.5.1 Proof of Lemma G.11

1. We first need to argue a lower bound on matrices  $\Sigma_t$  such that that  $\mathbf{z}_{t,0} \mid \mathcal{F}_{t-1} \sim \mathcal{N}(\overline{\mathbf{z}}_{t,0}, \Sigma_{t,0})$ , where  $\overline{\mathbf{z}}_{t,0}, \Sigma_{t,0}$  are  $\mathcal{F}_{t-1}$  measurable. It is straightforward to show that

$$
\Sigma_ {t, 0} = \left[ \begin{array}{c c} I & K _ {0} \\ K _ {0} ^ {\top} & K _ {0} ^ {\top} K _ {0} + I \end{array} \right],
$$

which by Dean et al. [2018, Lemma F.6], has least singular value bounded below as

$$
\lambda_ {\mathrm {m i n}} (\Sigma_ {t, 0}) \geq \min \left\{\frac {1}{2}, \frac {1}{1 + 2 \| K _ {0} \| ^ {2}} \right\} \geq \frac {1}{2 + 2 \| K _ {0} \| ^ {2}}.
$$

2. Next, we need an upper bound on

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} [ \mathrm {t r} (\pmb {\Lambda} _ {k, 0}) ] = \mathbb {E} [ \sum_ {t = \tau_ {k - 1}} ^ {\tau_ {k} - 1} \| \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| ^ {2} + \| \mathbf {z} _ {t} \| ^ {2} ] \\ \leq \mathbb {E} [ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau_ {k} - 1} \| \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| ^ {2} + \| \mathbf {z} _ {t} \| ^ {2} ] \\ \leq \tau_ {k} \left(1 + \| B _ {\star} \| ^ {2}\right) \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathrm {d l y a p} \left(A _ {K _ {0}}, I + K _ {0} ^ {\top} K _ {0}\right)\right) + \operatorname {t r} \tau_ {k} (I) \\ \leq 2 \tau_ {k} \left(1 + \| B _ {\star} \| ^ {2}\right) \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathrm {d l y a p} \left(A _ {K _ {0}}, I + K _ {0} ^ {\top} K _ {0}\right)\right) \\ \leq 2 \tau_ {k} \left(1 + \| B _ {\star} \| ^ {2}\right) J _ {K _ {0}} = 4 \left(\tau_ {k} - \tau_ {k - 1}\right) \left(1 + \| B _ {\star} \| ^ {2}\right) J _ {K _ {0}} \\ \leq 4 (\tau_ {k} - \tau_ {k - 1}) (1 + \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2}) J _ {K _ {0}} \lesssim \tau_ {k} \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2} J _ {K _ {0}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we use that  $I \preceq \mathsf{dlyap}(A_{K_0}, I + K_0^\top K_0)) \preceq \mathsf{dlyap}(A_{K_0}, R_{\mathbf{x}} + K_0^\top R_{\mathbf{u}}K_0)) = J_{K_0}$  for  $R_{\mathbf{u}}, R_{\mathbf{x}} \geq I$ . This proves the trace upper bound.

3. Using the second to last inequality in the above display, we see that for

$$
\tau_ {k} - \tau_ {k - 1} = \frac {1}{2} \tau_ {k} \geq \underbrace {\frac {2 0 0 0}{9} \left(2 d \log \frac {1 0 0}{3} + d \log (8 (1 + 1 \| K _ {0} \|) ^ {2} (1 + \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} ^ {2}) J _ {K _ {0}})\right)} _ {:= \underline {{\tau}}},
$$

Lemma E.4 implies (taking  $\mathcal{E} = \Omega$  to be the probability space and  $T = \tau_k / 2$ ) that, if  $\tau_{k}\gtrsim_{\star}\tau_{\mathrm{min}}$ , we have

$$
\mathbb {P} \left[ \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {k _ {0}} \not \gtrsim \frac {9 \tau_ {k}}{3 2 0 0} \Sigma_ {0} \right] \leq 2 \exp \left(- \frac {9}{4 0 0 0 (d + 1)} \tau_ {k}\right).
$$

Routine manipulations of give  $\mathsf{dlyap}$ ,  $1 + \| K_0\|^2 \leq \mathsf{dlyap}(A_{K_0}, I + \| K_0\|^2) \leq \mathsf{dlyap}(A_{K_0}, R_{\mathbf{x}} + K_0^\top R_{\mathbf{u}}K_0) = J_{K_0}$  for  $R_{\mathbf{u}}, R_{\mathbf{x}} \succeq I$ . Hence, with a bit of algebra, we can bound

$$
\underline {{\tau}} \lesssim \tau_ {\mathrm {m i n}} := d \log (1 + \Psi_ {B _ {\star}} J _ {K _ {0}}).
$$

Using the lower bound on  $\Sigma_0$  concludes the proof.
